-category of generalized homology theories of topological spaces taking values in
\mathcal{C}
.
, which is a presentable stable
\infty
-category. We have the following fact, given by the adjunction between
\mathbb{L}
and
\Omega^{\infty}
,

Proposition 3.2
.

Let
(f:A\rightarrow B)\in{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C})
be a morphism in
\mathcal{C},
and let
M
be an object in
\mathbf{CatMod}_{B}
, then there is a functorial equivalence of animae,

{\rm Map}_{\mathcal{C}_{/B}}(f,\Omega^{\infty}M)\simeq{\rm Map}_{\mathbf{CatMod}_{B}}(\mathbb{L}_{f},M).

Remark 3.3

(Tangent bundle of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebras)
.

In
[
37
]
, the tangent bundle of
\infty
-category of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings
\mathbf{CAlg}
has been well studied. Let
A
be an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring, the stabilization
\mathbf{CatMod}_{A}=\mathbf{Spt}(\mathbf{CAlg}_{/A})

is naturally equivalent to
\mathbf{Mod}_{A}
, the
\infty
-category of
A
-module spectra. The infinity loop functor
\Omega^{\infty}:\mathbf{Mod}_{A}\rightarrow\mathbf{CAlg}_{/A}

is given by sending an
A
-module
M
to
A\oplus M\rightarrow A
and the cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{A/(-)}:\mathbf{CAlg}_{/A}\rightarrow\mathbf{Mod}_{A}
coincides with the
topological André-Quillen homology
defined in
[
40
]
.

Remark 3.4

(Passing to connective algebras)
.

Let
(\mathcal{C}^{\otimes},\tau)
be a presentable symmetric monoidal stable
\infty
-category equipped with a complete
t
-structure, such that the monoidal product
\otimes
is right
t
-exact in
\mathcal{C}
. In this case, the connective cover functor
\tau_{\geq 0}:\mathbf{CAlg}(\mathcal{C})\rightarrow\mathbf{CAlg}(\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0})
is a right adjoint of the inclusion
\mathbf{CAlg}(\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0})\subset\mathbf{CAlg}(\mathcal{C}).
We claim that this induces an equivalence
{\tau}^{\prime}:\mathbf{Spt}(\mathbf{CAlg}(\mathcal{C})_{/A})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathbf{Spt}(\mathbf{CAlg}(\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0})_{/A})
for any
A\in\mathbf{CAlg}(\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0})
. Indeed, the functor
{\tau}^{\prime}
is a right adjoint functor by the definition of stabilizations, whose left adjoint
\widetilde{(-)}:\mathbf{Spt}(\mathbf{CAlg}(\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0})_{/A})\rightarrow\mathbf{Spt}(\mathbf{CAlg}(\mathcal{C})_{/A})
is given by sending a spectrum object
F:\mathbf{Ani}_{*}^{\rm fin}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}
to
\widetilde{F}:X\mapsto\varinjlim_{n}\Omega^{n}_{\mathcal{C}}F(\Sigma^{n}X)
. We show that
\widetilde{(-)}
is a quasi-inverse of
{\tau}^{\prime}
. First, we note that
{\tau}^{\prime}(\widetilde{F})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\leftarrow}}F
. On the other hand, assuming that
G\in\mathbf{Spt}(\mathbf{CAlg}(\mathcal{C})_{/A})
, we have to show that the natural map
\widetilde{{\tau}^{\prime}(G)}\rightarrow G
is an equivalence. Since equivalences of algebra objects are detected by the forgetful functor
\mathbf{CAlg}(\mathcal{C})\rightarrow\mathcal{C}
. In addition, since
\mathcal{C}
is
t
-complete, the equivalence of a map in
\mathcal{C}
can be detected by the induced maps of homotopies. Since
\mathbf{Ani}^{\rm fin}_{*}
is generated by the
0
-sphere
S^{0}
under finite colimits, we just need to show that

\widetilde{{\tau^{\prime}}(G)}(S^{0})=\varinjlim_{n}\Omega^{n}_{\mathcal{C}}\tau_{\geq 0}G(S^{n})\longrightarrow G(S^{0})

is an equivalence. This is clear because this map becomes an isomorphism passing to
n
-th homotopy
\pi_{n}
for any integer
n
. As a consequence, we have

{\rm T}_{\mathbf{CAlg}(\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0})}\simeq{\rm T}_{\mathbf{CAlg}(\mathcal{C})}\times_{\mathbf{CAlg}(\mathcal{C})}\mathbf{CAlg}(\mathcal{C}_{\geq 0}).

3.1.2
Tangent correspondences

Let
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{D}
be
\infty
-categories, a
correspondence

\mathfrak{M}:\mathcal{C}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{D}
from
\mathcal{C}
to
\mathcal{D}
is a relative
\infty
-category

\mathfrak{M}\rightarrow\Delta^{1}
, such that
\mathfrak{M}_{0}\simeq\mathcal{C}
and
\mathfrak{M}_{1}\simeq\mathcal{D}.

We have a functor
\pi_{\mathfrak{M}}:{\rm Fun}_{\Delta^{1}}(\Delta^{1},{\mathfrak{M}})\rightarrow\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{D}
which is given by the product of the two evaluation functors at source
{\rm ev}_{0}
and at target
{\rm ev}_{1}
. The functor

\pi_{\mathfrak{M}}
is a
bifibration
in the sense of
[
35
, Definition 2.4.7.2]
, see
[
35
, Proposition 2.4.7.10]
. On the other hand,
[
53
, Theorem A and Theorem B]
shows that the functor

\mathfrak{M}\mapsto\pi_{\mathfrak{M}}
gives rise to an equivalence of the
\infty
-category
{\rm Corr}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D})
of correspondences from
\mathcal{C}
to
\mathcal{D}
,
and the
\infty
-category
{\rm BFib}_{/\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{D}}
of bifibrations over
\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{D}.
A variant of
Grothendieck construction
gives
an equivalence of
\infty
-categories

{\rm BFib}_{/\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{D}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\rm Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\rm op}\times\mathcal{D},\mathbf{Ani}),\mathscr{E}_{/\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{D}}\mapsto((c,d)\mapsto\mathscr{E}_{(c,d)}),

c.f.
[
4
, Theorem 1.1.7]
. We can still define an
(\infty,2)
-category
{\mathbf{Corr}}
whose objects are
\infty
-categories, and mapping
\infty
-categories
{\rm Corr}(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{D})\simeq{\rm BFib}_{/\mathcal{C}\times\mathcal{D}}\simeq{\rm Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\rm op}\times\mathcal{D},\mathbf{Ani}),
see
[
4
, Theorem 1.26 and Definition 1.27]
.
The composition of correspondences
is given by the coends:

\displaystyle-\circ-:{\rm Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\rm op}\times\mathcal{D},\mathbf{Ani})

\displaystyle\times{\rm Fun}(\mathcal{D}^{\rm op}\times\mathcal{E},\mathbf{Ani})\longrightarrow{\rm Fun}(\mathcal{C}^{\rm op}\times\mathcal{E},\mathbf{Ani}),

\displaystyle(F,G)\mapsto\int^{d\in\mathcal{D}}F(-,d)\times G(d,-).

Example 3.5

(Fundamental correspondence)
.

Let
\mathfrak{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{0}:\mathcal{C}\rightsquigarrow{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C})
be a correspondence, whose correspondent functor is defined by

\mathfrak{M}_{\mathcal{C}}^{0}:\mathcal{C}^{\rm op}\times{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C})\longrightarrow\mathbf{Ani},(A,f:B\rightarrow C)\mapsto\{A\}\times_{{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{\{0\}},\mathcal{C})}{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{2},\mathcal{C})\times_{{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{\{1,2\}},\mathcal{C})}\{f\}.

In
[
37
, Notation 7.3.6.4]
, the fundamental correspondence is characterized by the following universal property:
Let
A\in{\rm sSet}_{/\Delta^{1}}
be a simplicial set over
\Delta^{1}
, let
K\subset\Delta^{1}\times\Delta^{1}
be a sub simplicial set spanned by verticals

(i,j),i\leq j.
We let
\overline{A}

be the pullback

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{1}\times A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{1}\times\Delta^{1}}

Then there is a functorial isomorphism of sets

{\rm Hom}_{{\rm sSet}_{/\Delta^{1}}}(A,\mathfrak{M}^{0}_{\mathcal{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\rm Hom}_{{\rm sSet}}(\overline{A},\mathcal{C}).

Example 3.6

(Categorical cylinder)
.

Let
F:\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
be a functor. The
categorical cylinder

\mathbf{C}(F):\mathcal{D}\rightsquigarrow\mathcal{C}
is given by:

\mathbf{C}(F):\mathcal{D}^{\rm op}\times\mathcal{C}\longrightarrow\mathbf{Ani},(d,c)\mapsto{\rm Map}_{\mathcal{D}}(d,F(c)).

Definition 3.7
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a presentable
\infty
-category, the
tangent correspondence
of
\mathcal{C}
is the composition of correspondences

{\mathfrak{M}}^{T}_{\mathcal{C}}:=\mathbf{C}(\Omega^{\infty})\circ\mathfrak{M}^{0}_{\mathcal{C}}:\mathcal{C}\rightsquigarrow{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C})\rightsquigarrow{\rm T}_{\mathcal{C}}

By definition, we have
{\mathfrak{M}}^{T}_{\mathcal{C}}\times_{\Delta^{1}}\{0\}\simeq\mathcal{C}
and
{\mathfrak{M}}^{T}_{\mathcal{C}}\times_{\Delta^{1}}\{1\}\simeq{\rm T}_{\mathcal{C}}.
Let
A\in\mathcal{C}
and
(M,B)\in{\rm T}_{\mathcal{C}}.
By the definition of tangent correspondence and the adjoint relation asserted in Proposition
3.2
, we get that the anima

{\rm Map}_{{\mathfrak{M}}^{T}_{\mathcal{C}}}(A,(M,B))
is spanned by morphisms in
\mathbf{CatMod}_{B}
which have the form
f_{!}\mathbb{L}_{A}\rightarrow M,
in which
f:A\rightarrow B
is an arrow in
\mathcal{C}
, and
f_{!}
is the corresponding coCartesian arrow in
\mathrm{T}_{\mathcal{C}}
.

Hence we have the following definition.

Definition 3.8
.

[
37
, Definition 7.4.1.1]

The
\infty
-category of derivations
\mathbf{Der}(\mathcal{C})
in
\mathcal{C}
is the sub-category of
{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},{\mathfrak{M}}^{T}_{\mathcal{C}})
spanned by functors

d:\Delta^{1}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{M}}^{T}_{\mathcal{C}}
such that the composition

\Delta^{1}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{M}}^{T}_{\mathcal{C}}\longrightarrow\Delta^{1}\times{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm ev}_{1}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Delta^{1}\times\mathcal{C}

is the inclusion
\Delta^{1}\rightarrow\Delta^{1}\times\{A\}\subset\Delta^{1}\times\mathcal{C}
for some
A\in\mathcal{C}
. We will use the notation
d:A\rightarrow M
for a derivation, where
M=d(1)\in{\rm T}_{\mathcal{C}}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\{A\}.

Remark 3.9
.

Assume that
\mathcal{C}
has an initial object
\emptyset
. The cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{A/\emptyset}
is initial in the
\infty
-category
\mathbf{Der}(\mathcal{C})_{A}=\mathbf{Der}(\mathcal{C})\times_{\mathcal{C}}\{A\}
. The relative cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{B/A}
of a map
f:A\rightarrow B
is equal to the cofiber of the natural map

\mathbb{L}_{A/\emptyset}\rightarrow\mathbb{L}_{B/\emptyset}
in
{\mathfrak{M}}^{T}_{\mathcal{C}}
. Therefore we get a cofiber sequence

f_{!}\mathbb{L}_{A/\emptyset}\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}_{B/\emptyset}\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}_{B/A}

in
\mathbf{CatMod}_{B}.

3.1.3
Replete tangent correspondences

Using the tangent correspondence, one can define cotangent complexes ( i.e.,
topological André-Quillen homology
) and derivations for
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings. One can also develop the deformation theory in the spectral context
[
38
, Chapter IV,V]
, which is almost parallel to the deformation theory of classical commutative rings (and schemes) developed in
[
24
]
,
[
25
]
and
[
26
]
. Unfortunately, the tangent bundle formalism is not suitable for logarithmic geometry. One reason is that there is not a good definition of "
log modules
" over log rings. Of course, there is a stable
\infty
-category
\mathbf{CatMod}_{(A,M)}
associated with the
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log ring
(A,M)
constructed in the last section by letting
\mathcal{C}=\mathbf{Log}
be the
\infty
-category of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log rings. Sadly, this category is very difficult to handle, and what’s worse is that we cannot do any computation in it. So we will provide a variant of tangent correspondences, which is called
replete tangent correspondences
. We generalize the constructions of
replete tangent bundles
for
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log rings in
[
33
]
.

Definition 3.10
.

Let
U:\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
be a presentable fibration. A replete tangent bundle with respect to
U
is an
\infty
-category
{\rm T}^{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{C}}
, such that there exists an
\infty
-category
{\rm Fun}^{\mathcal{D}}(\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C})\subset{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C})
, and a relative Bousfield localization

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm Fun}^{\mathcal{D}}(\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm ev}_{1}}

, and there exist presentable fibrations
U_{*}:{\rm Fun}^{\mathcal{D}}(\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C})\rightarrow\mathcal{C}
and

U_{*}^{\prime}:{\rm T}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathcal{D}}\rightarrow{\rm T}_{\mathcal{D}}
,
fitting into the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm T}_{\mathcal{C}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm T}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathcal{D}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm T}_{\mathcal{D}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm Fun}^{\mathcal{D}}(\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathcal{D})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\mathcal{C}}^{\infty}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{*}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{\infty}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\mathcal{D}}^{\infty}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}

where the functor
\Omega^{\infty}:{\rm T}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathcal{D}}\rightarrow{\rm Fun}^{\mathcal{D}}(\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C})
is the stable envelope of the functor
U_{*}:{\rm Fun}^{\mathcal{D}}(\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C})\rightarrow\mathcal{C}
. In addition, the following conditions are satisfied:

1.

The functor
U
is a right adjoint, whose left adjoint
G
is fully faithful

2.

The functor
U^{\prime}_{*}
is a right adjoint.

3.

The functor
{\rm Fun}^{\mathcal{D}}(\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C})\rightarrow\mathcal{C}
is a presentable fibration.

4.

The commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm T}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathcal{D}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm T}_{\mathcal{D}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}}

is a pullback square.

By the definition of replete tangent bundle given above, the presentable fibration

\Omega^{\infty}:{\rm T}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathcal{D}}\longrightarrow{\rm Fun}^{\mathcal{D}}(\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C})

admits a left adjoint, which we denote by
\mathcal{L}^{\mathcal{D}}
. We have the following definition:

Definition 3.11
.

The
Gabber’s cotangent complex
of the presentable fibration
U:\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
is the composition

\mathbb{L}^{\mathcal{D}}:{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C})\longrightarrow{\rm Fun}^{\mathcal{D}}(\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathcal{L}^{\mathcal{D}}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathrm{T}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathcal{D}}.

By definition, the Gabber’s cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}^{\mathcal{D}}:{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C})\rightarrow\mathrm{T}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathcal{D}}
admits a right adjoint
\Omega^{\infty,\mathcal{D}}:{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C})\rightarrow\mathrm{T}_{\mathcal{C}}^{\mathcal{D}}.

Definition 3.12
.

The replete tangent correspondence
{\mathfrak{M}}^{T^{\mathcal{D}}}_{\mathcal{C}}
of
U:\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\mathcal{D}
is defined as the compositions of correspondences in the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm Fun}^{\mathcal{D}}(\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm T}^{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{C}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{M}^{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{C}(i)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{C}(\Omega^{\infty})}

where the functor
i:{\rm Fun}^{\mathcal{D}}(\Delta^{1},\mathcal{D})\rightarrow{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C})
is the right adjoint of the Bousfield localization
{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathcal{C})\rightarrow{\rm Fun}^{\mathcal{D}}(\Delta^{1},\mathcal{D}).

Definition 3.13
.

The
\infty
-category of replete derivations
\mathbf{Der}^{\mathcal{D}}(\mathcal{C})
in
\mathcal{C}
is the subcategory of
{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},{\mathfrak{M}}^{T^{\mathcal{D}}}_{\mathcal{C}})
spanned by these functors

d:\Delta^{1}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{M}}^{T^{\mathcal{D}}}_{\mathcal{C}}
such that the composition

\Delta^{1}\longrightarrow{\mathfrak{M}}^{T^{\mathcal{D}}}_{\mathcal{C}}\longrightarrow\Delta^{1}\times\mathcal{C}

is the functor
\Delta^{1}\rightarrow\Delta^{1}\times\{A\}\subset\Delta^{1}\times C
for some
A\in\mathcal{C}.
For such functors we use the notation
d:A\rightarrow M
, where
M
is the image of
d
at
1\in\Delta^{1}

d(1)\in{\rm T}^{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{C}}\times_{\mathcal{C}}\{A\}\simeq\mathbf{CatMod}_{U(A)}\simeq\mathbf{Spt}(\mathcal{D}_{/U(A)}).

The
\infty
-category of replete derivations
\mathbf{Der}^{\mathcal{D}}(\mathcal{C})
is equivalent to the fiber product

{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},{\mathfrak{M}}^{T^{\mathcal{D}}}_{\mathcal{C}})\times_{{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\Delta^{1}\times\mathcal{C})}\mathcal{C}.

Remark 3.14
.

Assume that
\mathcal{C}
has an initial object
\emptyset
. The Gabber’s cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}^{\mathcal{D}}_{A/\emptyset}
is initial in the
\infty
-category
\mathbf{Der}^{\mathcal{D}}(\mathcal{C})_{A}=\mathbf{Der}^{\mathcal{D}}(\mathcal{C})\times_{\mathcal{C}}\{A\}
. The relative Gabber’s cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}^{\mathcal{D}}_{B/A}
of a map
f:A\rightarrow B
is equal to the cofiber of the natural map

\mathbb{L}^{\mathcal{D}}_{A/\emptyset}\rightarrow\mathbb{L}^{\mathcal{D}}_{B/\emptyset}
in
{\mathfrak{M}}^{T^{\mathcal{D}}}
. Therefore we get a cofiber sequence

f_{!}\mathbb{L}^{\mathcal{D}}_{A/\emptyset}\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}^{\mathcal{D}}_{B/\emptyset}\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}^{\mathcal{D}}_{B/A}

in
\mathbf{CatMod}_{B}.
Here we let
f_{!}
be a functor
\mathbf{CatMod}_{A}\rightarrow\mathbf{CatMod}_{B}
given by the coCartesian fibration
{\rm T}^{\mathcal{D}}_{\mathcal{C}}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}
.

3.2
Log derivations

We apply the constructions of replete tangent bundles and replete tangent correspondences to
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log rings. For this, we introduce the notion of exactification of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log rings, which generalizes the definition of
repletion of (
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-)log rings
introduced by Kato
[
29
, Section 2]
and Rognes
[
49
, Section 3]
for virtually surjective maps. We will also define the
\infty
-category of log derivations in this subsection.

3.2.1
Exactifications

We give a categorical description of the conception
exactifications
of log rings introduced in
[
29
]
,
[
49
]
and
[
51
]
in the spectral context. This construction is essential for building the deformation theory in logarithmic geometry.

Lemma 3.15
.

Let
f:M\rightarrow N
be a morphism of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-monoids, and let
\mathcal{C}(f)
be the subcategory of
\mathbf{CMon}_{M//N}
spanned by objects
M\stackrel{{\scriptstyle a}}{{\rightarrow}}M^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle b}}{{\rightarrow}}N
, such that the composition
b\circ a
is equivalent to
f
, and

a^{\rm gp}:M^{\rm gp}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\rightarrow}}M^{\prime\rm gp}
. Then
\mathcal{C}
admits a terminal object
M\rightarrow M^{\rm ex}\rightarrow N
.

Proof.

First, the
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}(f)
is filtered. Indeed, any functor
F:K\rightarrow\mathcal{C}(f),k\mapsto(M\rightarrow M^{\prime}_{k}\rightarrow N)
can be extended to
F^{\triangleright}:K^{\triangleright}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}(f)
, as we can let the value
F^{\triangleright}(\infty)
at the cone point
\infty
be
(M\rightarrow M\coprod_{{\rm colim}_{K}M}{\rm colim}_{K}M^{\prime}_{k}\rightarrow N)
.

We let
M^{\rm ex}:={\rm colim}_{M^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}(f)}M^{\prime}.
Then
(M\rightarrow M^{\prime}\rightarrow N)\in\mathcal{C}(f)
since there is an equivalence
M^{\rm ex,gp}\simeq({\rm colim}_{M^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}(f)}M^{\prime})^{\rm gp}\simeq{\rm colim}_{M^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}(f)}M^{\prime\rm gp}=M^{\rm gp}.

∎

Therefore, we have the following definition:

Definition 3.16
.

Let
f:M\rightarrow N
be a morphism of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-monoids. We say that
f
is exact if
f\simeq f^{\rm ex}
. Here
f^{\rm ex}
is a terminal object in
\mathcal{C}(f)
.

Corollary 3.17
.

[
14
, Proposition 2.21]

If the morphism
f:M\rightarrow N
admits a section
s:N\rightarrow M
, then the sequence
N\stackrel{{\scriptstyle s}}{{\rightarrow}}M^{\rm ex}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\rightarrow}}N
splits,
and
M^{\rm ex}\simeq N\oplus M^{\rm gp}/N^{\rm gp}
. The functor

Q:\mathbf{CMon}^{\rm ex}_{N//N}\rightarrow\mathbf{CMon}^{\rm gp},(N\rightarrow M\rightarrow N)\mapsto M/N

is an equivalence. Here
\mathbf{CMon}^{\rm ex}_{N//N}
is the subcategory of
\mathbf{CMon}_{N//N}
spanned by exact maps.

Proof.

We see that any
M^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}(f)
, up to homotopy, there is a unique morphism
M^{\prime}\rightarrow N\oplus M^{\rm gp}/N^{\rm gp}
, such that the following diagram commutes,

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N\oplus M^{\rm gp}/N^{\rm gp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M^{\rm gp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N^{\rm gp}\oplus M^{\rm gp}/N^{\rm gp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N^{\rm gp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

This implies that
N\oplus M^{\rm gp}/N^{\rm gp}
is a terminal object in
\mathcal{C}(f)
. In other words, this gives rise to an equivalence
M^{\rm ex}\simeq N\oplus M^{\rm gp}/N^{\rm gp}
.

The functor
Q
is clearly essentially surjective. We want to show that
Q
is fully faithful. Indeed, let
M,M^{\prime}\in\mathbf{CMon}^{\rm ex}_{N//N}
we have equivalences

\displaystyle{\rm Map}_{\mathbf{CMon}^{\rm ex}_{N//N}}(M,M^{\prime})

\displaystyle\simeq{\rm Map}_{\mathbf{CMon}^{\rm ex}_{N//N}}(N\oplus M^{\rm gp}/N^{\rm gp},N\oplus M^{\prime\rm gp}/N^{\rm gp})

\displaystyle\simeq{\rm Map}_{\mathbf{CMon}^{\rm gp}}(M^{\rm gp}/N^{\rm gp},M^{\prime\rm gp}/N^{\rm gp})

\displaystyle\simeq{\rm Map}_{\mathbf{CMon}^{\rm gp}}(Q(M),Q(M^{\prime})).

∎

Lemma 3.18
.

Let
\mathbf{CMon}^{\rm ex}_{/N}
be the subcategory of
\mathbf{CMon}_{/N}
spanned by exact morphisms. Then the inclusion
\mathbf{CMon}^{\rm ex}_{/N}\subset\mathbf{CMon}_{/N}
admits a left adjoint
M\mapsto M^{\rm ex},
which is called the exactification functor. The exactification functor provides a presentation of
\mathbf{CMon}^{\rm ex}_{/N}
, and therefore it is presentable.

Proof.

Let
P\rightarrow N
be an exact morphism, and let
M\rightarrow P
be a morphism in
\mathbf{CMon}_{/N}
. We have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M^{\rm gp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P^{\rm gp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N^{\rm gp}}

Now let
M^{\prime}
be an object in
\mathcal{C}(M\rightarrow N)
, i.e.,
there is a morphism
M\rightarrow M^{\prime}
over
N
, such that
M^{\rm gp}\simeq M^{\prime\rm gp}.
Let

P^{\prime}
be the pushout
P\coprod_{M}M^{\prime}
. We have
P\simeq P^{\prime}
, since
P^{\prime\rm gp}=P^{\rm gp}\coprod_{M^{\rm gp}}M^{\rm gp}\simeq P^{\rm gp}.
Then after inverting the map
P\rightarrow P^{\prime}
, we get a morphism
M^{\prime}\rightarrow P
, which is unique, up to homotopy, such that the following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P}

Passing to colimit, we get a functorial equivalence of animae

{\rm Map}_{\mathbf{CMon}_{/N}}(M,P)\simeq{\rm Map}_{\mathbf{CMon}_{/N}}(M^{\rm ex},P)\simeq{\rm Map}_{\mathbf{CMon}^{\rm ex}_{/N}}(M^{\rm ex},P).

∎

Remark 3.19
.

If the morphism
f:M\rightarrow N
is
virtually surjective
, i.e., the induced map
\pi_{0}(M^{\rm gp})\rightarrow\pi_{0}(N^{\rm gp})
is surjective, then
[
49
, Lemma 3.8]
shows that the fiber product
N\times_{N^{\rm gp}}M^{\rm gp}
is an exactification of
f
, and in which, Rognes refers to the construction
(M\rightarrow N)\mapsto N\times_{N^{\rm gp}}M^{\rm gp}
as the
repletion
of a virtually surjective map, c.f.
[
49
, Definition 3.6]
.

Remark 3.20

(The tangent bundle of
\mathbf{CMon}
)
.

Corollary
3.17
and Lemma
3.18
permit us to study the tangent bundle
{\rm T}_{\mathbf{CMon}}
of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-monoids. By definition, the tangent bundle
{\rm T}_{\mathbf{CMon}}
of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-monoids is the relative stabilization of the evaluation functor
{\rm ev}_{1}:{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{CMon})\rightarrow\mathbf{CMon}
. Let us calculate the fiber
{\rm T}_{\mathbf{CMon}}\times_{\mathbf{CMon}}\{M\},
which is equivalent to the stabilization of
\mathbf{CMon}_{M//M}
as there is a canonical equivalence
\mathbf{Spt}(\mathcal{C})\simeq\mathbf{Spt}(\mathcal{C}_{*/})
for
\mathcal{C}
, as explained in
[
37
, Remark 1.4.2.18]
. Let
\mathcal{E}
be an arbitrary presentable stable
\infty
-category, and let
F:\mathbf{CMon}_{M//M}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}
be a colimit-preserving functor. For instance, we have
F(M)\simeq 0
. One can see that there is a unique factorization of
F
,

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{CMon}_{M//M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{CMon}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F^{\prime}}

such that the extension
F^{\prime}
is colimit-preserving,
where
\pi
is the forgetful functor.
F^{\prime}
is given by sending an object
P\in\mathbf{CMon}
to
F(P\oplus M)\in\mathcal{E}.
Now we claim that the functor
F^{\prime}
uniquely factors through
\mathbf{CMon}^{\rm gp}
. Indeed, let
P_{\bullet}
be the simplicial diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces...}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P^{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

We have the following natural equivalences

F^{\prime}(BP)\simeq F^{\prime}({\rm colim}_{\Delta^{\rm op}}P_{\bullet})\simeq{\rm colim}_{\Delta^{\rm op}}F^{\prime}(P_{\bullet})\simeq\Sigma F^{\prime}(P).

This implies that we have equivalences

F^{\prime}(P)\simeq\Omega F^{\prime}(BP)\simeq\Omega F^{\prime}(BP^{\rm gp})\simeq F^{\prime}(P^{\rm gp}),

the second equivalence holds because
BP\simeq BP^{\rm gp}
.
Thus we have a commutative diagram of
\infty
-categories

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{CMon}_{M//M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{CMon}^{\rm ex}_{M//M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{CMon}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{CMon}^{\rm gp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

and therefore we get equivalences

{\rm Fun}^{L}(\mathbf{CMon},\mathcal{E})\simeq{\rm Fun}^{L}(\mathbf{CMon}^{\rm gp},\mathcal{E})\simeq{\rm Fun}^{L}(\mathbf{Spt},\mathcal{E}).

The second equivalence holds because
\mathbf{CMon}^{\rm gp}\simeq\mathbf{Spt}_{\geq 0}
by the recognition principle, and so its stabilization is
\mathbf{Spt}
.
i.e. we have
{\rm T}_{\mathbf{CMon}}\times_{\mathbf{CMon}}\{M\}\simeq\mathbf{Spt},
for any
M\in\mathbf{CMon}
, and the cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{M}
of a monoid is nothing but its group completion
M^{\rm gp}
.

Definition 3.21
.

Let
f:(A,M)\rightarrow(B,N)
be a morphism of connective
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-prelog rings. We say
f
is exact if the induced map
M\rightarrow N
is exact.

Let
\mathbf{PreLog}^{\rm ex}_{/(B,N)}
be the subcategory of
\mathbf{PreLog}_{/(B,N)}
spanned by exact morphisms
(A,M)\rightarrow(B,N)
. Hence we have a pullback square of
\infty
-categories

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{PreLog}^{\rm ex}_{/(B,N)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{PreLog}_{/(B,N)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{CMon}^{\rm ex}_{/N}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{CMon}_{/N}}

Then
\mathbf{PreLog}^{\rm ex}_{/(B,N)}
turns out to be presentable by
[
35
, Theorem 5.5.3.18]
.

Proposition 3.22
.

[
32
, Proposition 9.1]

The fully faithful inclusion
\mathbf{PreLog}^{\rm ex}_{/(B,N)}\subset\mathbf{PreLog}_{/(B,N)}
admits a left adjoint
{\rm ex}:(A,M)\mapsto(A,M)^{\rm ex},
which is called the exactification functor. More precisely, we have
(A,M)^{\rm ex}\simeq(A\otimes_{\mathbb{S}[M]}\mathbb{S}[M^{\rm ex}],M^{\rm ex}).

Proof.

Since the limits in
\mathbf{PreLog}_{/(B,N)}
and
\mathbf{PreLog}^{\rm ex}_{/(B,N)}
are computed point-wise. We obtain that the forgetful functor
\mathbf{PreLog}^{\rm ex}_{/(B,N)}\subset\mathbf{PreLog}_{/(B,N)}
of presentable
\infty
-category preserves arbitrary (small) limits. So there is a left adjoint

{\rm ex}:\mathbf{PreLog}_{/(B,N)}\rightarrow\mathbf{PreLog}^{\rm ex}_{/(B,N)}

of the inclusion
\mathbf{PreLog}^{\rm ex}_{/(B,N)}\subset\mathbf{PreLog}_{/(B,N)}.

Now let us compute the exactification of a morphism
f:(A,M)\rightarrow(B,N).
Let
\mathcal{D}(f)
be the subcategory of
\mathbf{PreLog}_{(A,M)//(B,N)}
spanned by
(A,M)\rightarrow(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})\rightarrow(B,N)
such that
M^{\prime}\rightarrow N
is exact. Note that the morphism
(A,M)\rightarrow(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})
factors through the morphism
(A,M)\rightarrow(A\otimes_{\mathbb{S}[M]}\mathbb{S}[M^{\prime}],M^{\prime}).
Hence, the subcategory of
\mathcal{D}(f)
spanned by those objects which are equivalent to
(A,M)\rightarrow(A\otimes_{\mathbb{S}[M]}\mathbb{S}[M^{\prime}],M^{\prime})\rightarrow(B,N)
such that
M^{\prime}\rightarrow N
is exact, is cofinal in
\mathcal{D}(f)
. We denote by
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(f)
the resulting
\infty
-category. It’s easy to see that the functor
(A\otimes_{\mathbb{S}[M]}\mathbb{S}[M^{\prime}],M^{\prime})\mapsto M^{\prime}
gives rise to an equivalence between
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(f)
and the
\infty
-category
\mathbf{CMon}^{\rm ex}_{M//N}
. As a consequence, we have

\displaystyle(A,M)^{\rm ex}\simeq\varprojlim_{\mathcal{D}(f)}(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})

\displaystyle\simeq\varprojlim_{M^{\prime}\in\mathbf{CMon}^{\rm ex}_{M//N}}(A\otimes_{\mathbb{S}[M]}\mathbb{S}[M^{\prime}],M^{\prime})

\displaystyle\simeq(A\otimes_{\mathbb{S}[M]}\mathbb{S}[M^{\rm ex}],M^{\rm ex}).

∎

Definition 3.23
.

Let
(B,N)
be a connective
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log ring. The
\infty
-category of exact connective
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log rings over
(B,N)
is the pullback of presentable
\infty
-categories.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{Log}^{\rm ex}_{/(B,N)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{Log}_{/(B,N)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{PreLog}^{\rm ex}_{/(B,N)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{PreLog}_{/(B,N)}}}

Corollary 3.24
.

The fully faithful inclusion
\mathbf{Log}^{\rm ex}_{/(B,N)}\subset\mathbf{Log}_{/(B,N)}
admits a left adjoint, which we still denote by

{\rm ex}:\mathbf{Log}_{/(B,N)}\longrightarrow\mathbf{Log}^{\rm ex}_{/(B,N)}

and call it the exactification functor.

Proof.

It follows from Proposition
3.22
and
[
35
, Theorem 5.5.3.18]
.
∎

Proposition 3.25
.

[
33
, Proposition 6.10]

Let
(B,N)
be a connective
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log ring. Then the forgetful functor
\mathbf{Log}^{\rm ex}_{(B,N)//(B,N)}\rightarrow\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}_{B//B}
is an equivalence.

Proof.

Consider the commutative diagram of
\infty
-categories

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{Log}^{\rm ex}_{(B,N)//(B,N)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{Log}_{(B,N)//(B,N)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{PreLog}^{\rm ex}_{(B,N)//(B,N)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{PreLog}_{(B,N)//(B,N)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{CMon}^{\rm ex}_{N//N}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{CMon}_{N//N}}

Every square is Cartesian. Corollary
3.17
shows that there is an equivalence

\mathbf{CMon}^{\rm gp}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbf{CMon}^{\rm ex}_{N//N},G\mapsto G\oplus N.

So the
\infty
-category
\mathbf{PreLog}^{\rm ex}_{(B,N)//(B,N)}
is equivalent to the full subcategory of
\mathbf{PreLog}_{(B,N)//(B,N)}
spanned by morphisms having the form
(B,N)\rightarrow(A,N\oplus G)\rightarrow(B,N).
The monoid morphism
G\rightarrow A
factors through

G\longrightarrow GL_{1}(A)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}GL_{1}(B)\oplus GL_{1}(A)/GL_{1}(B)\longrightarrow N\oplus GL_{1}(A)/GL_{1}(B)

It follows that we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(B,N)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(A,N\oplus G)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(B,N)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(B,N)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(A,N\oplus GL_{1}(A)/GL_{1}(B))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(B,N)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}

It’s easy to see that the morphism
(A,N)\rightarrow(A,N\oplus GL_{1}(A)/GL_{1}(B))
exhibits
(A,N\oplus GL_{1}(A)/GL_{1}(B))
as a logification of
(A,N\oplus G)
, and thus we have
(A,N\oplus GL_{1}(A)/GL_{1}(B))\in\mathbf{Log}^{\rm ex}_{(B,N)//(B,N)}.
We must show that
(A,N\oplus GL_{1}(A)/GL_{1}(B))
is the image of
(A,N\oplus G)
under the localization
\mathbf{PreLog}^{\rm ex}_{(B,N)//(B,N)}\rightarrow\mathbf{Log}^{\rm ex}_{(B,N)//(B,N)}
of the fully faithful inclusion
\mathbf{Log}^{\rm ex}_{(B,N)//(B,N)}\subset\mathbf{PreLog}^{\rm ex}_{(B,N)//(B,N)}
. Indeed, for any
(R,P)\in\mathbf{Log}^{\rm ex}_{(B,N)//(B,N)}
, and any morphism
(A,N\oplus G)\rightarrow(R,P)
, since
(R,P)
is an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log ring, the map uniquely factors through
(A,N\oplus G)\rightarrow(A,N\oplus GL_{1}(A)/GL_{1}(B)).

∎

3.2.2
Log derivations

Let
U:\mathbf{Log}\rightarrow\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}
be the forgetful functor, which is a right adjoint, and let
{\rm Fun}^{\rm ext}(\Delta^{1},\textbf{Log}):={\rm Fun}^{\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log})
be the subcategory of
{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\textbf{Log})
spanned by exact morphisms.

Proposition 3.26
.

1.

The evaluation functor
{\rm ev}_{1}:{\rm Fun}^{\rm ext}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log})\rightarrow\mathbf{Log}
is a presentable fibration.

2.

The forgetful functor
U_{*}:{\rm Fun}^{\rm ext}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log})\rightarrow{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn})
is a right adjoint.

Proof.

All fibers of the functor
{\rm ev}_{1}
are presentable. Corollary
3.24
implies that it is also a coCartesian fibration. Indeed, let
f:(A,M)\rightarrow(B,N)
be an arrow in
\mathbf{Log}
,
and let
g:(R,P)\rightarrow(A,M)
be a connective
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log ring over
(A,M)
. Then the exactification
(R,P)^{\rm ex}_{(B,N)}
of
(R,P)
with respect to
(B,N)
is a pushout of
(R,P)
along
f.
We denote by
f_{!}:\mathbf{Log}^{\rm ex}_{/(A,M)}\rightarrow\mathbf{Log}^{\rm ex}_{/(B,N)}
a pushout functor along
f
.

Using Grothendieck’s construction, the coCartesian fibration
{\rm ev}_{1}
gives rise to a functor

\chi:\mathbf{Log}\longrightarrow\mathbf{Cat}_{\infty},(A,M)\mapsto\mathbf{Log}^{\rm ex}_{/(A,M)}.

We have to prove that the functor
\chi
factors through
\mathbf{Log}\rightarrow\mathbf{Pr}_{L},
i.e., the functor
f_{!}
preserves small colimits.

There is a commutative diagram of presentable
\infty
-categories

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{Log}_{/(A,M)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{Log}_{/(B,N)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{Log}^{\rm ex}_{/(A,M)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{Log}^{\rm ex}_{/(B,N)}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rm ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rm ex}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{!}}

Let
K
be a simplicial set and
F:K\rightarrow\mathbf{Log}^{\rm ex}_{/(A,M)}
be a functor. Then we have a lifting
\tilde{F}:K\rightarrow\mathbf{Log}_{/(A,M)}.
Since the exactification functor preserves colimits, we have
{\rm colim}{F}\simeq({\rm colim}{\tilde{F}})^{\rm ex}.
Thus we have equivalences

\displaystyle f_{!}({\rm colim}{F})

\displaystyle\simeq f_{!}(({\rm colim}{\tilde{F}})^{\rm ex})\simeq(f_{!}{\rm colim}\tilde{F})^{\rm ex}

\displaystyle\simeq({\rm colim}f_{!}\tilde{F})^{\rm ex}\simeq{\rm colim}{f_{!}\tilde{F}}^{\rm ex}\simeq{\rm colim}f_{!}F.

As for the second statement, we only need to show that
U_{*}
preserves small limits. It follows that we need to show that the inclusion
{\rm Fun}^{\rm ext}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log})\subset{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log})
preserves small limits. It’s clear, because the inclusion is a relative right adjoint of the exactification functor
{\rm ex}:{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log})\rightarrow{\rm Fun}^{\rm ext}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log})
over
\mathbf{Log}
by Proposition
3.22
and Definition
3.23
.
∎

We let
{\rm T}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}
be the stable envelope of the presentable fibration
{\rm ev}_{1}:{\rm Fun}^{\rm ext}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log})\rightarrow\mathbf{Log}
. Using Proposition
3.26
, we can form a commutative diagram of
\infty
-categories

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm T}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm T}_{\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm Fun}^{\rm ext}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Log}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{\infty}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{\infty}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm ev}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm ev}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}

which satisfies condition
1,2
and
3
in the Definition
3.10
, the following proposition guarantees that this forms a replete tangent bundle formalism.

Proposition 3.27
.

[
33
, Corollary 6.22]

There is a natural equivalence
{\rm T}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}\simeq\mathbf{Log}\times_{\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}}\mathrm{T}_{\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}}

of
\infty
-categories over
\mathbf{Log}
.

Proof.

We just need to show that the two stable presentable fibrations
{\rm T}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}\rightarrow\mathbf{Log}
and
\mathbf{Log}\times_{\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}}\mathrm{T}_{\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}}
represent the same prestack

\chi_{{\rm T}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}}\simeq\chi_{\mathbf{Log}\times_{\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}}\mathrm{T}_{\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}}}:\mathbf{Log}\longrightarrow\mathbf{Pr}_{L}^{{\rm stab}}

It’s deduced immediately from Proposition
3.25
.
∎

Remark 3.28
.

Thanks to Proposition
3.27
,
we could identify the fiber
{\rm T}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}\times_{\mathbf{Log}}\{(A,M)\}
with
{\rm T}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}\times_{\mathbf{Log}}\{(A,GL_{1}(A))\}\simeq\mathbf{Mod}_{A}
via the functor

i_{!}:\mathbf{Mod}_{A}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\rm T}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}\times_{\mathbf{Log}}\{(A,M)\}

induced by the morphism
i:(A,GL_{1}(A))\rightarrow(A,M).

Therefore, we can apply the replete tangent formalism to logarithmic geometry.

Definition 3.29
.

We denote the replete tangent correspondence associated with
{\rm T}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}
as
\mathfrak{M}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}
. The
\infty
-category of log derivations is the
\infty
-category of replete derivations in
\mathfrak{M}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}
. The Gabber’s cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}^{G}
is the Gabber’s cotangent complex associated with
{\rm T}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}
. We also denote by

\mathbf{Der}^{\mathbf{Log}}_{(A,M)}
the fiber product
\mathbf{Der}^{\mathbf{Log}}\times_{\mathbf{Log}}\{(A,M)\}
, and refer to it as the
\infty
-category of log derivations of
(A,M)
.

Remark 3.30
.

Using Proposition
3.27
, Remark
3.3
and Remark
3.4
, we can concretely describe the infinity loop space functor

\Omega^{\infty}:{\rm T}^{\rm rep}_{\textbf{Log},(A,M)}\longrightarrow\textbf{Log}_{/(A,M)},

which sends
((A,M),I)
to
(A\oplus\tau_{\geq 0}I,M\oplus\tau_{\geq 0}I)\rightarrow(A,M)
.
The proof of Proposition
3.25
implies that
(A\oplus\tau_{\geq 0}I,M\oplus\tau_{\geq 0}I)
is exact over
(A,M)
.

We deduce the following properties of Gabber’s cotangent complex from its definition.

Theorem 3.31
.

For Gabber’s cotangent complexes, we have the following:

1.

(Universal characterization) Fix a morphism
(B,N)\rightarrow(A,M)
of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log rings.
The Gabber’s cotangent complex defined above is characterized by the following property: for any
I\in\mathbf{Mod}^{\rm cn}_{A}
, we have

{\rm Map}_{A}(\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(A,M)/(B,N)},I)\simeq{\rm Map}_{{(B,N)//(A,M)}}((A,M),(A\oplus I,M\oplus I)).

2.

(Conormal fiber sequence) Let
(R,L)\rightarrow(A,M)\rightarrow(B,N)
be a sequence of maps of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log rings, the following sequence

\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(A,M)/(R,L)}\otimes_{A}B\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(B,N)/(R,L)}\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(B,N)/(A,M)}

is exact in
\mathbf{Mod}_{B}
.

Proof.

This is deduced immediately from the categorical definition of Gabber’s cotangent complex.
∎

Corollary 3.32
.

Fix a morphism
(B,N)\rightarrow(A,M)
of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log rings.
There is an equivalence of
\infty
-categories:

{\rm ev}_{1}:\mathbf{Der}^{\mathbf{Log}}_{(A,M)}\times_{\mathbf{Der}^{\mathbf{Log}}_{(B,N)}}\mathbf{Der}^{\mathbf{Log},{\rm triv}}_{(B,N)}\longrightarrow(\mathbf{Mod}^{\rm cn}_{A})_{\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(A,M)/(B,N)}/}

In which
\mathbf{Der}^{\mathbf{Log},{\rm triv}}_{(B,N)}
is the full subcategory of
\mathbf{Der}^{\mathbf{Log}}_{(B,N)}
spanned by trivial derivations, i.e., derivations having the form

0:\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(A,M)/(B,N)}\rightarrow I
. In other words, the left hand term is the
\infty
-category of
(A,M)
-derivations which is trivial restricting to
(B,N).

Proof.

Denote by
\mathbf{Der}^{\mathbf{Log}}_{(A,M)/(B,N)}
the left hand term.
Theorem
3.31
guarantees that the evaluation

{\rm ev_{1}}:\mathbf{Der}^{\mathbf{Log}}_{(A,M)/(B,N)}\longrightarrow\mathbf{Mod}_{A}^{\rm cn}

factors through the comma category
(\mathbf{Mod}^{\rm cn}_{A})_{\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(A,M)/(B,N)}/}
,
and clearly the induced functor

{\rm ev}_{1}:\mathbf{Der}^{\mathbf{Log}}_{(A,M)/(B,N)}\longrightarrow(\mathbf{Mod}^{\rm cn}_{A})_{\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(A,M)/(B,N)}/}

is essentially surjective.
We must show that the functor
{\rm ev}_{1}
is fully faithful. Let
d:(A,M)\rightarrow I
and
d^{\prime}:(A,M)\rightarrow J
be two derivations such that the restrictions to
(B,N)
are trivial. The mapping space
{\rm Map}_{\mathbf{Der}^{\mathbf{Log}}_{(A,M)/(B,N)}}(d,d^{\prime})
is the anima spanned by commutative diagrams having the form

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(B,N)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(A,M)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces J}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d^{\prime}}

in
\mathfrak{M}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}
, such that the arrow
I\rightarrow J
is an
A
-module morphism. Hence it follows that the mapping space is equivalent to the pullback
of the following animae

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm Map}_{\mathfrak{M}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}}((A,M),I))\times_{{\rm Map}_{\mathfrak{M}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}}(B,N),I)}\{0\}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm Map}_{A}(I,J)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm Map}_{\mathfrak{M}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}}((A,M),J))\times_{{\rm Map}_{\mathfrak{M}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}}(B,N),J)}\{0\}}}

Theorem
3.31
shows that there are natural equivalences of animae

{\rm Map}_{\mathfrak{M}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}}((A,M),I))\times_{{\rm Map}_{\mathfrak{M}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}}(B,N),I)}\{0\}\simeq{\rm Map}_{A}(\mathbb{L}_{(A,M)/(B,N)},I),

and

{\rm Map}_{\mathfrak{M}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}}((A,M),J))\times_{{\rm Map}_{\mathfrak{M}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}}(B,N),I)}\{0\}\simeq{\rm Map}_{A}(\mathbb{L}_{(A,M)/(B,N)},J).

Then it turns out that we have equivalences

\displaystyle{\rm Map}_{\mathbf{Der}^{\mathbf{Log}}_{(A,M)/(B,N)}}(d,d^{\prime})

\displaystyle\simeq{\rm Map}_{A}(\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(A,M)/(B,N)},I)\times_{{\rm Map}_{A}(\mathbb{L}^{G}L_{(A,M)/(B,N)},J)}{\rm Map}_{A}(I,J)

\displaystyle\simeq{\rm Map}_{\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(A,M)/(B,N)}/}(I,J)

∎

We have some elementary calculations of Gabber’s cotangent complexes.

Lemma 3.33
.

[
51
, Section 4]
,
[
14
, Section 3]

1.

Let
f:A\rightarrow B
be a morphism of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings. Regard
f
as a morphism of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log rings with trivial log structures, then there is an equivalence
\mathbb{L}_{B/A}\simeq\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(B,GL_{1}(B))/(A,GL_{1}(A))}
.

2.

Let
f:(A,M)\rightarrow(B,N)
be a strict morphism, then there is a natural equivalence
\mathbb{L}_{B/A}\simeq\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(B,N)/(A,M)}.

3.

The Gabber’s cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(A[M],M)/(A[N],N)}
is equivalent to
A[M]\otimes_{\mathbb{S}}(M^{\rm gp}/N^{\rm gp}).

4.

\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(A,M)/(R,L)}
is canonically equivalent to the colimit of the following diagram

A\otimes_{\mathbb{S}}(M^{\rm gp}/L^{\rm gp})\longleftarrow A\otimes_{\mathbb{S}[M]}\mathbb{L}_{\mathbb{S}[M]/\mathbb{S}[L]}\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}_{A/R}.

Proof.

The assertion (1) are deduced from Proposition
3.27
. We now prove the
(2)
. There is a pushout square of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log rings:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(A,GL_{1}(A))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(B,GL_{1}(B))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(A,M)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(B,N)}}

Since the morphism
(A,M)\rightarrow(B,N)
is strict. On the other hand, Gabber’s cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}^{G}:\mathbf{Log}\rightarrow{\rm T}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}
preserves colimits, therefore we have a pushout square in
\mathfrak{M}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(A,GL_{1}(A))}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(B,GL_{1}(B))}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(A,M)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(B,N)}}}

Taking cofiber, we get

\mathbb{L}_{B/A}\simeq\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(B,GL_{1}(B)/(A,GL_{1}(A))}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(B,N)/(A,M)}.

To prove (3), let us consider the tangent bundle
{\rm T}_{\mathbf{CMon}}
of
the
\infty
-category
\mathbf{CMon}
.
Let
M
be an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-monoid. Using Remark
3.20
, we can form a commutative diagram of
\infty
-categories

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Spt}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{Mod}_{\mathbb{S}[M]}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{CMon}_{/M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{Log}_{/\mathbb{S}[M]}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P\mapsto(\mathbb{S}[P],P)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{L}_{M/-}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(R,L)\mapsto L}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{L}^{G}_{\mathbb{S}[M]/-}}

The relative cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{M/N}
is equivalent to
M^{\rm gp}/N^{\rm gp}
, for an arbitrary
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-monoid
N
. Let
J
be a connective
\mathbb{S}[M]
-module, then we have

\displaystyle{\rm Map}_{\mathbb{S}[M]}(\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(\mathbb{S}[M],M)/(\mathbb{S}[N],N)},J)

\displaystyle\simeq{\rm Map}_{\mathbf{Spt}}(\mathbb{L}_{M/N},J)

\displaystyle\simeq{\rm Map}_{\mathbf{Spt}}(M^{\rm gp}/N^{\rm gp},J)

\displaystyle\simeq{\rm Map}_{\mathbb{S}[M]}(\mathbb{S}[M]\otimes_{\mathbb{S}}(M^{\rm gp}/N^{\rm gp}),J).

As for (4), note that there is a pushout square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{S}[L]\rightarrow\mathbb{S}[M])}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{((\mathbb{S}[L],L)\rightarrow(\mathbb{S}[M],M))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(R\rightarrow A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{((R,L)\rightarrow(A,M))}}

in
{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log})
, and Gabber’s cotangent functor
\mathbb{L}^{G}:{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log})\rightarrow{\rm T}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}
is a left adjoint.
∎

Corollary 3.34

(Fundamental sequence)
.

[
14
, Corollary 3.9]

Let
f:(R,L)\rightarrow(A,M)
be a map of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log rings, then there is an exact sequence

A\otimes_{\mathbb{S}}(M^{\rm gp}/N^{\rm gp})\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(A,M)/(R,L)}\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}_{A/{R\otimes_{\mathbb{S}[L]}\mathbb{S}[M]}}.

Proof.

By Lemma
3.33
(4), we have a pushout square of
A
-modules

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{A\otimes_{\mathbb{S}[M]}\mathbb{L}_{\mathbb{S}[M]/\mathbb{S}[L]}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{L}_{A/R}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A\otimes_{\mathbb{S}}(M^{\rm gp}/L^{\rm gp})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(A,M)/(R,L)}}}

where the cofiber of the upper horizontal arrow is equivalent to

{\rm cofib}(\mathbb{L}_{R/\mathbb{S}[L]}\otimes_{R}A\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}_{A/\mathbb{S}[M]})\simeq\mathbb{L}_{A/R\otimes_{\mathbb{S}[L]}\mathbb{S}[M]}.

∎

At the end of this section, we give the following remark, which provides an intuition for our abstract construction of log cotangent complexes and log derivations, arising from the classical observations in log differential forms.

Remark 3.35

(Log Kähler differentials from replete diagonals)
.

Recall that the classical module of Kähler differential forms
\Omega^{1}_{A/R}
of an ordinary ring map
f:R\rightarrow A
is isomorphic to the indecomposables of its diagonal
\mu:A\otimes_{R}A\rightarrow A
, namely, it is the conormal
I/I^{2}
, where
I
is the kernel of
\mu
. In the derived setting, a similar result also holds, which identifies the cotangent complex of
f
with its derived indecomposables, c.f.
[
6
]
.

However, in log geometry, the indecomposables are not well defined for a log ring map
g:(R,L)\rightarrow(A,M)
, and the indecomposables of the underlying ring map of the diagonal

\mu:(A,M)\otimes_{(R,L)}(A,M)\longrightarrow(A,M)

only recover the differentials of the underlying ring map
R\rightarrow A
. An idea to resolve this issue is to pass the map
\mu
to a strict map in a functorial way, and we consider the indecomposables of the underlying ring map. Consider the exactification of the map
\mu
, and then we have a strict map whose underlying ring map is

\mu^{\rm ex}:(A\otimes_{R}A)\otimes_{R[M\oplus_{L}M]}R[(M\oplus_{L}M)^{\rm ex}]\longrightarrow A.

We refer to
\mu^{\rm ex}
as the replete diagonals of
g
.
As a consequence of
[
28
, Corollary 4.2.8(ii)]
, the module of indecomposables of
\mu^{\rm ex}
is canonically equivalent to the modules of log differentials
\Omega^{1}_{(A,M)/(R,L)}
.

3.3
Deformation theory

In this subsection, we apply the tangent bundles and tangent correspondences formalisms for studying the deformation of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log rings. We will define the notions of deformations of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log rings, which should be viewed as the logarithmic analog of deformations of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log rings. We also study the deformation theory of animated log rings. The main results of this subsection are Theorem
3.44
and Theorem
3.66
.

3.3.1
Square-zero extensions

Definition 3.36
.

Let
(A,M)
be an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log ring, and let
I\in\mathbf{Mod}_{A}
be an
A
-module. A
square-zero extension
of
A
with kernel
I
is a strict morphism
i:(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})\longrightarrow(A,M),
such that
A^{\prime}
is a square-zero
[
37
, Definition 7.4.1.6]
extension of
A
with kernel
I
. The
\infty
-category of square-zero extensions
\mathbf{Def}^{\mathbf{Log}}
is the subcategory of
{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log})
spanned by square-zero extensions.

Remark 3.37
.

If
(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})\rightarrow(A,M)
is a square-zero extension, the associated
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-monoid map
M^{\prime}\rightarrow M
exhibits
M
as a quotient
M^{\prime}/I
,
where the action of
I
on
M^{\prime}
is given by the canonical map
I\simeq(1+I)\subset GL_{1}(A^{\prime})\rightarrow M^{\prime}
.
We can view
M^{\prime}
as an
I
-torsor lying over
M
. The
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-monoid

M^{\prime}
is determined by an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-monoid map
M\rightarrow BI\simeq I[1]
, whose fiber is equivalent to
M^{\prime}
. In particular, we have a map of fiber sequences

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces BI}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{\infty}A^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{\infty}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces BI}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

Consider a log derivation
d:(A,M)\rightarrow I[1],I\in\mathbf{Mod}_{A}
. By the definition of log derivations, this gives rise to a pullback square in
\mathfrak{M}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(A,M)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{I[1]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d}

where the pullback
(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})\in\mathbf{Log}
and
(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})\rightarrow(A,M)
is strict, since there is an equivalence

(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})\simeq(A,M)\times_{(A\oplus\tau_{\geq 0}(I[1]),M\oplus\tau_{\geq 0}(I[1]))}(A,M)

given by two maps

({\rm id},0),({\rm id},d):(A,M)\rightarrow(A\oplus\tau_{\geq 0}(I[1]),M\oplus\tau_{\geq 0}(I[1])).

It turns out that it is a square-zero extension of
(A,M)
with kernel
I
. After taking projection to the upper horizontal arrows, we get a
functor

\gamma:\mathbf{Der}^{\mathbf{Log}}\longrightarrow\mathbf{Def}^{\mathbf{Log}}

Proposition 3.38
.

[
33
, Proposition 7.5]

The functor
\gamma
is essentially surjective. In other words, let
(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})\rightarrow(A,M)
be a square-zero deformation of
(A,M)
, then there exists a log derivation
d:(A,M)\rightarrow I[1]
, such that there is a pullback square:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(A,M)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{I[1]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d}

Proof.

We work in the
(\infty,2)
-category
\mathbf{Corr}
of correspondences. Consider a commutative diagram in
\mathbf{Corr}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm T}_{\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Log}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm Fun}^{\rm ext}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm T}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{M}^{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{M}^{T}_{\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{M}^{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{M}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}}

This gives rise to a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{M}}^{T}_{\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{M}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}

Restricted to the fibers over
0\in\Delta^{1}
, one has
F|_{0}:\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}\rightarrow\mathbf{Log}
is the functor that sends an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring
A
to
(A,GL_{1}(A))
, and restricted to the fibers over
1\in\Delta^{1}
, one has
F|_{1}:{\rm T}_{\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}}\simeq{\rm T}_{\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}}\times_{\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}}\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}\rightarrow{\rm T}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}\simeq{\rm T}_{\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}}\times_{\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}}\mathbf{Log}
is just the obvious functor.
Note that the functor
F
is fiber-wise left and right adjointable
[
49
, Section 7]
, and obviously preserves Cartesian and coCartesian edges. Then by
[
37
, Proposition 7.3.2.6]
,
F
admits a relative right adjoint
G^{R}:\mathfrak{M}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{M}}^{T}_{\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}}
, which should be thought of as the forgetful functor, and a relative left adjoint functor
G^{L}:\mathfrak{M}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{M}}^{T}_{\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}}
, which should be thought of as the trivial locus functor. Informally, the functor
G^{L}
sends an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log ring
(A,M)\in\mathfrak{M}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}\times_{\Delta^{1}}\{0\}\simeq\mathbf{Log}
to
A[M^{-1}]=A\otimes_{\mathbb{S}[M]}\mathbb{S}[M^{\rm gp}]
, and sends an object
((A,M),I)\in\mathfrak{M}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}\times_{\Delta^{1}}\{1\}\simeq{\rm T}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}
to
(A[M^{-1}],I\otimes_{A}A[M^{-1}])
.

Thus the functor
F
preserves both arbitrary small limits and colimits. Now consider a square-zero extension
(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})\rightarrow(A,M)
of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log rings. The underlying square-zero extension
A^{\prime}\rightarrow A
gives rise to a pullback square in
\mathfrak{M}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(A^{\prime},GL_{1}(A^{\prime}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(A,GL_{1}(A))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{I[1]}}

On the other hand, we have a pushout square of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log rings

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(A^{\prime},GL_{1}(A^{\prime}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(A,GL_{1}(A))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(A,M)}}

because of the strictness of the morphisms
(A^{\prime},GL_{1}(A^{\prime}))\rightarrow(A,GL_{1}(A))
and
(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})\rightarrow(A,M)
. Thus we can form a commutative diagram in
\mathfrak{M}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(A^{\prime},GL_{1}(A^{\prime}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(A,GL_{1}(A))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(A,M)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{I[1]}}

We must show that the lower square is a pullback square. Unwinding the definition of replete correspondence
\mathfrak{M}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}
, we have to prove the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(A,M)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(A,M)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(A\oplus\tau_{\geq 0}(I[1]),M\oplus\tau_{\geq 0}(I[1]))}}

Since the limit in the
\infty
-category
\mathbf{Log}
is calculated pointwise, we have to check the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{M\oplus\tau_{\geq 0}(I[1])}}

is a pullback square in
\mathbf{Log}
. Indeed, we have
M\simeq M^{\prime}/\tau_{\geq 0}I
, and the upper horizontal arrow and the lower horizontal arrow have the same fibers.
∎

Remark 3.39
.

As mentioned in
[
37
, Warning 7.4.1.10]
, the functor
\gamma
is not an equivalence. A derivation
d:(A,M)\rightarrow I[1]
defines a square-zero extension
(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})\rightarrow(A,M)
via the functor
\gamma
, and every square-zero extension
(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})\rightarrow(A,M)
arises in this way. However, the derivation
d
is not uniquely determined by
(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})
. For example, it is not sensitive to the coconnective part
\tau_{\leq 0}I
of
I
.

Definition 3.40
.

Let
(R,L)
be an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log ring, and let
(R^{\prime},L^{\prime})
be a square-zero extension of
(R,L)
with kernel
I\in\mathbf{Mod}_{R}
. Let
(A,M)
be an
(R,L)
-algebra.
A deformation of
(A,M)
to
(R^{\prime},L^{\prime})
is a
square-zero extension
(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})
of
(A,M)
with kernel
J\in\mathbf{Mod}_{A}
, such that there is an equivalence
J\simeq I_{A}=I\otimes_{R}A
of
A
-modules, and the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(R^{\prime},L^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(R,L)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(A,M)}}

is a pushout square in
\mathbf{Log}
.

Definition 3.41
.

Let
\mathbf{Def}^{\mathbf{Log},+}
be the subcategory of
\mathbf{Def}^{\mathbf{Log}}
spanned by the following data:

1.

Objects are same with those in
\mathbf{Def}^{\mathbf{Log}}
;

2.

Morphisms are those morphisms
((R^{\prime},L^{\prime})\rightarrow(R,L))\rightarrow((A^{\prime},M^{\prime})\rightarrow(A,M))
in
\mathbf{Def}^{\mathbf{Log}}
such that
(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})
is a deformation of
(A,M)
to
(R^{\prime},L^{\prime}).

Definition 3.42
.

Let
\mathbf{Der}^{\mathbf{Log},+}
be the subcategory of
\mathbf{Der}^{\mathbf{Log}}
spanned by the following data:

1.

Objects are derivations
d:(A,M)\rightarrow I[1]
such that
I
is connective;

2.

Morphisms are those morphisms
f:(d:(A,M)\rightarrow I)\rightarrow(d^{\prime}:(B,N)\rightarrow J)
in
\mathbf{Der}^{\mathbf{Log}}
such that the induced map
I\otimes_{A}B\rightarrow J
is an equivalence.

Remark 3.43
.

The existence of the
\infty
-categories
\mathbf{Def}^{\mathbf{Log},+}
and
\mathbf{Der}^{\mathbf{Log},+}
is provided by
[
39
, Proposition 4.1.2.10]
.

The following is the main theorem of this section:

Theorem 3.44

(Deformation theory)
.

The functor
\gamma^{+}:\mathbf{Der}^{\mathbf{Log},+}\rightarrow\mathbf{Def}^{\mathbf{Log},+}
induced from
\gamma
is a left fibration.

The proof of Theorem
3.44
will be given in the next subsection. Let us consider various consequences of it. Unwinding Theorem
3.44
, let
(d:(R,L)\rightarrow I[1])\in\mathbf{Der}^{\mathbf{Log},+}
be a derivation, we get an equivalence
\mathbf{Der}^{\mathbf{Log},+}_{d/}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathbf{Def}^{\mathbf{Log},+}_{\gamma^{+}(d)/}
. The left hand term is the
\infty
-category of morphisms of derivations

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(R,L)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{I[1]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(A,M)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{J[1]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d^{\prime}}

such that
J[1]\simeq I[1]\otimes_{R}A,
and the right hand term is the
\infty
-category of deformations
(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})\rightarrow(A,M)
to
(R^{\prime},L^{\prime})
. In particular, we have

Corollary 3.45
.

The functors
\mathbf{Der}^{\mathbf{Log},+}_{d/}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\gamma^{+}}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathbf{Def}^{\mathbf{Log},+}_{\gamma^{+}(d)/}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle F}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathbf{Log}_{(R^{\prime},L^{\prime})/}

are equivalences.

Proof.

We only need to prove that the second functor
F
is an equivalence of
\infty
-categories. Let
(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})
be an
(R^{\prime},L^{\prime})
-algebra. The fiber of
F
at
(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})
is a contractible anima by
[
35
, Proposition 4.3.2.15]
. On the other hand, the functor
F
is essentially surjective. It follows that
F
is a trivial Kan fibration.
∎

Corollary 3.46
.

Let
(R^{\prime},L^{\prime})\rightarrow(R,L)
be a square-zero extension with kernel
I\in\mathbf{Mod}_{R}^{\rm cn}
, induced by the derivation
d:(R,L)\rightarrow I[1]
.

1.

Let
(R,L)\rightarrow(A,M)
be a morphism of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log rings. The obstruction to the existence of deformations of
(A,M)
to
(R^{\prime},L^{\prime})
lies in
\mathrm{Ext}^{2}(\mathbb{L}_{(A,M)/(R,L)},I_{A}).

2.

If the map
\mathbb{L}_{(R,L)}\otimes_{R}A\rightarrow I_{A}[1]
induced from
d
vanishes (for example,
d
is a trivial derivation), then there is an equivalence of animae

{\rm Map}_{A}(\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(A,M)/(R,L)},I_{A}[1])\simeq\mathbf{Def}^{\simeq}((A,M)/(R,L),d)

where
\mathbf{Def}^{\simeq}((A,M)/(R,L),d)
is the anima of deformations of
(A,M)
to
(R^{\prime},L^{\prime}).

Proof.

The existence of deformations of
(A,M)
to
(R^{\prime},L^{\prime})
is equivalent to the existence of extensions of
d\otimes_{R}A
to the map
\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(A,M)}\rightarrow I_{A}[1]
by the above Corollary
3.45
.
As we have the conormal exact sequence of Gabber’s cotangent complexes

\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(A,M)/(R,L)}[-1]\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(R,L)}\otimes_{R}A\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(A,M)},

the extension exists if and only if the composition
\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(A,M)/(R,L)}[-1]\rightarrow\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(R,L)}\otimes_{R}A\rightarrow I_{A}[1]
vanishes, i.e., it’s vanishing in the mapping class group
\pi_{0}{\rm Map}(\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(A,M)/(R,L)}[-1],I_{A}[1])\simeq{\rm Ext}^{2}(\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(A,M)/(R,L)}[-1],I_{A}[1]).

∎

The assertion (2) is directly obtained from Theorem
3.44
.

Remark 3.47
.

For classical log rings, the equivalences mentioned in Corollary
3.46
can be constructed directly; see
[
27
]
and
[
46
]
for details. We provide a brief sketch. Let
(R,L)
be a discrete log ring and let
I
be a discrete
R
-module. Let
\partial\in\pi_{0}{\rm{\rm Map}}(\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(R,L)},I[1])={\rm Ext}^{1}(\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(R,L)},I)
be a log derivation, the corresponding square-zero extension
(R^{\partial},L^{\partial})
is given by the following pullback square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(R^{\partial},L^{\partial})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(R,L)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(R,L)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(R\oplus I[1],L\oplus I[1])}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{\rm triv}}

Conversely, assume
(R^{\prime},L^{\prime})
is a square-zero extension of
(R,L)
. The underlying ring
R^{\prime}
is a square-zero extension of
R
, it is determined by a derivation

d:R\longrightarrow R\oplus I[1].

The derivation
d_{L}:L\longrightarrow L\oplus I[1]
on the log part is defined using the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{1+I}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{1+I}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{R^{\prime*}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{L^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{R^{*}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

by letting
d_{L}
be the natural map
L\longrightarrow\Sigma(1+I)\simeq I[1].

Remark 3.48
.

As mentioned in the proof of Proposition
3.38
, there is a fully faithful embedding
\mathfrak{M}^{T}_{\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}}\subset\mathfrak{M}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}
over
\Delta^{1}
, and it follows that there is a fully faithful embeddings
\mathbf{Der}(\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn})\subset\mathbf{Der}^{\mathbf{Log}}
and
\mathbf{Def}(\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn})\subset\mathbf{Def}
. Restricted to
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log rings which have trivial log structures, the deformation theory of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log ring Theorem
3.44
coincides with the deformation theory of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log rings established in
[
37
, Theorem 7.4.2.7]

3.4
Proof of Theorem
3.44

In this subsection, we will prove Theorem
3.44
. Our approach depends on certain constructions and results of deformation theory of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings formulated by Lurie in
[
37
]
.

Let us consider the functors
\Omega^{\infty}:{\rm T}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}\rightarrow\mathbf{Log}
, and
\Omega^{\infty}:{\rm T}_{\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}}\rightarrow\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}
. Applying Grothendieck construction, we get morphisms of fibered
\infty
-categories
\omega_{\mathrm{Log}}:\mathfrak{M}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}\rightarrow\mathbf{Log}\times\Delta^{1}
over
\Delta^{1}
, and
\omega:{\mathfrak{M}}^{T}_{\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}}\rightarrow\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}\times\Delta^{1}
over
\Delta^{1}.

Consider the functor
G^{R}:\mathfrak{M}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{M}}^{T}_{\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}}
defined in the proof of Proposition
3.38
, which preserves Cartesian edges. Form a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{M}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{M}}^{T}_{\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Log}\times\Delta^{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}\times\Delta^{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Log}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega_{\mathrm{Log}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G^{R}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\omega}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}}

Where the middle and lower horizontal arrows are forgetful functors. Therefore we get a relative functor:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{M}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{M}}^{T}_{\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}}\times_{\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}}\mathbf{Log}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta^{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi}

One can see that the functor
\Psi
preserves Cartesian edges.
Moreover, we will show that it is an equivalence.

Lemma 3.49
.

The functor
\Psi
is an equivalence of
\infty
-categories.

Proof.

We only need to check the equivalence at the level of fibers. We have that

\Psi_{0}:\mathfrak{M}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}\times_{\Delta^{1}}\{0\}\simeq\mathbf{Log}\longrightarrow\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}\times_{\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}}\mathbf{Log}\simeq\mathbf{Log}

is actually equivalent to the identity functor. Consider the fibers over
1\in\Delta^{1}
. We have that the functor

\mathfrak{M}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}\times_{\Delta^{1}}\{1\}\simeq{\rm T}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}},

and

({\mathfrak{M}}^{T}_{\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}}\times_{\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}}\mathbf{Log})\times_{\Delta^{1}}\{1\}\simeq{\rm T}_{\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}}\times_{\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}}\mathbf{Log}.

The functor
\Psi_{1}
turns out to be an equivalence from Proposition
3.27
.
∎

Now we consider the
\infty
-category
\mathbf{Der}^{\mathbf{Log}}.
The fully faithful embedding

\mathbf{Der}^{\mathbf{Log}}\hookrightarrow{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathfrak{M}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}})

exhibits
\mathbf{Der}^{\mathbf{Log}}
as a full subcategory of

{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},{\mathfrak{M}}^{T}_{\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}})\times_{{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn})}{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log}).

Then it’s easy to see that we have a factorization

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Der}^{\mathbf{Log}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{Der}\times_{{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn})}{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},{\mathfrak{M}}^{T}_{\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}})\times_{{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn})}{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Phi}

The functor
\Phi
is fully faithful.
Unwinding the definition of log derivations, the essential image of
\Phi
is identified with the subcategory of

{\mathbf{Der}\times_{{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn})}{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log})}

spanned by verticals
\partial
satisfying the following condition:

•

The image of
\partial
under the projection to the second factor

\pi_{2}:\mathbf{Der}\times_{{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn})}{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log})\longrightarrow{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log})

has the form of
(A,M)\rightarrow(A\oplus\tau_{\geq 0}(I[1]),M\oplus\tau_{\geq 0}(I[1]))
for some
(A,M)\in\mathbf{Log}
and
I[1]\in\mathbf{Mod}_{A}
.

We denote by
\mathbf{Der}^{+}
the
\infty
-category constructed in
[
37
, Notation 7.4.2.4]
. Informally, it’s the subcategory of
\mathbf{Der}(\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn})
which has the objects
d:A\rightarrow I[1]
with
I\in\mathbf{Mod}_{A}^{\rm cn}
, and morphisms
f:(d:A\rightarrow I)\rightarrow(d^{\prime}:B\rightarrow J)
that satisfy the condition that the induced map
B\otimes_{A}I\rightarrow J
is an equivalence of
A
-modules. Then we have a pullback square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{Der}^{\mathbf{Log},+}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Der}^{+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Der}^{\mathbf{Log}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Der}}

We also denote by
\mathbf{Def}^{+}
the subcategory of
{\rm Fun}^{+}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn})
constructed in
[
37
, Notation 7.4.2.6]
, spanned by square-zero extensions. Informally, objects in
\mathbf{Def}^{+}
are square-zero extensions and morphisms in
\mathbf{Def}^{+}
are deformations. There is a forgetful functor
\eta:\mathbf{Def}^{\mathbf{Log},+}\longrightarrow\mathbf{Def}^{+}

, and a pullback square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{Def}^{\mathbf{Log},+}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Def}^{+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Def}^{\mathbf{Log}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Def}}

The above two pullback squares give rise to fully faithful embeddings

\mathbf{Der}^{\mathbf{Log},+}\subset\mathbf{Der}^{+}\times_{{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn})}{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log})

\mathbf{Def}^{\mathbf{Log},+}\subset\mathbf{Def}^{+}\times_{{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn})}{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log}).

Then we can form a commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{Der}^{\mathbf{Log},+}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Der}^{+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{Def}^{\mathbf{Log},+}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Def}^{+}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma^{+}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta}

Lemma 3.50
.

The commutative diagram given as above is a pullback square.

Proof.

We denote by

\alpha:\mathbf{Der}^{\mathbf{Log},+}\longrightarrow\mathbf{Der}^{+}\times_{\mathbf{Def}^{+}}\mathbf{Def}^{\mathbf{Log},+}

the functor induced by the above commutative diagram. The functor
\alpha
is essentially surjective. Indeed, an object
X
in the right hand term is represented by a triple
(d,\phi,\sigma),
where
d:A\rightarrow I[1]
is a derivation such that
I
is connective, and
\phi:(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})\rightarrow(A,M)
is a square-zero extension which fits into a pullback square in
\mathfrak{M}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}
as follows:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(A,M)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{J[1]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d^{\prime}}

and
\sigma
is an equivalence of square-zero extensions
\sigma:(A^{\prime\prime}\rightarrow A)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\rightarrow}}(A^{\prime}\rightarrow A)
, where
(A^{\prime}\rightarrow A)
is the underlying square-zero extension of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings of
\phi
and
A^{\prime}\rightarrow A
is the square-zero extension of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings induced by
d
. Note that this implies that there is an
A
-module equivalence
I[1]\simeq J[1]
. Therefore, the derivation
d^{\prime}
belongs to
\mathbf{Der}^{\mathbf{Log},+},
and the image of
d^{\prime}
under the functor
\alpha
is equivalent to
X
.

Then we have to prove that the functor
\alpha
is fully faithful. Using the embeddings

\mathbf{Der}^{\mathbf{Log},+}\subset\mathbf{Der}^{+}\times_{{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn})}{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log})

and

\mathbf{Def}^{\mathbf{Log},+}\subset\mathbf{Def}^{+}\times_{{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn})}{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log})

and then we form a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{Der}^{\mathbf{Log},+}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{Der}^{+}\times_{{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn})}{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{Def}^{\mathbf{Log},+}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{Der}^{+}\times_{\mathbf{Def}^{+}}\mathbf{Def}^{+}\times_{{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn})}{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{Der}^{+}\times_{{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn})}{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

Clearly, the functor
\alpha
is fully faithful.
∎

Finally, using Lemma
3.50
and the deformation theory of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings in
[
37
, Theorem 7.4.2.7]
, we get the desired result.

3.5
Deformation theory of animated log rings

The theory of animated log rings provided another building block of derived logarithmic geometry. The difference between
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log rings and animated log rings is that the animated log rings are required to have extra structures via the theorem of Barr-Beck-Lurie. As we have argued in
subsection 2.4
, the
\infty
-category of animated log rings can be viewed as the
\infty
-category of modules over
\mathbf{Log}
with respect to a certain monad
T
. We can associate to every animated log ring
(A,M)
an underlying
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log ring structure
(A^{\circ},M^{\circ})
via the forgetful functor
\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Theta_{\rm Log}}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathbf{Log}_{\mathbb{Z}}\rightarrow\mathbf{Log}
. This functor admits a left adjoint
\Theta^{L}_{\rm Log}
, which is monadic, because the forgetful functor
\mathbf{Log}_{\mathbb{Z}}\rightarrow\mathbf{Log}
is monadic (because it is conservative, preserves arbitrary small limits, and geometric realizations).

Remark 3.51
.

Even restricted to the characteristic zero case, the forgetful functor
\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta}_{\mathbb{Q}}\rightarrow\mathbf{Log}_{\mathbb{Q}}
is not an equivalence.

3.5.1
Algebraic log cotangent complexes and algebraic log derivations

Definition 3.52
.

The
replete tangent bundle of animated log rings
is the fiber product
\mathbf{Ani}{\rm T}^{\rm rep}_{{\mathbf{Log}}^{\Delta}}:={\rm T}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}\times_{\mathbf{Log}}{\mathbf{Log}}^{\Delta}
.

Remark 3.53
.

The replete tangent bundle of animated log rings
\mathbf{Ani}{\rm T}^{\rm rep}_{{\mathbf{Log}}^{\Delta}}
is not equivalent to the replete tangent bundle given by the forgetful functor
U:{\mathbf{Log}}^{\Delta}\rightarrow\mathbf{CAlg}^{\Delta}.

Consider the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Ani}{\rm T}^{\rm rep}_{{\mathbf{Log}}^{\Delta}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm T}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm Fun}^{\rm ext}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log})\times_{\mathbf{Log}}\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm Fun}^{\rm ext}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Log}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\Omega}^{\infty}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm pr}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{\infty}}

Here the functor
{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta})\rightarrow{\rm Fun}^{\rm ext}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log})\times_{\mathbf{Log}}\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta}
is given by the product
\lambda\times{\rm ev}_{1}
, where
\lambda
is defined as the composition

\lambda:{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(-)^{\circ}}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\rm ex}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log})

Lemma 3.54
.

The functor
\widetilde{\Omega}^{\infty}:\mathbf{Ani}{\rm T}^{\rm rep}_{{\mathbf{Log}}^{\Delta}}\rightarrow{\rm Fun}^{\rm ext}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log})\times_{\mathbf{Log}}\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta}
uniquely factors through a limit-preserving functor

\Omega^{\infty,\Delta}:\mathbf{Ani}{\rm T}^{\rm rep}_{{\mathbf{Log}}^{\Delta}}\rightarrow{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta})
, such that the following diagram is commutative.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Ani}{\rm T}^{\rm rep}_{{\mathbf{Log}}^{\Delta}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm T}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{\infty,\Delta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm pr}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega_{\rm Log}^{\infty}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\circ}}

Proof.

We first prove the existence of the functor
\Omega^{\infty,\Delta}.

Note that all functors except
\Omega^{\infty,\Delta}
in the commutative diagram are left adjointable. We have to prove that there is a colimit-preserving functor
\mathcal{L}:{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta})\rightarrow\mathbf{Ani}{\rm T}^{\rm rep}_{{\mathbf{Log}}^{\Delta}},
such that the following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},{\mathbf{Log}}^{\Delta})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm T}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Ani}{\rm T}^{\rm rep}_{{\mathbf{Log}}^{\Delta}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{L}^{G}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\Delta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{L}}

Here the functor
(-)^{\Delta}
is the left adjoint of the functor
(-)^{\circ}.
We can choose the functor
\mathcal{L}
as the left Kan extension of
\mathbb{L}^{G}

along the functor
(-)^{\Delta}.
The right adjoint
\hat{\Omega}^{\infty,\Delta}
of
\mathcal{L}
satisfies the desired property, which is terminal in the
\infty
-category of all functors
\omega:\mathbf{Ani}{\rm T}^{\rm rep}_{{\mathbf{Log}}^{\Delta}}\rightarrow{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},{\mathbf{Log}}^{\Delta})
such that the diagram appearing in this proposition commutes.

We prove the uniqueness of the functor
\Omega^{\infty,\Delta}
. Let
\omega:\mathbf{Ani}{\rm T}^{\rm rep}_{{\mathbf{Log}}^{\Delta}}\rightarrow{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},{\mathbf{Log}}^{\Delta})
be any functor which makes the diagram in this proposition commute. There is a unique natural transformation
\xi:\omega\rightarrow\hat{\Omega}^{\infty,\Delta}
according to the construction of the functor
\hat{\Omega}^{\infty,\Delta}
.
The forgetful functor
(-)^{\circ}
is monadic. Let
T
be the monad associated with
(-)^{\circ}.
Let us identify the
\infty
-category
{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},{\mathbf{Log}}^{\Delta})
with
{\mathbf{L}}\mathbf{Mod}_{T}({\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log}))
the
\infty
-category of
T
-modules in
{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log})
. It follows that the equivalence of
\xi
is detected by its underlying natural transformation
(-)^{\circ}\circ\xi
. It’s clear that the natural transformation
(-)^{\circ}\circ\xi
is an equivalence.
∎

Definition 3.55
.

The algebraic Gabber’s cotangent complex functor

\mathbb{L}^{G,\Delta}:{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta})\rightarrow\mathbf{Ani}{\rm T}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta}}

is the left adjoint of the functor
\Omega^{\infty,\Delta}
.

Remark 3.56

(Gabber’s cotangent complex as derived functors)
.

The algebraic Gabber’s cotangent complex of a map of animated log rings
(A,M)\rightarrow(B,N)
is characterized by the following universal properties

{\rm Map}_{(A,M)//(B,N)}((B,N),(B\oplus J,N\oplus J))\simeq{\rm Map}_{B}(\mathbb{L}^{G,\Delta}_{(B,N)/(A,M)},J),J\in\mathbf{Mod}_{B}^{\rm cn}.

The Gabber’s cotangent complex of animated log rings is originally defined as the non-abelian derived functor of the functor of modules of log differential forms, c.f.
[
46
, Definition 8.5]
,
[
51
, Definition 4.7]
and
[
14
, Definition 3.5]
. By definition, it’s characterized by the same universal property with
\mathbb{L}^{G,\Delta}
