. This implies that the two definitions of Gabber’s cotangent complex coincide.

Definition 3.57
.

The
replete tangent correspondence of animated log rings

\mathbf{Ani}\mathfrak{M}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta}}
is the fiber product

\mathfrak{M}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}\times_{\mathbf{Log}}\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta}.

Definition 3.58
.

The
\infty
-category of
algebraic log derivations

\mathbf{Der}^{\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta}}
is the subcategory of
{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Ani}\mathfrak{M}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta}})
spanned by those functors

d:\Delta^{1}\rightarrow\mathbf{Ani}\mathfrak{M}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta}}
such that the composition

\Delta^{1}\longrightarrow\mathbf{Ani}\mathfrak{M}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta}}\longrightarrow\Delta^{1}\times\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta}

is the functor
\Delta^{1}\rightarrow\Delta^{1}\times\{(A,M)\}\subset\Delta^{1}\times\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta}
for some animated log ring
(A,M)
.

Unwinding the definition, we can form a commutative diagram of
\infty
-categories

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Der}^{\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{Der}^{\mathbf{Log}}\times_{{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log})}{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Ani}\mathfrak{M}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathfrak{M}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}})\times_{{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log})}{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

3.5.2
Algebraic deformation theory

Definition 3.59
.

Let
(A,M)
be an animated log ring, and let
I\in\mathbf{Mod}_{A}
be an
A
-module. A
square-zero extension
of
A
with kernel
I
is a strict morphism of animated log rings
i:(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})\rightarrow(A,M)
, such that
A^{\prime}
is a square-zero extension of
A
with kernel
I
.

Remark 3.60
.

Obviously there is a pullback square of
\infty
-categories

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Def}^{\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Def}^{\mathbf{Log}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log})}}

Definition 3.61
.

Let
(R,L)
be an animated log ring, and let
(R^{\prime},L^{\prime})
be a square-zero extension of
(R,L)
with kernel
I\in\mathbf{Mod}_{R}
. Let
(A,M)
be an
(R,L)
-algebra,
a deformation of
(A,M)
to
(R^{\prime},L^{\prime})
is a
square-zero extension
(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})
of
(A,M)
with kernel
J\in\mathbf{Mod}_{A}
, such that there is an equivalence
J\simeq I_{A}=I\otimes_{R}A
of
A
-modules, and the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(R^{\prime},L^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(R,L)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(A,M)}}

is a pushout square in
\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta}
.

Definition 3.62
.

Let
\mathbf{Def}^{\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta},+}
be the subcategory of
\mathbf{Def}^{\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta}}
spanned by the following data:

1.

Objects are same with those in
\mathbf{Def}^{\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta}}
;

2.

An morphism
((R^{\prime},L^{\prime})\rightarrow(R,L))\rightarrow((A^{\prime},M^{\prime})\rightarrow(A,M))
in
\mathbf{Def}^{\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta}}
belongs to
\mathbf{Def}^{\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta},+}
if and only if
(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})
is a deformation of
(A,M)
to
(R^{\prime},L^{\prime}).

Definition 3.63
.

Let
\mathbf{Der}^{\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta},+}
be the subcategory of
\mathbf{Der}^{\mathbf{Log}\Delta}
as follows:

1.

Objects are those derivations
d:(A,M)\rightarrow I[1]
such that
I
is connective.

2.

Morphisms are those mosprhisms having the form
f:(d:(A,M)\rightarrow I)\rightarrow(d^{\prime}:(B,N)\rightarrow J)
such that the induced map
I\otimes_{A}B\rightarrow J
is an equivalence.

Remark 3.64
.

There are pullback squares

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{Der}^{\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta},+}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{Der}^{\mathbf{Log},+}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{Def}^{\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta},+}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{Def}^{\mathbf{Log},+}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log})}}

Remark 3.65
.

We have a functor
\gamma^{+}:\mathbf{Der}^{\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta},+}\rightarrow\mathbf{Def}^{\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta},+}
same as that defined in Theorem
3.44

We have the analogous of Theorem
3.44
in the animated context.

Theorem 3.66

(Algebraic deformation theory)
.

The functor
\gamma^{+}:\mathbf{Der}^{\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta},+}\rightarrow\mathbf{Def}^{\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta},+}
induced from
\gamma
is a left fibration.

Proof.

Using Remark
3.65
, we have a pullback square of
\infty
-categories

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{Der}^{\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta},+}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{Der}^{\mathbf{Log},+}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{Def}^{\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta},+}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{Def}^{\mathbf{Log},+}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma^{+}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma^{+}}

Then applying Theorem
3.44
, we get the desired result.
∎

4
Log stacks

The development of logarithmic geometry is due to the study of the geometry and cohomology of singular varieties and schemes. The foundation of this theory was given by Kato
[
29
]
, following the ideas of Fontaine-Illusie. A
log scheme
is a triple
(X,\mathcal{M},\alpha)
, where
X
is a scheme and
\alpha:\mathcal{M}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}
is a map of sheaves of monoids, with an extra condition
\mathcal{O}_{X}^{*}\times_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{M}\simeq\mathcal{O}^{*}_{X}
. Earlier than that, in Deligne’s papers
[
19
]
,
[
20
]
and
[
21
]
on the theory of mixed Hodge structures, he studied an original version of logarithmic geometry, in which the geometric objects he considered are complex projective manifolds equipped with normal crossing divisors
(X,D)
, which should be thought as varieties with boundary. We note that this approach is naturally related to the singular varieties, as for any resolution of singularities (if it exists)
\pi:\widetilde{X}\rightarrow X
of a variety
X
over a field
k
, it gives rise to a pair
(\widetilde{X},E)
, where
E
is the exceptional divisor. One feature of logarithmic geometry is that it enlarges the notion of smoothness to log-smoothness, which allows us to treat a large class of singular schemes, for example, varieties with resolvable singularities and semi-stable schemes over DVRs, similarly to smooth ones.

On the other hand, the derived algebraic geometry studies higher structures of geometric objects in algebraic geometry. Many algebraic and topological invariants associated to schemes came from derived algebraic geometry, such as cotangent complexes, topological André-Quillen homology, topological Hochschild homology and so on. So the logarithmic geometry and derived algebraic geometry meet in the study of the logarithmic version of certain higher invariants.

In this section, we continue working in the
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-context. We will study the notion of
charted log stacks
, which has been studied in
[
51
]
in the context of simplicial log rings, and
log structures
associated with spectral Deligne-Mumford stacks. Then we will study the moduli stack of all log stacks and will prove the descent property.

4.1
Charted log stacks

In classical logarithmic geometry, a chart on a log scheme
(X,\mathcal{M})
is a map of monoids
\tau:M\rightarrow\Gamma(X,\mathcal{M}),
which yields an isomorphism of sheaves of monoids
\tau^{\prime}:\underline{M}^{a}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{M},

where
\underline{M}
is the constant sheaf on
X
associated with
M
.
A charted log scheme is a quadruple
(X,\mathcal{M},M,\tau),
where
(X,\mathcal{M})

is a log scheme, and
\tau:M\rightarrow\Gamma(X,\mathcal{M})
is a chart of
(X,\mathcal{M})
.
We will generalize this notion to spectral stacks in this subsection.

4.1.1
Topologies on
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log rings

Let
f:(A,M)\rightarrow(B,N)
be a morphism of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log rings, recall that we have constructed Gabber’s cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(B,N)/(A,M)}
. Similar to derived algebraic geometry, we have the following definition:

Definition 4.1
.

We say that a morphism of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log rings
f:(A,M)\rightarrow(B,N)
is

1.

Formally log-smooth
if the cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(B,N)/(A,M)}
is projective in
\mathbf{Mod}_{B}
, i.e., it is a direct summand of a free
B
-module.

2.

Formally log-étale
if the cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(B,N)/(A,M)}
is contractible.

3.

Log-smooth
if it is formally smooth and the underlying ring map
f:A\rightarrow B
is almost finitely presented, c.f.
[
37
, Definition 7.2.4.26]
.

4.

Log-étale
if it is formally étale and the underlying ring map
f:A\rightarrow B
is almost finitely presented.

Definition 4.2
.

The collection of strict étale (resp. log-étale) morphisms forms a Grothendieck topology on the
\infty
-category
\mathbf{Log}^{\rm op}
. We denote by
\mathbf{Log}^{\rm op}_{{\rm s\acute{e}t}}
(resp.
\mathbf{Log}^{\rm op}_{{\rm l}{\rm\acute{e}t}}
) the corresponding site.

Let
(A,M)
be an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log ring,
denote by
(A,M)_{{\rm s\acute{e}t}}
(resp.
(A,M)_{{\rm l}{\rm\acute{e}t}}
) the subcategory of
\mathbf{Log}_{(A,M)/}
spanned by strict étale (resp. log-étale) objects over
(A,M)
.

The following proposition gives an analogue in the context of higher algebra of a result due to Vidal about the nil-invariance of the classical log étale site, c.f.
[
55
]
.

Proposition 4.3
.

[
33
, Theorem 1.3]

Let
(A,M)
be an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log ring, and let
(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})
be a square-zero extension of
(A,M)
. Then the base change gives rise to equivalences

\displaystyle-\otimes_{A^{\prime}}A:(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})_{{\rm s\acute{e}t}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}(A,M)_{{\rm s\acute{e}t}},

\displaystyle(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})_{{\rm l}{\rm\acute{e}t}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}(A,M)_{{\rm l}{\rm\acute{e}t}}.

Proof.

The two functors are essentially surjective by Corollary
3.45
. Now we need to prove that it’s fully faithful. The two cases are similar, so we only give a proof for log-étale topology. Let
(B^{\prime},N^{\prime})
and
(C^{\prime},P^{\prime})
be objects in
(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})_{{\rm l}{\rm\acute{e}t}}
, and let
(B,N),(C,P)
be the corresponding base changes to
(A,M)
respectively. We have to prove the following equivalence

{\rm Map}_{(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})}((B^{\prime},N^{\prime}),(C^{\prime},P^{\prime}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\rm Map}_{(A,M)}((B,N),(C,P)).

Note that we have a factorization

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm Map}_{(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})}((B^{\prime},N^{\prime}),(C^{\prime},P^{\prime}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm Map}_{(A,M)}((B,N),(C,P))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm Map}_{(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})}((B^{\prime},N^{\prime}),(C,P))}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

We need to show the map

{\rm Map}_{(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})}((B^{\prime},N^{\prime}),(C^{\prime},P^{\prime}))\longrightarrow{\rm Map}_{(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})}((B^{\prime},N^{\prime}),(C,P))

is an equivalence. Since the mapping space functor preserves limits with respect to the second variable, we just need to show the equivalence for letting
(C^{\prime},P^{\prime})
be
(C\oplus I,P\oplus I)
for some
1
-connective
C
-module
I.
In this case, the fiber of this map at an arbitrary point
g\in{\rm Map}_{(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})}((B^{\prime},N^{\prime}),(C,P))
is equivalent to the mapping space

{\rm Map}_{C}(C\otimes_{B}\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(B,N)/(A,M)},I),

which is contractible because
\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(B,N)/(A,M)}\simeq 0
by assumption.
∎

4.1.2
Precharted log stacks

Definition 4.4
.

A
precharted log stack
is a sheaf over the site
\mathbf{Log}_{{\rm s\acute{e}t}}.
Denote by
\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{Stk}^{\rm prechart}:=\mathbf{Shv}(\mathbf{Log}^{\rm op}_{{\rm s\acute{e}t}},\mathbf{Ani})
the
\infty
-category of precharted log stacks.

Definition 4.5
.

Let
{\rm Spec}(A,M):\mathbf{Log}_{{\rm s\acute{e}t}}\rightarrow\mathbf{Ani}
be the functor
{\rm Map}((A,M),-).
We call it the
charted log-affine scheme
associated with
(A,M)
.

Proposition 4.6
.

[
51
, Proposition 7.13]

The functor
{\rm Spec}(A,M)

satisfies hyperdescent with respect to strict étale topology.

Lemma 4.7
.

Let
f:A\rightarrow B
be a faithfully flat map of connective
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings. Then one has
GL_{1}(A)\simeq GL_{1}(B)\times_{\Omega^{\infty}B}\Omega^{\infty}A
.

Proof.

Consider the fiber sequence

\Omega^{\infty+1}B\longrightarrow\Omega^{\infty}A\times_{\Omega^{\infty}B}GL_{1}(B)\longrightarrow\Omega^{\infty}A\times GL_{1}(B).

It induces a long exact sequence of homotopies

\longrightarrow\pi_{*+1}(B)\longrightarrow\pi_{*}(\Omega^{\infty}A\times_{\Omega^{\infty}B}GL_{1}(B))\longrightarrow\pi_{*}(A)\times\pi_{*}(GL_{1}(B))\longrightarrow\pi_{*}(B)\longrightarrow

For
*\neq 0
, we have
\pi_{*}(B)\simeq\pi_{*}(GL_{1}(B))
. Hence, the exact sequence splits when
*\geq 1
, so we have an isomorphism of abelian groups
\pi_{*}(\Omega^{\infty}A\times_{\Omega^{\infty}B}GL_{1}(B))\simeq\pi_{*}(A),*\neq 0,
and

\pi_{0}(A\times_{B}GL_{1}(B))\simeq\pi_{0}(A)\times_{\pi_{0}(B)}\pi_{0}(GL_{1}(B)).

Since
A\rightarrow B
is faithfully flat,
\pi_{0}(A)\rightarrow\pi_{0}(B)
is faithfully flat too.
It is therefore enough to show the lemma for discrete rings. This is clear. Indeed,
let
f:A\rightarrow B
be a faithfully flat map of discrete rings. Clearly, we have
GL_{1}(A)\subseteq f^{-1}(GL_{1}(B)).
On the other hand, let
x
be an element in
f^{-1}(GL_{1}(B))
, we will construct an inverse of
x
. The
A
-module map
\phi_{x}:A\rightarrow A,a\mapsto ax
is an isomorphism because
\phi_{x}\otimes_{A}B
is an isomorphism. Therefore, there exists
y\in A
such that
\phi_{x}(y)=xy=1.

∎

Lemma 4.8
.

Let
f:(A,M)\rightarrow(B,N)
be a strict morphism such that the underlying map
A\rightarrow B
of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings is faithfully flat, then

N\simeq M\coprod_{GL_{1}(A)}GL_{1}(B)

and
f
is exact.

Proof.

Here
N
is equivalent to the logification of the
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-prelog ring given by
M\rightarrow\Omega^{\infty}A\rightarrow\Omega^{\infty}B
. We note that

\displaystyle M\times_{\Omega^{\infty}B}GL_{1}(B)

\displaystyle\simeq M\times_{\Omega^{\infty}A}\Omega^{\infty}A\times_{\Omega^{\infty}B}GL_{1}(B)

\displaystyle\simeq M\times_{\Omega^{\infty}A}GL_{1}(A)

\displaystyle\simeq GL_{1}(A)

by Lemma
4.7
. We also have

\displaystyle M\times_{N}GL_{1}(B)

\displaystyle\simeq M\times_{N}(GL_{1}(B)\times_{\Omega^{\infty}B}N)

\displaystyle\simeq M\times_{\Omega^{\infty}B}GL_{1}(B)

\displaystyle\simeq M\times_{\Omega^{\infty}A}\Omega^{\infty}A\times_{\Omega^{\infty}B}GL_{1}(B)

\displaystyle\simeq M\times_{\Omega^{\infty}A}GL_{1}(A)

\displaystyle\simeq GL_{1}(A)

This implies that the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces GL_{1}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces GL_{1}(B)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N}

is both Cartesian and coCartesian. Now, we will show that the map
M\rightarrow N
is exact. Assume that there is a factorization
M\rightarrow M^{\prime}\rightarrow N
such that
M^{\prime}
is an exactification of
M
over
N
, we have to show that
M\simeq M^{\prime}
. We have a commutative diagram of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-monoids with exact rows

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces GL_{1}(B)/GL_{1}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M^{\rm gp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N^{\rm gp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces GL_{1}(B)/GL_{1}(A)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rm gp}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rm gp}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rm gp}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

By the assumption
M^{\prime\rm gp}\simeq M^{\rm gp}
, we have that the composition
M^{\prime}\rightarrow N\rightarrow GL_{1}(B)/GL_{1}(A)
is vanishing, and therefore it uniquely factors through
M
. It shows that
M\simeq M^{\prime}
because
M
also has the universal property of exactification.
∎

Proof of Proposition
4.6
.

Let
f^{\bullet}:(B,N)\rightarrow(C^{\bullet},P^{\bullet})
be a hypercover of
(B,N)
with respect to strict étale topology. We have to show that the induced map
\lim f^{\bullet}:(B,N)\rightarrow\lim_{\Delta}(C^{\bullet},P^{\bullet})=:(C^{-1},P^{-1})
is an equivalence. By Lemma
4.8
, it is also a hypercover consisting of exact maps. This means that the map
(B,N)\rightarrow(C^{-1},P^{-1})
could be calculated in the
\infty
-category
{\rm Fun}^{\rm ext}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Log})
. In particular, we have
N\rightarrow P^{-1}
is exact and
N^{\rm gp}\simeq(P^{-1})^{\rm gp}
. By the universal property of exactness, we have
N\simeq P^{-1}
.
∎

Remark 4.9
.

According to the proof of Proposition
4.6
, we see that the functor
{\rm Spec}(A,M)
also satisfies the
strict flat hyperdescent
property.

Besides strict étale topology, the log-étale covers also define a topology on
\mathbf{Log},
which is strictly stronger than the strict étale topology. But there is an issue that this topology is not subcanonical. We have the following example:

Example 4.10
.

Let
(A,M)
be an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log ring. We can construct two different log structures on the
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring
A\times A
. Let
M^{a}
be the logification of the diagonal map

M\longrightarrow\Omega^{\infty}A\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Delta}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Omega^{\infty}(A\times A),

and let

M\times M\longrightarrow\Omega^{\infty}(A\times A)

be the product of the log structure
M\rightarrow\Omega^{\infty}A.

There is a natural morphism
(A\times A,M^{a})\rightarrow(A\times A,M\times M),
which is a log-étale cover. However, the induced Čech nerve of this map is the constant diagram
\{(A\times A,M\times M)\}^{\infty}_{n=0}.
This implies that the log-étale topology is not subcanonical.

4.1.3
Moduli functor of precharted log structures

Applying Lemma
3.33
, one has that there is an adjoint pair

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}_{{\rm\acute{e}t}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Log}_{{\rm s\acute{e}t}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}

of sites. Here
U
is the forgetful functor to the underlying
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings, and
i
is the natural embedding from the
\infty
-category of connective
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings to the
\infty
-category of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log rings.
The induced functor

U^{*}:\mathbf{Stk}\longrightarrow\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{Stk}^{\rm prechart}

preserves both small limits and colimits, and it follows that it admits a right adjoint
U_{*}
and a left adjoint
U_{!}
. In particular, one can see that the functor
U_{!}
is equivalent to the left Kan extension of the composition
\mathbf{Log}^{\rm op}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle U}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn,op}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm Spec}}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathbf{Stk}
along the inclusion
\mathbf{Log}^{\rm op}\subset\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{Stk}^{\rm prechart}
. We have the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Log}^{\rm op}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn,op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Stk}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{Stk}^{\rm prechart}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(A,M)\mapsto{\rm Spec}A}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{!}}

We also denote by
\underline{X}:=U_{!}(X)
a precharted log stack
X\in\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{Stk}^{\rm prechart}
, and refer to it as the
underlying stack
of
X
.
Let
\underline{f}:\underline{X}\rightarrow\underline{Y}
be a morphism of stacks, and let
Y\in\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{Stk}^{\rm prechart}\times_{\mathbf{Stk}}\{\underline{Y}\}
, i.e.,
Y
be a precharted log stack whose underlying stack is equivalent to
\underline{Y}
. One can directly check that the projection map
Y\times_{U^{*}\underline{Y}}U^{*}\underline{X}\rightarrow Y
is a Cartesian edge with respect to
\underline{f}.
We will denote
X
by
\underline{f}^{*}Y
in this case. We deduce that the functor
U_{!}
is a Cartesian fibration.

Definition 4.11
.

We say that a morphism
f:X\rightarrow Y
is strict, if it is a Cartesian edge with respect to the underlying map of stacks
\underline{f}:\underline{X}\rightarrow\underline{Y}.

Remark 4.12
.

By definition, the strictness is stable under base change and composition.

Lemma 4.13
.

Let
\alpha:{\rm Spec}A\rightarrow{\rm Spec}B
be a map of affine spectral schemes, and let
(B,N)
be an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log ring, then we have
\alpha^{*}{\rm Spec}(B,N)\simeq{\rm Spec}(A,N)^{a}
. So the strictness of maps of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log rings and that of maps of charted log-affine schemes coincide.

Proof.

The logification
(A,N)^{a}
is identified with the pushout
(B,N)\otimes_{(B,GL_{1}(B))}(A,GL_{1}(A))
. Here, the tensor product is formed in the
\infty
-category
\mathbf{Log}
. We have the following equivalences of sheaves over the strict étale site

\displaystyle\alpha^{*}{\rm Spec}(B,N)

\displaystyle\simeq{\rm Spec}(B,N)\times_{U^{*}{\rm Spec}B}U^{*}{\rm Spec}A

\displaystyle\simeq{\rm Spec}((B,N)\otimes_{(B,GL_{1}(B))}(A,GL_{1}(A))

\displaystyle\simeq{\rm Spec}(A,N)^{a}.

∎

Let
X
be a precharted log stack, and let
\underline{X}
be its underlying stack
U_{!}(X)
, We have a Cartesian fibration

U^{X}_{!}:\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{Stk}^{\rm prechart}_{/X}\longrightarrow\mathbf{Stk}_{/\underline{X}}.

Let
\underline{Y}\rightarrow\underline{X}
be a map of stacks. We denote by
\widehat{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}}_{X}^{\rm prechart}(\underline{Y})
the fiber of
U^{X}_{!}
at
\underline{Y},
and denote by
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm prechart}_{X}
its core. This defines prestacks

\widehat{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}}^{\rm prechart}_{X}:\mathbf{Stk}^{\rm op}_{/\underline{X}}\longrightarrow\mathbf{Cat}_{\infty},\underline{Y}\mapsto\widehat{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}}_{X}^{\rm prechart}(\underline{Y})

and

\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm prechart}_{X}:\mathbf{Stk}^{\rm op}_{/\underline{X}}\longrightarrow\mathbf{Ani},\underline{Y}\mapsto\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{X}^{\rm prechart}(\underline{Y}).

The functor
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{X}^{\rm prechart}
should be thought of as the moduli functor of precharted log stacks which are lying over
X.

4.1.4
Deformation theory of stacks

In this subsection, we recall the basic theory of deformations of stacks, developed in
[
34
]
and
[
38
]
.
Recall that for a prestack
X\in{\rm Fun}(\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn},\mathbf{Ani})
, there is a notion of
\infty
-category of quasi-coherent complexes defined in
[
38
, Section II.6.2]
. We have seen that the tangent bundle formalism
{\rm T}_{\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}}\rightarrow\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}
is a presentable fibration. In particular, it’s a coCartesian fibration, and hence it determines a functor

\mathbf{Mod}:\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}\longrightarrow\mathbf{Cat}_{\infty},

which sends a connective
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring
A
to the
\infty
-category of modules over
A
. Passing to right Kan extension, we get the functor of the
\infty
-category of quasi-coherent complexes

\mathbf{QCoh}:{\rm Fun}(\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn},\mathbf{Ani})^{\rm op}\longrightarrow\mathbf{Cat}_{\infty}.

Informally, one can refer to the
\infty
-category of quasi-coherent complexes
\mathbf{QCoh}(X)
as the limit

\mathbf{QCoh}(X):={\varprojlim}_{(R,f)}\mathbf{Mod}_{R},

where
(R,f)
runs over all connective
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings lying over
X
, i.e., all maps
f:{\rm Spec}R\rightarrow X
. What’s more, there is a spectral geometric variant
[
38
, Proposition 6.2.3.1]
of Grothendieck’s descent theorem for classical quasi-coherent sheaves in algebraic geometry,
which asserts that the
\infty
-category of quasi-coherent complexes is stable under flat sheafification and satisfies flat descent.

Definition 4.14
.

[
38
, Definition 17.2.4.2]

Let
f:X\rightarrow S
be a map of prestacks. We say that
f
admits a cotangent complex if there exists an almost connective quasi-coherent complex
\mathbb{L}_{X/S}\in\mathbf{QCoh}(X)
, satisfying the following property:

For any connective
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring
R
, and any connective
R
-module
I,
and any
x\in X(R)
, the fiber of the following natural map

X(R\oplus I)\longrightarrow X(R)\times_{S(R)}S(R\oplus I)

at the point
(x,\widetilde{f(x)}),
where
\widetilde{f(x)}
is the following map

{\rm Spec}(R\oplus I)\rightarrow{\rm Spec}R\longrightarrow X\longrightarrow S,

which is denoted by
\mathfrak{D}_{X/S}(x,I)
, is functorially equivalent to the mapping space
{\rm Map}_{R}(x^{*}\mathbb{L}_{X/S},I).

Remark 4.15
.

Informally, the anima
\mathfrak{D}_{X/S}(x,I)
is equivalent to the anima consisting of the following liftings

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Spec}R}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Spec}(R\oplus I)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}

in which the left vertical arrow is induced by the trivial square-zero extension, and the lower horizontal map is the map

{\rm Spec}(R\oplus I)\longrightarrow{\rm Spec}R\longrightarrow X\longrightarrow S.

From this point of view, one can see that the cotangent complex controls the relative square-zero thickenings of relative prestacks.

Definition 4.16
.

[
38
, Definition 17.3.1.5, 17.3.2.1 and 17.3.4.1]

Let
X
be a prestack, we say that
X
is:

1.

Infinitesimally cohesive
, if for any pullback square of connective
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{A^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{B^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}

such that the maps
A\rightarrow B
and
B^{\prime}\rightarrow B
are surjective on
\pi_{0}
with nilpotent kernels. the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X(A^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X(A)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X(B^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{X(B)}}

is a pullback square.

2.

Nilcomplete
, if for any connective
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring
A
, we have an equivalence
X(A)\simeq\varprojlim_{n}X(\tau_{\leq n}A).

3.

Integrable
, if for any
complete connective
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring
A
, with respect to a finitely generated ideal
\mathfrak{a}\subseteq\pi_{0}(A)
, we have an equivalence
X(A)\simeq\varprojlim_{n}X(A/\mathfrak{a}^{n})={\rm Map}({\rm Spf}A,X).

Let
f:X\rightarrow Y
be a map of prestacks. We say that
f
is infinitesimally cohesive (resp. nilcomplete or integrable) if for any connective
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring
R
, and any map
{\rm Spec}R\rightarrow Y,
the pullback
X_{R}:=X\times_{Y}{\rm Spec}R
is infinitesimally cohesive (resp. nilcomplete or integral) as prestacks over
\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}_{R}
.

Definition 4.17
.

[
34
, Definition 3.4.3]
[
1
, Definition 4.6]

Let
f:X\rightarrow Y
be a map of prestacks. We say that
f
is

1.

Formally smooth
if it is nilcomplete, infinitesimally cohesive, and admits a cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{X/Y}
, which is dual to a connective perfect quasi-coherent complex.

2.

Smooth
, if it is formally smooth and locally of finite presentation.

3.

Formally étale
, if it is formally smooth and
\mathbb{L}_{X/Y}\simeq 0.

4.

Étale
, if it is smooth and formally étale.

4.1.5
Deformation theory of charted log stacks

In this subsection, we will define the notion of charted log stacks and study the deformation property of the moduli functor
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{X}^{\rm chart}
of charted log stacks. We will prove that the functor
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{X}^{\rm chart}
admits a cotangent complex, and when restricted to charted log-affine schemes, it coincides with Gabber’s cotangent complex.

In classical logarithmic geometry, a charted log scheme is a quadruple
(X,\mathcal{M},P,u)
, consisting of a log scheme
(X,\mathcal{M})
(we assume that
X
is quasi-compact and quasi-separated) and a chart, i.e., a map of sheaves of monoids
u:\underline{P}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}
, where
\underline{P}
is a constant sheaf induced from a monoid
P
. We refer to
[
7
, Section 1.1]
for more details on classical charted log schemes. We will generalize this to the
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-context.

Definition 4.18
.

A precharted log stack
X
is called a
charted log stack
if, for any
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring
R
and any map
f:{\rm Spec}R\rightarrow\underline{X}
from
{\rm Spec}R
to its underlying stacks, the pullback
f^{*}X
is charted log-affine.

In particular, if
X
is a charted log stack whose underlying stack
\underline{X}\simeq{\rm Spec}R
is affine, then
X
is charted log-affine, i.e., there is a unique
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log ring
(R,L)
, such that
X\simeq{\rm Spec}(R,L)
.

Definition 4.19
.

Let
X
be a charted log stack. We define the
\infty
-category
\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{Stk}^{\rm chart}
of charted log stacks as the subcategory of
\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{Stk}^{\rm prechart}
spanned by charted log stacks, and define
the
moduli
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{X}^{\rm chart}
of charted log stacks lying over
X
as the subfunctor of
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm prechart}_{X}
spanned by charted objects.

Remark 4.20
.

One can see that the natural projection
\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{Stk}^{\rm chart}\rightarrow\mathbf{Stk}
is equivalent to the Grothendieck construction associated with the right Kan extension of the functor
\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}\rightarrow\mathbf{Cat}_{\infty}
which sends an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring
A
to the
\infty
-category of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log rings with underlying
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings equivalent to
A
.

Lemma 4.21
.

Let
X
be a charted log stack, such that the underlying stack
\underline{X}
is representable by a quasi-compact and quasi-separated spectral Deligne-Mumford stack
(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}})
. Then there is an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log ring
(R^{0},L^{0}),
and a strict étale cover of charted log stacks

\pi:{\rm Spec}(R^{0},L^{0})\longrightarrow X.

As a consequence, we can write
X
as the geometric realization of a strict étale of
X
forms of charted log-affine schemes

{\rm colim}_{\Delta}{\rm Spec}(R^{\bullet},L^{\bullet})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}X.

Proof.

Choose an étale cover
\pi:{\rm Spec}A^{0}\rightarrow\underline{X}
. Then the pullback map
\pi^{*}X\rightarrow X
forms a strict étale cover of
X
from a charted log-affine scheme
\pi^{*}X
.
∎

Theorem 4.22
.

Let
S
be a charted log stack, whose underlying stack is representable by a spectral Deligne-Mumford stack
\mathcal{S}
, then

1.

The restricted functor
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{S}^{\rm chart}:\mathbf{SpDM}_{/\mathcal{S}}^{\rm op}\rightarrow\mathbf{Ani}
admits a cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{S}^{\mathrm{chart}}/\underline{S}}
, which is
(-1)
-connective.

2.

If
{\rm Spec}A\rightarrow\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{{\rm Spec}(R,L)}^{\rm chart}
classifies an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log ring map
(R,L)\rightarrow(A,M)
, then
\mathbb{L}_{{\rm Spec}A/\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{{\rm Spec}(R,L)}^{\rm chart}}\simeq\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(A,M)/(R,L)}.

Remark 4.23
.

Here we identify the functor
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{S}^{\rm chart}
as a relative prestack lying over
\underline{S}
via the canonical equivalence
{\rm Fun}(\mathbf{SpDM}_{/\mathcal{S}},\mathbf{Ani})\simeq{\rm Fun}(\mathbf{SpDM},\mathbf{Ani})_{/\underline{S}}
.

Proof of Theorem
4.22
.

We first reduce to the case that
\underline{S}
is equivalent to
{\rm Spec}(R,L)
for some
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log ring
(R,L).
Indeed, the Lemma
4.21
shows that we have a strict étale cover
{\rm Spec}(R^{\bullet},L^{\bullet})\rightarrow S
. We also have
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm chart}_{S}\times_{S}{\rm Spec}R^{\bullet}\simeq\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm chart}_{{\rm Spec}(R^{\bullet},L^{\bullet})}
.
Thus we can assume that the charted log stack
S
is equivalent to a charted log-affine scheme
{\rm Spec}(R,L)
.
Let
f:R\rightarrow A
be an
R
-algebra, and let
I
be a connective
A
-module. Let
x\in\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm chart}_{{\rm Spec}(R,L)}(A)

be a point which represents a charted log structure over
{\rm Spec}A.
The point
x
represents a charted log-affine scheme
{\rm Spec}(A,M)
. We will determine the specific form of the fiber
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm chart}_{{\rm Spec}(R,L)}/R}(x,I)
of the following map

\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm chart}_{{\rm Spec}(R,L)}(A\oplus I)\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm chart}_{{\rm Spec}(R,L)}(A)\times_{{\rm Spec}R(A)}{\rm Spec}R(A\oplus I)

at
(x,\widetilde{f})
lying over
f:R\rightarrow A
.
Let
X
be an object in
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm chart}_{{\rm Spec}(R,L)}/R}(x,I).
It gives rise to a strict morphism of charted log stacks

{\rm Spec}(A,M)\longrightarrow X.

Using
[
38
, Lemma 17.1.3.7]
,
X
is charted log-affine.
Hence the anima
\mathfrak{D}_{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm chart}_{{\rm Spec}(R,L)}/R}(x,I)
is identified with the anima of deformations of
(A,M)
to
A\oplus I
over
(R,L)
. Applying Corollary
3.46
, this space is equivalent to

\displaystyle{\rm fib}({\rm Map}(\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(A,M)/(R,L)},I[1])\longrightarrow{\rm Map}(\mathbb{L}_{A/G},I[1]))

\displaystyle\simeq{\rm Map}(\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(A,M)/(A,L^{a})},I[1]).

Let us write the
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log rings
(A,M)
and
(A,L^{a})
as colimits

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}[M]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{S}[L]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{S}[M],M)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(A,M)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathbb{S}[L],L)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(A,L^{a})}}

Using the left adjointness of Gabber’s cotangent complexes, we obtain that
\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(A,M)/(A,L^{a})}
is naturally equivalent to the colimit of the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(\mathbb{S}[L],L)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{L}_{\mathbb{S}[L]}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{L}_{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(\mathbb{S}[M],M)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{L}_{\mathbb{S}[M]}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{L}_{A}}

where the colimit is formed in the
\infty
-category
\mathfrak{M}^{\rm rep}_{\mathbf{Log}}
, which is equivalent to

A\otimes_{\mathbb{S}[M]}{\rm cofib}(\mathbb{L}_{\mathbb{S}[M]/\mathbb{S}[L]}\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(\mathbb{S}[M],M)/(\mathbb{S}[L],L)}).

Denote by
\mathscr{D}_{M/L}[1]
the cofiber of the morphism
\mathbb{L}_{\mathbb{S}[M]/\mathbb{S}[L]}\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(\mathbb{S}[M],M)/(\mathbb{S}[L],L)}
. we have an equivalence

\mathfrak{D}_{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm chart}_{{\rm Spec}(R,L)}/R}(x,I)\simeq{\rm Map}_{A}(A\otimes_{\mathbb{S}[M]}\mathscr{D}_{M/L},I).

This implies that the object
A\otimes_{\mathbb{S}[M]}\mathscr{D}_{M/L}
corepresents the functor

I\mapsto{\rm fib}_{x}(\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm chart}_{{\rm Spec}(R,L)}(A\oplus I)\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm chart}_{{\rm Spec}(R,L)}(A)).

Note that the collection of all maps
x:{\rm Spec}A\rightarrow\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm chart}_{{\rm Spec}(R,L)}
forms a cover

\coprod_{(A,x)}{\rm Spec}A\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm chart}_{{\rm Spec}(R,L)}

of
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm chart}_{{\rm Spec}(R,L)}
. In particular, we have an equivalence of presentable stable
\infty
-categories

\mathbf{QCoh}(\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm chart}_{{\rm Spec}(R,L)})\simeq\varprojlim_{(A,x)}\mathbf{Mod}_{A}.

Thus, we need to show that those objects
\{A\otimes_{\mathbb{S}[M]}\mathscr{D}_{M/L}\}
form a projective system, i.e., for any ring map
A\rightarrow B
lying over
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm chart}_{{\rm Spec}(R,L)}
, the following canonical map

A\otimes_{\mathbb{S}[M]}\mathscr{D}_{M/L}\otimes_{A}B\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}B\otimes_{\mathbb{S}[M]}\mathscr{D}_{M^{a}/L}

is an equivalence.
This holds because the left hand term is equivalent to
B\otimes_{\mathbb{S}[M]}\mathscr{D}_{M/L}
, which could be described as the
(-1)
-shift of the colimit of the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(\mathbb{S}[L],L)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{L}_{\mathbb{S}[L]}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{L}_{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(\mathbb{S}[M],M)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{L}_{\mathbb{S}[M]}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{L}_{B}}

which is equivalent to the
(-1)
-shift of the cofiber of the map

\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(B,L^{a})}\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(B,M^{a})}
,
which is equivalent to
B\otimes_{\mathbb{S}[M]}\mathscr{D}_{M^{a}/L}
by the construction of
\mathscr{D}_{M^{a}/L}.
The
(-1)
-connectivity of the cotangent complex of
{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{{\rm Spec}(R,L)}}
is immediately deduced from the conormal exact sequence of stacks.
∎

4.2
Spectral log stacks

We have defined the charted log stacks in the previous section. However, there is an issue that the charted log structures do not satisfy any descent property, which can be viewed as a similar phenomenon in classical logarithmic geometry: one can only define charts on a log scheme locally, but there is no way to glue them together to obtain a global chart in general. We have the following example, which shows that the functor
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm chart}_{S}
does not satisfy étale descent.

Example 4.24
.

Let
(A,M)
be an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log ring. We denote by
(A\times A,M^{a})
the log ring associated to the diagonal map
M\rightarrow A\times A.
Then we have a map of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log rings

(A\times A,M^{a})\rightarrow(A\times A,M\times M).
This defines a map of charted log-affine schemes
\pi:{\rm Spec}(A\times A,M\times M)\longrightarrow{\rm Spec}(A\times A,M^{a}),
which is not an equivalence by the definition of charted log stacks.
On the other hand, let us consider the natural embeddings
i_{1},i_{2}:{\rm Spec}A\rightarrow{\rm Spec}(A\times A)
. The pullback of
\pi
along
i_{\alpha},\alpha=1,2
gives rise to a homotopy equivalence
i_{\alpha}^{*}\pi\simeq id:{\rm Spec}(A,M)\rightarrow{\rm Spec}(A,M).
But
\pi
is not an equivalence. This shows that the functor
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{X}^{\rm chart}
doesn’t preserve finite products.

The reason that the descent of the moduli of charted log stacks fails is that the "chart" is not allowed to glue in the following sense: consider a classical charted log scheme
(X,\mathscr{M},P)
, which is equivalent to giving a map
X\rightarrow{\rm Spec}\mathbb{Z}[P]
, such that
\mathscr{M}
is isomorphic to the pullback of the canonical log structure on
{\rm Spec}\mathbb{Z}[P]
. However, if we have a class of strict étale maps of log schemes

\{(U_{i},\mathscr{N}_{i})\rightarrow(X,\mathscr{M})\}^{n}_{i=1}
which we equip each
(U_{i},\mathscr{N}_{i})
with a chart
P_{i}
, then we do not always obtain a charted map
(U_{i},\mathscr{N}_{i},P_{i})\rightarrow(X,\mathscr{M},P)
, because the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm Spec}\mathbb{Z}[P_{i}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm Spec}\mathbb{Z}[P]}}

will not exist.

Definition 4.25
.

Let
(A,M)
be an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-prelog ring. The
spectral log-affine scheme

{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(A,M)
associated with
(A,M)
is a pair
({\rm Sp\acute{e}t}A,\mathscr{M}),
in which:

1.

{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}A
is the étale spectrum of
A
defined in
[
38
, Definition 1.4.2.5]
;

2.

\mathscr{M}:\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}_{A}\rightarrow\mathbf{CMon}
is the étale sheafification of

\mathscr{M}_{0}:\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}_{A}\ni A^{\prime}\mapsto M^{a},

where
M^{a}
is the
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-monoid of the logification of the
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-prelog ring given by
\mathbb{S}[M]\rightarrow A\rightarrow A^{\prime}
.

Remark 4.26
.

By the definition of the sheaf
\mathscr{M}
, it is the sheafification of the functor which sends
A^{\prime}\in A_{\rm\acute{e}t}
to
{\rm colim}(M\leftarrow M\times_{\Omega^{\infty}A}GL_{1}(A)\rightarrow GL_{1}(A))
. Equivalently,
\mathscr{M}
is equivalent to the colimit of the following diagram

\underline{M}\longleftarrow\underline{M}\times_{\Omega^{\infty}\mathcal{O}_{A}}GL_{1}(\mathcal{O}_{A})\longrightarrow GL_{1}(\mathcal{O}_{A}),

where the colimit is formed in
\mathbf{Shv}_{{\rm\acute{e}t}}(\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn,op},\mathbf{CMon})
. In other words, the sheaf
\mathscr{M}
is equivalent to the sheafification of the point-wise logification of the constant prelog structure
\underline{M}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{A}
.

Caution 4.27
.

We have to point out that the functor
\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}_{A}\rightarrow\mathbf{CMon},A^{\prime}\mapsto M^{a}
is not a sheaf, as it does not preserve finite products. Although it has descent along an fpqc hypercover
A\rightarrow A^{\bullet}
by Proposition
4.6
.

Definition 4.28
.

A
spectral prelog Deligne-Mumford stack
is a triple
(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{M},\alpha),
where
\mathcal{X}
is a quasi-compact and quasi-separated spectral Deligne-Mumford stack
10
10

10

See
[
38
, Definition 1.4.4.2]
,
\mathscr{M}
is an étale sheaf of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-monoids over the underlying
\infty
-topos of
\mathcal{X}
, and
\alpha:\mathscr{M}\rightarrow\Omega^{\infty}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
is a morphism of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-monoids.

We denote
\mathbf{PreLog}\mathbf{SpDM}
as the
\infty
-category of spectral log Deligne-Mumford stacks.

Definition 4.29
.

A
spectral log Deligne-Mumford stack

(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{M},\alpha)
is a spectral prelog Deligne-Mumford stack, such that the induced morphism
\mathscr{M}\times_{\Omega^{\infty}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}}GL_{1}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}})\rightarrow GL_{1}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}})
is an equivalence.

We denote
\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{SpDM}
as the
\infty
-category of spectral log Deligne-Mumford stacks.

Caution 4.30
.

The functor
\mathbf{Log}^{\rm op}\rightarrow\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{SpDM},(A,M)\mapsto{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(A,M)
is not fully faithful.

Let us give a concrete definition of the
\infty
-categories
\mathbf{PreLog}\mathbf{SpDM}
and
\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{SpDM}
as follows. We denote by
\mathbf{CMon}(\mathcal{X}):=\mathbf{Shv}(\mathcal{X},\mathbf{CMon})
the
\infty
-category of sheaves of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-monoids over the underlying
\infty
-topos of
\mathcal{X}.
The assignment
\mathcal{X}\mapsto\mathbf{Shv}(\mathcal{X},\mathbf{CMon})
defines a functor
\mathbf{CMon}(-):\mathbf{SpDM}^{\rm op}\rightarrow\mathbf{Cat}_{\infty}
. We can define a functor
\widehat{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{PreLog}}:\mathbf{SpDM}^{\rm op}\rightarrow\mathbf{Cat}_{\infty}
as the limit of the following diagram of functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{CMon})(-)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{CMon}(-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{\infty}\mathcal{O}_{(-)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm ev}_{1}}

Using the Grothendieck construction, we obtain a Cartesian fibration

\pi:\mathbf{PreLog}\mathbf{SpDM}\rightarrow\mathbf{SpDM}.

We refer to
\mathbf{PreLog}\mathbf{SpDM}
as the
\infty
-category of spectral prelog Deligne-Mumford stacks, and
we refer to
\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{SpDM}
as the subcategory of
\mathbf{PreLog}\mathbf{SpDM}
spanned by log objects, which is called the
\infty
-category of spectral log Deligne-Mumford stacks.

Remark 4.31
.

The
\infty
-category
\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{SpDM}
has finite limits, and it’s easy to see that

{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(A,M)\times_{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R,L)}{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(B,N)\simeq{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}((A,M)\otimes_{(R,L)}(B,N))

by definition.

Remark 4.32

(Derived log stacks)
.

Similar to the
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-context, one can also construct a Cartesian fibration
\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{DM}^{\Delta}\rightarrow\mathbf{DM}^{\Delta}
over the
\infty
-category of derived Deligne-Mumford stacks in the same way, where
\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{DM}^{\Delta}
is the
\infty
-category of derived log Deligne-Mumford stacks. A derived log Deligne-Mumford stack is a triple
(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{M},\alpha)
, where
\mathcal{X}
is a derived Deligne-Mumford stack,
\mathscr{M}
is a sheaf of animated monoids on the underlying
\infty
-topos of
\mathcal{X}
, and
\alpha:\mathscr{M}\rightarrow\Omega^{\infty}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
is a map of sheaves of animated monoids, such that the induced map
\mathscr{M}\times_{\Omega^{\infty}\mathcal{O}_{X}}GL_{1}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}})\rightarrow GL_{1}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}})
is an equivalence.

We should point out that all constructions, definitions, and results in this paper for spectral log Deligne-Mumfords also hold for derived log Deligne-Mumford stacks. Especially, the deformations and representability properties hold for the moduli stack of derived log structures. For this reason, we will mainly work on the
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-context, and only collect the main results for derived log Deligne-Mumford stacks in
subsection 6.3
.

4.2.1
Functoriality

We recall some basic functoriality of (pre)-log structures developed in
[
51
, Section 3.4]

[
14
, Section 2]
and
[
44
, Section II.1.2]
. We have the following operations on prelog structures:

1.

(Pullback) Since the functor
\pi:\mathbf{PreLog}\mathbf{SpDM}\rightarrow\mathbf{SpDM}
is a Cartesian fibration, we have pullback functors for prelog structures. We can describe it as follows: let
(\mathcal{Y},\mathscr{M},\alpha)
be a spectral prelog Deligne-Mumford stack, and let
f:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
be a morphism of spectral Deligne-Mumford stacks.
The spectral prelog Deligne-Mumford stack with a structure map

f^{-1}\mathscr{M}\xrightarrow{\alpha}\Omega^{\infty}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}

is a Cartesian edge lying over
f
.

2.

(Pushout) The functor
\pi:\mathbf{PreLog}\mathbf{SpDM}\rightarrow\mathbf{SpDM}
is also a coCartesian fibration. We construct coCartesian edges as follows: let
(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{N},\beta)
be a spectral prelog Deligne-Mumford stack, and let
f\colon\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{Y}
be a morphism of Deligne-Mumford stacks.
The following fiber product of sheaves of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-monoids

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{*}\mathscr{N}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{\infty}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{Y}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\mathbf{Shv}}_{*}\mathscr{N}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\mathbf{Shv}}_{*}\Omega^{\infty}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta}

gives a coCartesian edge lying over
f
,
where
f_{*}^{\mathbf{Shv}}
is the sheaf-theoretic direct image functor.

Remark 4.33
.

It’s easy to see that the pushout functor
f_{*}
preserves log objects.

Thus we have the following fact:

Proposition 4.34
.

[
51
, Proposition 3.26]

The restricted functor
\pi^{\prime}:\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{SpDM}\rightarrow\mathbf{SpDM}
of
\pi
is a presentable fibration.

Proof.

Remark
4.33
implies that
\pi^{\prime}
is a coCartesian fibration. Observe that the pushout functor preserves limits of prelog structures, and log structures are stable under limits. Therefore, we just need to show that
\pi^{\prime}
has presentable fibers. The same arguments as Proposition
2.9
give the desired result.
∎

Definition 4.35
.

Let
(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{M},\alpha)
be a spectral log Deligne-Mumford stack, we say that
(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{M},\alpha)
is:

1.

Quasi-coherent
, if there is an étale cover
U\rightarrow\mathcal{X},
such that
U\simeq\coprod_{j}{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}A_{j}
is a finite disjoint union of spectral affine schemes, and the restriction
(U,\mathscr{M}|_{U})\simeq\coprod_{j}{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(A_{j},M_{j})
is a finite disjoint union of spectral log-affine schemes;

2.

Coherent
, if it is quasi-coherent, and there is a choice of
\{(A_{j},M_{j})\}
, which appears in assertion (1), such that for any
j
,
(A_{j},M_{j})
is obtained from an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-prelog ring
(A_{j},M_{j}^{\prime})
satisfying the property that
\mathbb{S}[M_{j}^{\prime}]
is almost finitely presented over
\mathbb{S}
.

3.

Fine
, if it’s coherent, and there is a choice of
\{(A_{j},M_{j})\}
, which appears in assertion (2), such that for any
j
, the commutative ring
\pi_{0}\mathbb{S}[M_{j}]=\mathbb{Z}[\pi_{0}M_{j}]
is a finitely generated integral
\mathbb{Z}
-algebra.

Definition 4.36
.

Let
f:(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{M})\rightarrow(\mathcal{Y},\mathscr{N})
be a morphism of spectral log Deligne-Mumford stacks. We say that
f
is strict if it is a Cartesian edge with respect to
\underline{f}:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
. Or equivalently, the log structure
\mathscr{M}
is equivalent to the pullback of
\mathscr{N}
along
\underline{f}
.

Remark 4.37
.

The log structure on
{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(A,M)
is equivalent to the pullback of the log structure on
{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(\mathbb{S}[M],M)
along
{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}A\rightarrow{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}\mathbb{S}[M]
.

Remark 4.38

(Local charts for strict maps)
.

Let
f:{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(A,M)\rightarrow{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R,L)
be a strict map. We denote by
\mathscr{L}
and
\mathscr{M}\simeq f^{*}\mathscr{L}
the log structures of
{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R,L)
and
{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(A,M)
respectively. Then there is an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log ring
(A,M^{\prime})
such that
{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(A,M)\simeq{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(A,M^{\prime})
, and the map
f
is induced from an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log ring map
(R,L)\rightarrow(A,M^{\prime})
. Indeed, we can form the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(A,M)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R,L)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(\mathbb{S}[L],L)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

The composition
{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(A,M)\rightarrow{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(\mathbb{S}[L],L)
is strict, as the strictness is stable under compositions. It turns out that
\mathscr{M}
is equivalent to the pullback of the log structure of
{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(\mathbb{S}[L],L)
. It is easy to see that
{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(A,M)\simeq{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(A,L)^{a}

f
is induced from
(R,L)\rightarrow(A,L)^{a}
. We have to point out that there is no reason wonder that there is a map
(R,L)\rightarrow(A,M)
induces
f
in general.

Caution 4.39

(Non-existence of local charts for general maps)
.

Let us work in classical logarithmic geometry. Recall that a map of quasi-coherent log schemes
f:(X,\mathscr{M})\rightarrow(S,\mathscr{L})
locally admits a chart. If after an étale localization on both source and target, there is a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(X,\mathscr{M})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(Y,\mathscr{N})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(\mathbb{Z}[M],M)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(\mathbb{Z}[L],L)}}

such that vertical maps are strict. The existence of local charts will not hold in general. But under some finiteness assumption on the target, one can show that such maps locally admit charts, see
[
7
, Section 1.1]
.

At the end of this subsection, we consider the topology used to define log structures. Recall that a log structure on a spectral Deligne-Mumford stack
\mathcal{X}
is a sheaf of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-monoids defined on the underlying
\infty
-topos of
\mathcal{X}
, namely, the big étale
\infty
-topos
\mathcal{X}_{\rm\acute{E}t}
. In the vast majority of cases in geometry and arithmetic, when logarithmic geometry appears, this definition is perfectly applicable. For example, if we consider log structures coming from strict normal crossing divisors on algebraic varieties over fields or semi-stable proper schemes defined over DVRs. One can show that this kind of log structure also has fpqc (hyper)descent in the following sense: to give such a kind of log structure
\mathscr{M}
on a scheme
X
, it is equivalent to give a map

X\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}\simeq{\rm Div}_{\mathbb{Z}}
. More generally, it seems that log structures on (spectral) schemes given by
Deligne-Faltings structures
are also suitable for work on the fpqc topology instead of the étale topology, see
[
54
, Section 2]
for details. We have the following variants of the definition of log structures:

1.

In the definition of
{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(A,M)
, we define the log structure
\mathscr{M}
as the (hyper)sheafification with respect to the fpqc topology instead of the étale topology.

2.

Let
\mathbf{SpDM}_{\rm fpqc}
be the subcategory of
\mathbf{SpDM}
spanned by objects
\mathcal{X}
having fpqc (hyper)descent
11
11

11

This means the associated Yoneda functor
h_{\mathcal{X}}:\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}\rightarrow\mathbf{Ani}
has fpqc (hyper)descent.
. The
\infty
-category
\mathbf{PreLog}\mathbf{SpDM}_{\rm fpqc}
is the Grothendieck construction associated with the functor

\mathbf{SpDM}^{\rm op}_{\rm fpqc}\rightarrow\mathbf{Cat}_{\infty}
given as

\lim(*\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Omega^{\infty}\mathcal{O}_{(-)}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbf{CMon}^{(\wedge)}(-)\longleftarrow{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{CMon})^{(\wedge)}(-)).

The
\infty
-category
\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{SpDM}_{\rm fpqc}
is the subcategory of
\mathbf{PreLog}\mathbf{SpDM}_{\rm fpqc}
spanned by log objects.

3.

In the definitions of quasi-coherent, coherent, and fine log structures, we also use fpqc covers instead of étale covers.

Remark 4.40
.

By the same procedure, one can also construct the
\infty
-category of spectral log Delinge-Mumford stacks using other topologies: Zariski, Nisnevich, fppf, fpqc… Each topology has its own advantages. In particular, as we will work on
\infty
-stacks in
section 7
, which is defined as an inverse limit of a sequence of certain functors. It seems that using fpqc topology is more convenient than others, as a sequential limit of fpqc covers is also an fpqc cover.

Convention 4.41
.

For the sake of simplicity, we omit set-theoretic issues. Under mild assumptions on set theory, we always assume that any presheaf has an fpqc sheafification, and that the
\infty
-category
\mathbf{Stk}_{\rm fpqc}
of fpqc stacks is presentable.

4.3
The moduli stack of spectral log stacks

In this subsection, we provide a construction of the moduli stack of log structures. Then we study certain properties of this functor, including the descent property and deformation properties in the sense of
[
38
, Part VIII]
.
Let
(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})
be a spectral log Deligne-Mumford stack. The Cartesian fibration
\pi^{\prime}:\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{SpDM}\rightarrow\mathbf{SpDM}
induces a Cartesian fibration

\pi^{\prime}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}:\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{SpDM}_{/(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}\longrightarrow\mathbf{SpDM}_{/\mathcal{S}}.

This gives rise to a moduli functor of spectral log structures:

Definition 4.42
.

Let
(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})
be a spectral log Deligne-Mumford stack. We define the following functor

\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}:\mathbf{SpDM}^{\rm op}_{/\mathcal{S}}\longrightarrow\mathbf{Ani},

which sends a quasi-compact and quasi-separated spectral Deligne-Mumford stack
\mathcal{X}
to the anima consisting of maps of spectral log Deligne-Mumford stacks
(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{M})\rightarrow(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L}).
More precisely,
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}
is the functor associated with
\pi^{\prime}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}
via the Grothendieck construction.

We denote by
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}^{\rm qcoh}
,
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}^{\rm coh}
and
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}^{\rm fin}
the subfunctors of
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}
spanned by quasi-coherent, coherent, or fine log structures, respectively.

4.3.1
Descent property of log structures

In this subsection, we will prove the descendability of the moduli of log structures.

Convention 4.43
.

In this section, we should fix a topology for defining our spectral log Deligne-Mumford stacks. By the proof of Theorem
4.44
, any choice of one of the following topologies:

Zariski, étale, fppf, fpqc…

and any choice of one of the following descendability:

descent, hyperdescent

for using to define spectral log Deligne-Mumford stacks will give the parallel result of the descent property. More precisely, if we use the topology
\tau
, and (hyper-)descendability to define our log Deligne-Mumford stacks, the resulting moduli functor has (hyper-)descent with respect to
\tau
. For simplicity, in this section, we will employ
étale topology
and
sheaves
to define our log stacks.

Theorem 4.44
.

Let
\mu\in\{\emptyset,{\rm qcoh,coh,fin}\}
, and let
(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})
be a
\mu
-spectral Deligne-Mumford stack. Then
the following functors

\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})},\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm qcoh}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})},{\ }\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm coh}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})},{\ }\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm fin}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}

are étale sheaves.

As a corollary, we obtain the following result that will contribute to the local study of log stacks.

Corollary 4.45
.

Let
\mu\in\{\rm qcoh,coh,fin\}
. Let
(R,L)
be an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log ring, and let

\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R,L)}^{\rm aff,\mu}:\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}_{R}\longrightarrow\mathbf{Ani}

be the subfunctor of
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\mu}_{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R,L)}
consisting of all log-affine structures. The inclusion
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R,L)}^{\rm aff,\mu}\subset\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\mu}_{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R,L)}
exhibits the latter as the sheafification of
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R,L)}^{\rm aff,\mu}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{F}:\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}_{R}\rightarrow\mathbf{Ani}
be a sheaf. We aim to prove that the following natural map

i^{*}:{\rm Map}_{\mathbf{Shv}}(\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R,L)}^{\mu},\mathcal{F})\longrightarrow{\rm Map}_{\rm pre\mathbf{Shv}}(\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R,L)}^{\rm aff,\mu},\mathcal{F})

induced by the inclusion
i:\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R,L)}^{\rm aff,\mu}\subset\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\mu}_{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R,L)}
is an equivalence.
We prove that the map
i^{*}
is essentially surjective and
(-1)
-truncated, i.e., it has contractible or empty homotopy fibers.

Obviously, the map
i^{*}
is
(-1)
-truncated, because the functor
i
is point-wise
(-1)
-truncated. We now prove that
i^{*}
is essentially surjective.
Let
f:\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R,L)}^{\rm aff,\mu}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}
be a morphism. We have to construct an extension
\tilde{f}:\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R,L)}^{\mu}\rightarrow\mathcal{F}
, making the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R,L)}^{\rm aff,\mu}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{F}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R,L)}^{\mu}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{f}}

commutative. Let
A\in\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}_{R}
be a connective
R
-algebra, and let
\mathscr{M}
be a
\mu
-log structure on
X={\rm Sp\acute{e}t}A
. Then we know that there is a faithfully flat étale
A
-algebra
A^{\prime}
, such that the log structure
\mathscr{M}^{\prime}:=\mathscr{M}|_{A^{\prime}}

on
{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}A^{\prime}
obtained from pulling back the log structure
\mathscr{M}
along the covering map
{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}A^{\prime}\rightarrow{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}A
is log-affine. By Theorem
4.44
, one can write
({\rm Sp\acute{e}t}A,\mathscr{M})
as a colimit

({\rm Sp\acute{e}t}A,\mathscr{M})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longleftarrow}}{{\rm colim}}_{\Delta^{\rm op}}({\rm Sp\acute{e}t}A^{\prime\otimes_{R}(\bullet+1)},\mathscr{M}|_{A^{\prime\otimes_{R}(\bullet+1)}}).

Then we define the functor
\tilde{f}
as the left Kan extension of
f
along the inclusion
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R,L)}^{\rm aff,\mu}\subset\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\mu}_{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R,L)}.

Therefore, we conclude that the map
i^{*}
is essentially surjective.
∎

Remark 4.46
.

The Corollary
4.45
implies that
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R,L)}^{\mu}
admits a surjective map from the union of all spectral affine schemes having the form of
{\rm Spec}R[M]
, where
M
runs over all
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-monoids with the desired property.

Now, we are ready to prove the descent theorem for moduli of log structures. In fact, it is enough to show the statement of Theorem
4.44
for the stack of all log structures, in light of the following Lemma.

Lemma 4.47
.

If
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}
is an étale sheaf, then so are
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm qcoh}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})},\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm coh}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}
and
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm fin}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}
.

Proof.

Assume that
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}
satisfies étale descent. We will show that the functors
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm qcoh}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})},\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm coh}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}
and
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm fin}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}
also satisfy étale descent.
Let
f:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
be an étale cover of spectral Deligne-Mumford stacks. Let
\mu\in\{\rm qcoh,coh,fin\}
. Consider the following commutative diagrams

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}^{\mu}(\mathcal{Y})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}^{\mu}(\mathcal{X})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}(\mathcal{Y})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}(\mathcal{X})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}

The fiber product

\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}^{\mu}(\mathcal{X})\times_{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}(\mathcal{X})}\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}(\mathcal{Y})

is the anima consisting of triples
(\mathscr{M},\mathscr{N},\rho)
, where
\mathscr{M}\in\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}^{\mu}(\mathcal{X})
is a
\mu
-log structure over
\mathcal{X}
,
\mathscr{N}\in\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}(\mathcal{Y})
is a log structure over
Y
, and
\rho:f^{*}\mathscr{N}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathscr{M}
is an equivalence of log structures. This implies that
\mathscr{N}\in\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}(\mathcal{Y})
is actually a
\mu
-log structure. since a log structure has the property
\mu
, it is, by definition, a local question. We have that the canonical map

\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}^{\mu}(\mathcal{Y})\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}^{\mu}(\mathcal{X})\times_{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}(\mathcal{X})}\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}(\mathcal{Y})

has a cosection
\pi
, given by the projection onto the second variable. It’s obvious that
\pi
has contractible fibers. We deduce that the projection
\pi
is an equivalence. Let
\mathcal{Y}_{\bullet}
be an arbitrary Čech cover of
\mathcal{Y}
; we obtain an equivalence of cosimplicial animae

\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}^{\mu}(\mathcal{Y})\simeq\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}^{\mu}(\mathcal{Y}_{\bullet})\times_{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}(\mathcal{Y}_{\bullet})}\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}(\mathcal{Y}),

Passing to the limit, we acquire the following equivalences

\displaystyle\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}^{\mu}(\mathcal{Y})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\lim}_{\Delta}\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}^{\mu}(\mathcal{Y}_{\bullet})\times_{{\lim}_{\Delta}\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}(\mathcal{Y}_{\bullet})}\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}(\mathcal{Y})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\lim}_{\Delta}\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}^{\mu}(\mathcal{Y}_{\bullet})\times_{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}(\mathcal{Y})}\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}(\mathcal{Y})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}{\lim}_{\Delta}\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}^{\mu}(\mathcal{Y}_{\bullet}).

∎

For this reason, we will focus on the moduli functor of arbitrary log structures.
Let
(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})
be a spectral log Deligne-Mumford stack, we want to define two new functors

\widehat{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}},\widehat{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{PreLog}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}}:\mathbf{SpDM}^{\rm op}_{/\mathcal{S}}\longrightarrow\mathbf{Cat}_{\infty},

which sends a relative spectral Deligne-Mumford stack
\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{S}
to the
\infty
-category of maps to spectral log (or prelog) Deligne-Mumford stacks
(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{M})\rightarrow(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L}).
We thus have enough to show the descendability of
\widehat{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}}
and
\widehat{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{PreLog}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}}
.
[
5
, Corollary 2.3.8]
shows that the functor

\mathbf{CMon}(-):\mathbf{SpDM}_{/\mathcal{S}}^{\rm op}\longrightarrow\mathbf{Cat}_{\infty}

satisfies étale descent.
We let the functor
\widehat{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{PreLog}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}}
be the limit of the diagram of functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{2},\mathbf{CMon})(-)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{CMon}(-)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{CMon}(-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm ev}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm ev}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Omega^{\infty}\mathcal{O}(-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f\mapsto f^{*}\mathscr{L}}

and let
\widehat{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}}
be the subfunctor of
\widehat{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{PreLog}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}}
spanned by log objects. We immediately obtain the following fact:

Lemma 4.48
.

The functor
\widehat{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{PreLog}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}}
satisfies étale descent.

Now we can prove Theorem
4.44
.

Proof of Theorem
4.44
.

Let
f:\mathcal{X}\rightarrow\mathcal{Y}
be an étale cover of a spectral Deligne-Mumford stack. We will show that the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widehat{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}}(\mathcal{Y})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widehat{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}}(\mathcal{X})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widehat{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{PreLog}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}}(\mathcal{Y})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widehat{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{PreLog}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}}(\mathcal{X})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{*}}

is a pullback square. This is clear because a prelog structure
\mathscr{M}
over
\mathcal{Y}
is a log structure if and only if its restriction to
\mathcal{X}
is a log structure. Then applying the same argument in Lemma
4.47
, we obtain that the functor

\widehat{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}}
satisfies étale descent, then so does
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}
, as
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}
is the core of
\widehat{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}}
. Applying Lemma
4.47
, we know that
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\mu}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})},\mu\in\{\rm qcoh,coh,fin\}
also satisfies étale hyperdescent.
∎

Remark 4.49

(Log structures in fpqc topology)
.

We can define another variation of the functor
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}
using fpqc topology. In this case, we can regard as a functor

\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\mu}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}:\mathbf{SpDM}_{\rm fpqc}\rightarrow\mathbf{Ani}
. The same method of the proof Theorem
4.44
shows this is an fpqc sheaf.

4.3.2
Comparison with the charted log structures and logarithmic cotangent complexes

Let
S
be a charted log stack whose underlying stack
\underline{S}
is represented by a spectral Deligne-Mumford stack
\mathcal{S}.
Denote by
\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{SpDM}^{\rm chart}
the subcategory of
\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{Stk}^{\rm chart}
that consists of charted log stacks whose underlying stacks are representable by spectral Deligne-Mumford stacks. We define a functor

|-|:\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{SpDM}^{\rm chart}\longrightarrow\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{SpDM}

given by the left Kan extension of the functor
{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(-):\mathbf{Log}^{\rm op}\rightarrow\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{SpDM}.
We have the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Log}^{\rm op}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{SpDM}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{SpDM}^{\rm chart}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Spec}(-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces|-|}

Thus, we get a morphism of presheaves

|-|:\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm chart}_{S}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm qcoh}_{|S|}.

Proposition 4.50
.

The morphism
|-|
exhibits
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm qcoh}_{|S|}
as a sheafification of
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm chart}_{S}
.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we can assume that
S={\rm Spec}(R,L)
is charted log affine.
Let
\mathcal{C}
be the subcategory of
\mathbf{Aff}_{/\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm chart}_{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R,L)}}
forms of maps
{\rm Spec}R[M]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm chart}_{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R,L)}
classifying log structures
M\rightarrow\Omega^{\infty}R[M]
.
Using Remark
4.46
, the disjoint union of objects in
\mathcal{C}
forms a cover of
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm qcoh}_{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R,L)}
. This proves that
|-|
is an effective epimorphism after sheafification. Note that
\mathcal{C}
in
\mathbf{Aff}_{/\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm chart}_{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R,L)}}
and its essential image
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
in
\mathbf{Aff}_{/\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm qcoh}_{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R,L)}}
are both cofinal. It follows that the sheafification of
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm qcoh}_{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R,L)}
is equivalent to
{\rm colim}_{\mathcal{C}}{\rm Spec}R[M]\simeq\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm qcoh}_{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R,L)}.

∎

Remark 4.51
.

If we work on the fpqc topology, then the same method shows that the fpqc sheafifiaction of
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{S}^{\rm chart}
also gives the moduli stack of quasi-coherent log structures on
|S|
defined by fpqc topology.

Lemma 4.52
.

Let
(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{M})
be a quasi-coherent spectral log Deligne-Mumford stack. Assume that there is a strict morphism
f:{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(A,M)\rightarrow(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{M})
, such that the underlying map of stacks
\underline{f}:{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}A\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
is a square-zero extension. Then there is a unique (up to homotopy) square-zero extension
i:(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})\rightarrow(A,M)
of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log rings, such that
{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})\simeq(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{M})
, and
f
is induced from
i
.

Proof.

[
38
, Lemma 17.1.3.7]
shows that the underlying stack
\mathcal{X}
is affine, and we denote it by
{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}A^{\prime}
, where
A^{\prime}
is a square-zero extension of
A
with kernel
I\in\mathbf{Mod}_{A}
. Let us identify the underlying
\infty
-topoi of
{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}A
and
{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}A^{\prime}
, and restrict
\mathscr{M}
to the small étale site of
{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}A
, we therefore regard
\mathscr{M}
as a sheaf of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-monoids on
A_{\rm\acute{e}t}
. Denote by
\mathscr{M}_{0}
the pullback of
\mathscr{M}
to
{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}A^{\prime}
, which is equivalent to the log structure of
{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(A,M)
by assumption. As sheaves on
{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}A
, we have a fiber sequence

\mathscr{M}\longrightarrow\mathscr{M}_{0}\longrightarrow B\mathcal{I}\simeq\mathcal{I}[1]

, where
\mathcal{I}
is the quasi-coherent sheaf associated with
I
.

As
\mathscr{M}_{0}
is given by the sheafification of the functor
A_{\rm\acute{e}t}\rightarrow\mathbf{CMon},A^{\prime}\mapsto M^{a}
, we can form a commutative diagram of presheaves on
A_{\rm\acute{e}t}
as follows:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{M^{\prime}}^{a}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M^{a}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B\mathcal{I}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{M}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B\mathcal{I}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

where
(-)^{a}
is the point-wise logification,
M^{a}\rightarrow B\mathcal{I}
is induced from the composition

\phi:M\longrightarrow\Gamma({\rm Sp\acute{e}t}A,\mathscr{M}_{0})\longrightarrow\Gamma({\rm Sp\acute{e}t}A,B\mathcal{I})\simeq BI,

and
M^{\prime}
is its fiber. After taking the sheafification, the middle arrow becomes an equivalence, and so does the left vertical map. It follows that
(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{M})
is equivalent to
{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})
, where
(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})
is clearly a square-zero extension of
(A,M)
.

We now consider the uniqueness. If there is another square-zero extension
j:(A^{\prime},M^{\prime\prime})\rightarrow(A,M)
, such that
(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{M})
is equivalent to
{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(A^{\prime},M^{\prime\prime})
, and
f
is induced from
j
. Then, by virtue of the same argument as above, we can show that the induced map
{M^{\prime\prime}}\rightarrow M
can be given as the fiber of the map
\phi
, and it turns out that
M^{\prime}
is canonically equivalent to
M^{\prime\prime}
. We conclude that
(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})
is unique up to homotopy.
∎

Remark 4.53
.

The proof of Lemma
4.52
also holds when we work in the fpqc topology,
as we observe that the sequence
\mathscr{M}\rightarrow\mathscr{M}_{0}\rightarrow B\mathcal{I}
is also a fiber sequence (as fpqc sheaves restricted to the small étale site of
{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}A
).

Remark 4.54
.

One can check that the proof of Lemma
4.52
also holds for the following case:
A
is
n
-truncated, and
{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}A\rightarrow\mathcal{X}\simeq{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}A^{\prime}
is the embedding induced from the
n
-truncation map.

Using Lemma
4.52
, one can easily deduce the following result:

Corollary 4.55
.

Let
S
be a charted spectral log Deligne-Mumford stack. Let
A^{\prime}\rightarrow A
be a square-zero extension with kernel
I\in\mathbf{Mod}_{A}^{\rm cn}
. Then the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{S}^{\rm chart}(A^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{S}^{\rm qcoh}(A^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{S}^{\rm chart}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{S}^{\rm qcoh}(A)}

is a pullback square.

Combining Theorem
4.22
with Corollary
4.55
, we can conclude the existence of the cotangent complex on the moduli of log structures.

Theorem 4.56
.

Let
\mu\in\{\rm qcoh,coh,fin\},
and let
(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})
be a
\mu
-spectral log Deligne-Mumford stack. The functor
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\mu}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}
admits a
(-1)
-connective cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\mu}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}/\mathcal{S}}
. If
\mu=\rm coh
or
\rm fin
, then the cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\mu}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}/\mathcal{S}}
is almost perfect.

Proof.

As the existence of the cotangent complex of a relative stack
F/G
is local on the target, we replace
(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})
with a strict étale log-affine cover
{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R,L)
. On the other hand, it’s easy to see that if a log structure
\mathscr{M}
over
\mathcal{X}
has property
\mu
, then so does
\mathscr{M}^{\prime}
of a square-zero extension by Lemma
4.52
.
From this point of view, we will let
\mu={\rm qcoh}
. Using the following Lemma
4.57
, together with Lemma
4.52
, we obtain the desired result.
∎

Lemma 4.57
.

Let
X:\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}\rightarrow\mathbf{Ani}
be a presheaf, assume that
X
admits a cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{X}
. If its étale (or fpqc) (hyper)sheafification
a:X\rightarrow X^{\prime}

satisfies the following condition: for any
s\in X^{\prime}(A)
who belongs to the essential image of
a
, the fiber of the natural map

\eta:X^{\prime}(A\oplus I)\rightarrow X^{\prime}(A)
at
s
is equivalent to the fiber of
X(A\oplus I)\rightarrow X(A)
at
s
via the functor
a
.

Then
X^{\prime}
also admits a cotangent complex.
In particular, one has
a^{*}\mathbb{L}_{X^{\prime}/R}\simeq\mathbb{L}_{X}
.

Proof.

First, the pullback along
a
gives rise to an equivalence
a^{*}:\mathbf{QCoh}(X^{\prime})\rightarrow\mathbf{QCoh}(X)
by
[
38
, Proposition 6.2.3.1]
. Let
\mathcal{L}
be the quasi-coherent complex over
X^{\prime}
given by
(a^{*})^{-1}\mathbb{L}_{X}
, here
(a^{*})^{-1}
is a quasi-inverse of
a^{*}
.
We have to show that
\mathcal{L}
is indeed a cotangent complex of
X^{\prime}
. Let
t\in X^{\prime}(A)
be an arbitrary section. Since
a
is a (hyper)sheafification morphism, there is an étale (resp. flat) hypercover
A^{\bullet}
of
A
, such that the restriction
t^{\bullet}\in X^{\prime}(A^{\bullet})
belongs to the essential image of
a
. It follows that the fiber of
\eta
at
t
is equivalent to the limit of the following mapping spaces

\lim_{\Delta}{\rm Map}_{A^{\bullet}}((t^{\bullet})^{*}\mathcal{L},I\otimes_{A}A^{\bullet})\simeq\lim_{\Delta}{\rm Map}_{A}(t^{*}\mathcal{L},I\otimes_{A}A^{\bullet})\simeq{\rm Map}_{A}(t^{*}\mathcal{L},I),

where the equivalences above are deduced from the quasi-coherence of
\mathcal{L}
and the flat hyperdescent of quasi-coherent complexes. This means that
\mathcal{L}
is a cotangent complex of
X^{\prime}
.
∎

Definition 4.58
.

Let
f:(\mathcal{X},\mathfrak{M})\rightarrow(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})
be a map of quasi-coherent spectral log Deligne-Mumford stacks, the log cotangent complex of
f
is the relative cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}^{\rm Log}_{(\mathcal{X},\mathfrak{M})/(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}:=\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm qcoh}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}}.

Remark 4.59
.

By the proof of Theorem
4.22
. If the map
f:{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(A,M)\rightarrow{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R,L)
is induced from an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log ring map
(R,L)\rightarrow(A,M)
. Then we have a canonical equivalence
\mathbb{L}^{\rm Log}_{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(A,M)/{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R,L)}\simeq\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(A,M)/(R,L)}.

Using the conormal sequence for Gabber’s cotangent complex and reducing to the local arguments, we prove the following results:

Theorem 4.60
.

Let
(\mathcal{X},\mathfrak{M})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\rightarrow}}(\mathcal{Y},\mathscr{N})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle g}}{{\rightarrow}}(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})
be a sequence of quasi-coherent spectral log Deligne-Mumford stacks, then there is a fiber sequence of quasi-coherent sheaves over
\mathcal{X}
:

f^{*}\mathbb{L}^{\rm Log}_{(\mathcal{Y},\mathscr{N})/(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}^{\rm Log}_{(\mathcal{X},\mathfrak{M})/(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}^{\rm Log}_{(\mathcal{X},\mathfrak{M})/(\mathcal{Y},\mathscr{N})}.

Remark 4.61
.

The proof of Theorem
4.56
shows that the moduli stack of quasi-coherent log structures defined using fpqc also has a cotangent complex, which coincides with the cotangent complex on
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}^{\rm qcoh}
.

4.3.3
Infinitesimal properties and finiteness

Proposition 4.62

(Infinitesimally Cohesive)
.

Let
\mu\in\{\rm qcoh,coh,fin\}
, and let
(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})
be a
\mu
-spectral log Deligne-Mumford stack. Then, the relative functor
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\mu}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}
is relatively infinitesimally cohesive.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we assume that
(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})\simeq{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R,L)
is log affine. Thanks to
[
38
, Proposition 17.3.6.1]
, we only need to prove that for any
R
-algebra
A
, any connective
A
-module I, and any
R
-linear derivation
d:A\rightarrow A\oplus I[1]
, the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R,L)}^{\mu}(A^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R,L)}^{\mu}(A)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R,L)}^{\mu}(A)}}
