{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R,L)}^{\mu}(A\oplus I[1])}}

is a pullback square, where
A^{\prime}
is the square-zero extension associated with
d
. Note that we can assume that
\mu=
qcoh.
We then use Corollary
4.55
. Therefore, we only need to show the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{{\rm Spec}(R,L)}^{\rm chart}(A^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{{\rm Spec}(R,L)}^{\rm chart}(A)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{{\rm Spec}(R,L)}^{\rm chart}(A)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{{\rm Spec}(R,L)}^{\rm chart}(A\oplus I[1])}}

is a pullback square. In other words, we should show that the following natural map

v:{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{{\rm Spec}(R,L)}^{\rm chart}(A^{\prime})}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{{\rm Spec}(R,L)}^{\rm chart}(A)}\times_{{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{{\rm Spec}(R,L)}^{\rm chart}(A\oplus I[1])}}{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{{\rm Spec}(R,L)}^{\rm chart}(A^{\prime})}

is an equivalence. The right hand term is the anima consisting of triples
((A,M),(A,M^{\prime}),\tau)
, where
\tau:d^{*}(A,M)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\rightarrow}}d^{*}_{\rm triv}(A,M^{\prime})
, and where we let
d_{\rm triv}
be the trivial derivation
A\rightarrow A\oplus I[1]
.
We can construct an inverse
u
of
v
, which sends a triple
((A,M),(A,M^{\prime}),\tau)
to the fiber product
(A,M)\times_{d^{*}(A,M)}(A,M^{\prime})
. One can check that
u\circ v\simeq{\rm id}
and
v\circ u\simeq{\rm id}
.
∎

Remark 4.63
.

The moduli stack of quasi-coherent log structures defined using fpqc topology also has the infinitesimally cohesive property.

Proposition 4.64

(Nil-completeness and integrability)
.

Let
\mu\in\{\rm qcoh,coh,fin\}
, and let
(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})
be a
\mu
-spectral log Deligne-Mumford stack, which is equivalent to
|S|
for some charted spectral log stack
S
. Fix a sequence of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings

...\longrightarrow A_{n}\longrightarrow A_{n-1}\longrightarrow...\longrightarrow A_{0}

lying over
\mathcal{S}
, with limit
A
.
Assume that one of the following conditions holds:

1.

A_{n}\simeq\tau_{\leq n}A
, and the transition maps
A_{n}\rightarrow A_{n-1}
are truncation maps;

2.

A
is a discrete complete Noetherian local ring, and the transition maps
A_{n}\simeq A/\mathfrak{m}^{n}\rightarrow A_{n-1}\simeq A/\mathfrak{m}^{n-1}
are the maps given in the obvious way, where
\mathfrak{m}\subset A
is the maximal ideal of
A
.

Then the following canonical map of animae

\varphi:\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\mu}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}({\rm Sp\acute{e}t}A)\longrightarrow{\varprojlim}_{n}\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\mu}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}({\rm Sp\acute{e}t}A_{n})

is an equivalence.

Lemma 4.65
.

Fix a charted log stack
S
with an underlying spectral Deligne-Mumford stack. We have the following facts:

1.

The functor
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm chart}_{S}:\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}_{/\underline{S}}\rightarrow\mathbf{Ani}
is nil-complete and infinitesimally cohesive.

2.

Let
A
be a discrete local
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring that is complete with respect to a finitely generated ideal
\mathfrak{a}\subset A
, then one has
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm chart}_{S}(A)\simeq\varprojlim\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm chart}_{S}(A/\mathfrak{a}^{n})
.

Proof.

For assertion
(1)
, we first consider the natural map
\phi:\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm chart}_{S}(A)\rightarrow\varprojlim\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm chart}_{S}(\tau_{\leq n}A)
. This is an equivalence, as we can construct an inverse
\phi^{\prime}
of
\phi
, which sends a projective system
\{(\tau_{\leq nA},M_{n})\}^{\infty}_{n=0}
to
\varprojlim(\tau_{\leq n}A,M_{n})
. Now, we let
\{(\tau_{\leq nA},M_{n})\}^{\infty}_{n=0}
be the image of
(A,M)
under
\phi
. As pointed out in Remark
3.37
,
M_{n+1}\rightarrow M_{n}
is a
\pi_{n+1}A[n+1]
-torsor, and we have a fiber sequence

M_{n+1}\longrightarrow M_{n}\longrightarrow\pi_{n+1}A[n+2].

By induction, we can regard
M\rightarrow\lim_{n}M_{n}

as a map of torsors over
\varprojlim_{m}(\tau_{\leq m}\tau_{\geq n+1}A)[1]\simeq(\tau_{\geq n+1}A)[1]
, which is obviously an equivalence. This shows that
\phi^{\prime}\circ\phi\simeq{\rm id}
. The same argument also gives
\phi\circ\phi^{\prime}\simeq{\rm id}
. This shows that
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm chart}_{S}
is nil-complete. As
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm chart}_{S}
is nil-complete and admits a cotangent complex. The differential criterion of infinitesimal cohesivity
[
38
, Proposition 17.3.6.1]
implies that we only need to check the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{S}(A^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{S}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{S}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{S}(A\oplus I[1])}}

is a pullback square, where
A^{\prime}\rightarrow A
is the square-zero extension of
A
induced by a derivation
A\rightarrow A\oplus I[1]
. This can be deduced from a similar argument as stated above.

For the assertion
(2)
. We also want to show that the natural map

\nu:\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm chart}_{S}(A)\rightarrow\varprojlim\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm chart}_{S}(A/\mathfrak{a}^{n})
admits an inverse
\mu:\{(A/\mathfrak{a}^{n},M_{n})\}^{\infty}_{n=0}\mapsto\varprojlim(A/\mathfrak{a}^{n},M_{n})
.
As
A
is a local ring, then
A/\mathfrak{a}^{n}
is also a local ring for all
n\geq 0
. An element
x
in
A
is invertible if and only if it is not contained in its maximal ideal. This is equivalent to saying that its image
x_{n}\in A/\mathfrak{a}^{n}
. So the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces GL_{1}(A/\mathfrak{a}^{n+k})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces GL_{1}(A/\mathfrak{a}^{n})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A/\mathfrak{a}^{n+k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A/\mathfrak{a}^{n}}

is a pullback square for any
0\leq k\leq\infty
. It follows that the map
M_{n+k}\rightarrow M_{n}
exhibits
M_{n+k}
as a
{\rm fib}(GL_{1}(A/\mathfrak{a}^{n+k})\rightarrow GL_{1}(A/\mathfrak{a}^{n}))
-torsor. Passing to the limit, we see that
M:=\varprojlim_{k}M_{n+k}\rightarrow M_{n}
is a
{\rm fib(GL_{1}(A)\rightarrow GL_{1}(A/\mathfrak{a}^{n}))}
-torsor, and the natural map
M\rightarrow\varprojlim M_{n}
is an equivalence of torsors over
M_{n}
. This holds because the filtered limits commute with the classifying space functor when restricted to discrete groups. The similar argument as the proof of the nil-completeness part of assertion (1) demonstrates that
\mu
is an inverse of
\nu
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
4.64
.

As the natural map
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm chart}_{S}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm qcoh}_{|S|}
is a sheafification map. We will abbreviate the functor
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm chart}_{S}
to
\mathscr{F}
for short.
We have a canonical equivalence

\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm qcoh}_{|S|}(A)\simeq\varinjlim_{C^{0}_{A}\in\mathcal{U}_{A}}\lim_{B\in C^{0}_{A}}\mathscr{F}(B),

where
B
runs over the covering sieve
C^{0}_{A}
, and the colimit runs over the filtered
\infty
-category
\mathcal{U}_{A}
of all covering sieves of
A
. The map
\varphi
can be reformulated into the following form

\gamma:\varinjlim_{C^{0}_{A}\in\mathcal{U}_{A}}\lim_{B\in C^{0}_{A}}\mathscr{F}(B)\longrightarrow\varprojlim_{n}\varinjlim_{C^{0}_{A_{n}}\in\mathcal{U}_{A_{n}}}\lim_{B_{n}\in C^{0}_{A_{n}}}\mathscr{F}(B_{n}).

For case
(1)
, we have
A_{\rm\acute{e}t}\simeq(A_{n})_{\rm\acute{e}t}
. We can first replace
C^{0}_{A_{n}}
by its subcategory
C_{A}^{{\rm\acute{e}t}}
spanned by étale objects, and then we identify
C^{\rm\acute{e}t}_{A}
with
C^{\rm\acute{e}t}_{A_{n}}
, and identify
\mathcal{U}_{A}
with
\mathcal{U}_{A_{n}}
. We can rewrite
\gamma
as follows:

\gamma^{(1)}:\varinjlim_{C^{0}_{A}\in\mathcal{U}_{A}}\lim_{B\in C^{\rm\acute{e}t}_{A}}\mathscr{F}(B)\longrightarrow\varprojlim_{n}\varinjlim_{C^{0}_{A}\in\mathcal{U}_{A}}\lim_{B\in C^{\rm\acute{e}t}_{A}}\mathscr{F}(\tau_{\leq n}B).

For case
(2)
, we observe that any covering
C^{0}_{A}
has a refinement which is generated by a single map
\{A\rightarrow B\}
such that
B
is connected by Hensel’s lemma. In particular, in this case,
B
is a finite local algebra lying over
A
. Note that the Čech nerve of
A\rightarrow B
is cofinal in this covering.
Then we can simplify the map
\gamma
as the following map

\gamma^{(2)}:\varinjlim_{B}\lim_{\Delta}\mathscr{F}(B^{\bullet})\longrightarrow\varprojlim_{n}\varinjlim_{B}\lim_{\Delta}\mathscr{F}(B^{\bullet}\otimes_{A}A/\mathfrak{m}^{n}),

where
B
runs over the category of finite étale local
A
-algebras.

Now, we prove the equivalenceness of
\gamma^{(1)}
and
\gamma^{(2)}
. Denote by
B_{n}
either
\tau_{\leq n}B
or
B\otimes_{A}A/\mathfrak{m}^{n}

We first compute the fiber of the map
\mathscr{F}(B)\rightarrow\mathscr{F}(B_{n})
. We denote by
L
a cotangent complex of
\mathscr{F}
. The fiber of
\mathscr{F}(B_{n+1})\rightarrow\mathscr{F}(B_{n})
at a point
(B_{n},M_{n})\in\mathscr{F}(B_{n})
is equivalent to
{\rm Map}_{B_{n}}(L|_{B_{n}},J_{n})
. Here, we let

J_{n}=\begin{cases}\pi_{n+1}B[n+1],&\quad B_{n}=\tau_{\leq n}B;\\
\mathfrak{m}^{n}B/\mathfrak{m}^{n+1}B,&\quad B_{n}=B\otimes_{A}A/\mathfrak{m}^{n}.\end{cases}

Applying induction, if we denote by
F_{m,n}(B)
be the fiber of the map
\mathscr{F}(B_{m})\rightarrow\mathscr{F}(B_{m})
, then there is a tower

...\longrightarrow F_{m,n}(B)\longrightarrow F_{m-1,n}(B)\longrightarrow...\longrightarrow F_{n+1,n}(B)\simeq{\rm Map}_{B_{n}}(L|_{B_{n}},J_{n}),

whose layers are given by
{\rm fib}(F_{m,n}(B)\rightarrow F_{m-1,n}(B))\simeq F_{m+1,m}(B)\simeq{\rm Map}_{B_{m}}(L|_{B_{m}},J_{m})
.
Using the fact
\mathscr{F}(B)\simeq\varprojlim\mathscr{F}(B_{n})
proved in Lemma
4.65
, the fiber of the map
\mathscr{F}(B)\rightarrow\mathscr{F}(B_{n})
at
(B_{n},M_{n})
is equivalent to the inverse limit
F_{\infty,n}(B):=\varprojlim_{k}F_{n+k,n}(B).

Note that the layer
B\mapsto{\rm Map}_{B_{n}}(L|_{B_{n}},J_{n})
of the tower
\{F_{m,n}(-)\}_{m}
defines an étale sheaf on
A
. Applying the induction, we know that
F_{m,n},n\leq m\leq\infty
are sheaves on the étale site of
A
. And it follows that the fiber of
\gamma^{(i)},i=1,2
at a point
\{(A_{n},M_{n})\}_{n=0}^{\infty}
is equivalent to
\varprojlim_{n}F_{\infty,n}(A)\simeq\varprojlim_{n}\varprojlim_{m}F_{m,n}(A)\simeq\varprojlim_{n}F_{n,n}(A)=0
. This shows that
\varphi
is
(-1)
-truncated.

We still need to show that
\varphi
is essentially surjective. Choose an étale cover
A_{0}\rightarrow A_{0}^{\prime}
of
A_{0}
such that the pullback
\mathscr{M}^{\prime}_{0}=\mathscr{M}_{0}|_{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}A_{0}^{\prime}}
is log-affine.
Then by the rigidity theorem of Lurie
[
37
, Theorem 7.5.4.2]
, there is essentially a unique commutative diagram
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces...}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{n-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces...}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces...}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{\prime}_{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{\prime}_{n-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces...}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{0}^{\prime}}

such that all vertical maps are étale covers. The limit map
A\rightarrow\varprojlim_{n}A^{\prime}_{n}
is also an étale cover by
[
37
, Theorem 7.5.4.2]
again. Let
\mathscr{M}_{n}^{\prime}
be the pullback of
\mathscr{M}_{n}
to
{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}A^{\prime}_{n}
for
n\geq 0
. By Lemma
4.52
, the square-zero extension of a charted log-affine object
{\rm Spec}(A,M)
also has a chart, which is functorially dependent on the
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log ring
M
. More precisely, one can construct a sequence of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log rings
(A^{\prime}_{n},M^{\prime}_{n})
, such that for any
n\geq 0,
the pullback of
M^{\prime}_{n+1}
along the map
A^{\prime}_{n+1}\rightarrow A^{\prime}_{n}
is equivalent to
M^{\prime}_{n}
, and the map
{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(A^{\prime}_{n},M^{\prime}_{n})\rightarrow{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(A^{\prime}_{n+1},M_{n+1}^{\prime})
is induced from
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring map
(A^{\prime}_{n+1},M^{\prime}_{n+1})\rightarrow(A^{\prime}_{n},M^{\prime}_{n})
. Passing to the limit, we get a log-affine stack
{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})=|{\rm Spec}(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})|,
where
(A^{\prime},M^{\prime})=\varprojlim(A^{\prime}_{n},M^{\prime}_{n}),
which is a pre-image of
\{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(A_{n}^{\prime},M_{n}^{\prime})\}_{n=0}^{\infty}\in\varprojlim\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm qcoh}_{|S|}(A_{n}^{\prime})
. Finally, applying the Descendability Theorem
4.44
of
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm qcoh}_{|S|},
one gets a log structure
\mathscr{M}
over
{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}A,
which is as desired.
∎

Let
f:X\rightarrow Y
be a morphism of functors defined over the
\infty
-category
\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}.
We say that
f
is
locally almost of finite presentation
if it satisfies the following condition:

Let
m\geq 0
, and let
\{A_{j}\}_{j\in J}
be a filtered diagram of
m
-truncated connective
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings
with colimit
A
. Then the canonical map

\theta:\varinjlim X(A_{j})\longrightarrow X(A)\times_{Y(A)}\varinjlim Y(A_{j})

is an equivalence.

Proposition 4.66
.

Let
\mu\in\{\rm coh,fin\}
, and let
\mathscr{L}
be a
\mu
-log structure over
\mathcal{S}
.The morphism
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\mu}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}\rightarrow\mathcal{S}
is locally almost of finite presentation.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we may replace
\mathcal{S}
by some étale cover of it and, therefore, assume that
(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})\simeq{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R,L)
is log-affine. We have to show that the functor

\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\mu}_{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R,L)}:\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}_{R}\longrightarrow\mathbf{Ani}

is locally almost of finite presentation, i.e., for any filtered diagram of
m
-truncated
R
-algebras
\{A_{j}\}_{j\in J}
with colimit
A
, the following natural map

\theta:\varinjlim\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\mu}_{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R,L)}(A_{j})\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\mu}_{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R,L)}(A)

is an equivalence. Applying
[
38
, Corollary 17.4.2.2]
, Theorem
4.56
and Proposition
4.62
, we will assume that
A_{j}
is discrete for any
j\in J.

We first prove that the map
\theta
is effectively epimorphic. Let
\mathscr{M}
be a
\mu
-log structure over
{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}A.
Choose an étale cover
A\rightarrow A^{\prime}
, such that the restriction
\mathscr{M}|_{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}A^{\prime}}
is log-affine. Using
[
3
, Lemma 29.36.14]
, we might assume that
A^{\prime}
is a finite product of standard étale
A
-algebras, i.e., there exist
a_{1},a_{2},...,a_{n}\in A
and
f_{1},f_{2},...,f_{n}\in A[x],
such that
A^{\prime}=\prod_{i}A[a_{i}^{-1}][x]/(f_{i}).
Then we might also assume that
a_{i},i=1,2,...,n,
and all coefficients of
f_{i},i=1,2,...,n,
belong to
A_{j},j\in J.
Let
A^{\prime}_{j}=\prod_{i}A_{j}[a_{j}^{-1}][x]/(f_{j})
, then we may assume that the natural map
A_{j}\rightarrow A_{j}^{\prime}
is an étale cover for any
j\in J.
We have an isomorphism
\varinjlim A_{j}^{\prime}\simeq A^{\prime}
of discrete rings. Form a commutative diagram of animae

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\varinjlim\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\mu}_{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R,L)}(A_{j})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\mu}_{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R,L)}(A)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\varinjlim\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\mu}_{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R,L)}(A^{\prime}_{j})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\mu}_{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R,L)}(A^{\prime})}}

Since
({\rm Sp\acute{e}t}A^{\prime},\mathscr{M})
is log-affine, there is a
\mu
-
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log ring
(A^{\prime},M^{\prime}),
such that
({\rm Sp\acute{e}t}A^{\prime},\mathscr{M})\simeq{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(A^{\prime},M^{\prime}).
In particular, there is an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-monoid
P
, such that
\mathbb{S}[P]
is almost of finite presentation, and there is an equivalence
M^{\prime}=P^{a}.
The finiteness of
P
implies that the
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring map
\mathbb{S}[P]\rightarrow A^{\prime}
factors through some
A^{\prime}_{j}
. Denote by
M^{\prime}_{j}
the monoid of logification of the
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-prelog ring
(A^{\prime}_{j},P),
and let
\mathscr{M}^{\prime}_{j}
be the corresponding log structure over
{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}A_{j}^{\prime}.
Applying the descendability Theorem
4.44
of
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\mu}_{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R,L)},
one obtains a unique log structure
\mathscr{M}_{j}
over
{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}A_{j}
, and the pullback of
\mathscr{M}_{j}
along the map
{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}A\rightarrow{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}A_{j}
is equivalent to
\mathscr{M}.

We prove that the map
\theta
has contractible fibers. Let
F
be the fiber of
\theta
at
\mathscr{M}
, and let
\mathscr{M}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\mu}_{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R,L)}(A_{i}),\mathscr{M}_{j}\in\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\mu}_{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R,L)}(A_{j})
, such that the pullback of them to
{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}A
are equivalent to
\mathscr{M}
. By virtue of the argument above, there is an index
k\geq i,j
and a log structure
\mathscr{M}_{k}
over
{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}A_{k}
, such that
\mathscr{M}_{k}
is equivalent to the pullback of
\mathscr{M}_{i}
and
\mathscr{M}_{j}.
It turns out that we can identify
\mathscr{M}_{i}
and
\mathscr{M}_{j}
via the canonical equivalence
f_{ik}^{*}\mathscr{M}_{i}\simeq f_{jk}^{*}\mathscr{M}_{j}
, in which we denote
f_{ik}
and
f_{jk}
as the transition maps
A_{i}\rightarrow A_{k}
and
A_{j}\rightarrow A_{k}
. This shows that
F
is contractible.
∎

4.3.4
Truncatedness

Definition 4.67
.

Let
(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{M})
be a spectral log Deligne-Mumford stack. We say that
(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{M})
is
n
-
admissible
, if there is an étale cover
\coprod^{N}_{j=1}{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}R_{j}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
of spectral Deligne-Mumford stacks, such that the pullback

\mathscr{M}|_{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}R_{j}}
are given by

n
-admissible
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log rings. We say that it is admissible if it is
n
-admissible for some
n<+\infty
.

Remark 4.68
.

By Remark
2.32
, the
n
-admissibility of spectral log Deligne-Mumford stacks is local on the underlying Deligne-Mumford stacks with respect to the fpqc topology.

Remark 4.69
.

{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(A,M)
is
n
-admissible if and only if
(A,M)
is
n
-admissible.

Proposition 4.70
.

Let
(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{M}),(\mathcal{Y},\mathscr{N})
be spectral log Deligne-Mumford stacks. Assume that
the following conditions are satisfied:

1.

\mathcal{X}
and
\mathcal{Y}
are affine;

2.

\mathcal{X}
is
m
-truncated;

3.

(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{M})
is
n
-admissible

Then the mapping space
{\rm Map}((\mathcal{X},\mathscr{M}),(\mathcal{Y},\mathscr{N}))
is
(1+\max\{n,m\})
-truncated.

Proof.

Let
(\mathcal{Y},\mathscr{N})
be an arbitrary spectral log Deligne-Mumford stack. Then by the definition of the
\infty
-category
\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{SpDM}
introduced in
subsection 4.2
, there is a canonical map

\theta:{\rm Map}((\mathcal{X},\mathscr{M}),(\mathcal{Y},\mathscr{N}))\longrightarrow{\rm Map}(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y}).

The anima
{\rm Map}(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y})
is
m
-truncated. We must consider the fibers of the map
\theta
. Let
f\in\pi_{0}{\rm Map}(\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y})
. The fiber of
\theta
at
f
is equivalent to the anima
{\rm Map}_{/\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}}(f^{*}\mathscr{N},\mathscr{M})\simeq\lim_{R\in\mathcal{X}_{{\rm\acute{e}t}}}{\rm Map}_{/R}(f^{*}\mathscr{N}(R),\mathscr{M}(R)).
Note that each term
{\rm Map}_{/R}(f^{*}\mathscr{N}(R),\mathscr{M}(R))
is
(1+\max\{n,m\})
-truncated, then so is
{\rm Map}_{/\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}}(f^{*}\mathscr{N},\mathscr{M}).
It follows that
{\rm Map}((\mathcal{X},\mathscr{M}),(\mathcal{Y},\mathscr{N}))
is
(1+\max\{n,m\})
-truncated.
∎

Definition 4.71
.

Let
\mu\in\{\rm qcoh,coh,fin\}
, and let
(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})
be a
\mu
-spectral log stack.
Note that
n
-admissible objects are stable under pullbacks, so there are subfunctors
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{n-\rm Adm,\mu}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}
and
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm Adm,\mu}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}
of
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\mu}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}
consisting of
n
-admissible and admissible objects respectively.

Remark 4.72
.

It’s easy to see that
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{n-\rm Adm,\mu}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}
and
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm Adm,\mu}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}
satisfy étale hyperdescent and all infinitesimal properties investigated in
subsubsection 4.3.3
.

The stack
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm Adm,\mu}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}
admits a filtration of substacks

\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{0-{\rm Adm,\mu}}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}\subset\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{1-{\rm Adm},\mu}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}\subset...\subset\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{n-{\rm Adm},\mu}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}\subset...

where
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{n-{\rm Adm},\mu}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}
is the substack of
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{{\rm Adm},\mu}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}
consisting of
n
-admissible objects. We have
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{{\rm Adm},\mu}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}\simeq\bigcup_{n}\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{n-{\rm Adm},\mu}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}
.

Lemma 4.73
.

The inclusions
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{n-{\rm Adm},\mu}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}\subset\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{m-{\rm Adm},\mu}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})},n\leq m
and
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{{\rm Adm},\mu}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}\subset\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\mu}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}
are formally étale. As a consequence, the stack
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{n-{\rm Adm},\mu}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}
admits a cotangent complex for any
n\leq+\infty
.

Proof.

Let
A
be a connective
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring and let
I\in\mathbf{Mod}_{A}^{\rm cn}
. Let us form a commutative diagram of stacks

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Spec}(A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{n-{\rm Adm},\mu}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm Spec}(A\oplus I)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{m-{\rm Adm},\mu}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x^{\prime}}

Without loss of generality, we assume that both
x
and
x^{\prime}
classify affine log structures
{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(A,M)
and
{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(A\oplus I,M^{\prime})
respectively, such that
(A\oplus I,M^{\prime})
is a square-zero extension of
(A,M)
.
Using Remark
2.32
, the
n
-admissibility is preserved under square-zero extensions, and therefore the space of liftings
{\rm Spec}(A\oplus I)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{n-{\rm Adm},\mu}
is contractible.
∎

5
Spectral algebraic stacks

In order to study the moduli functor of spectral log structures, we have to consider the algebraicity in some sense. In other words, we want a conception of
Artin stacks
in spectral algebraic geometry. At first, we need a concept of smoothness for defining the notion of atlas
U
over stacks
X
. However, in spectral algebraic geometry, following
[
38
, Section III.11.2.2]
, there are at least two kinds of smoothness for maps of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings (and spectral Deligne-Mumford stacks)
f:A\rightarrow B
:

1.

Fiber smooth
, the map of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings
f:A\rightarrow B
is flat, and the induced map
\pi_{0}(f):\pi_{0}(A)\rightarrow\pi_{0}(B)
is smooth as ordinary rings;

2.

Differentially smooth
(in the following we refer to “smooth” for simplicity),
the map of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings
f:A\rightarrow B
is almost of finite presentation, and
the cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{B/A}
is a projective object in the
\infty
-category
\mathbf{Mod}_{B}
of
B
-modules.

It seems that if we adopt the fiber-smoothness to define our algebraic stack, the associated deformation theory will be pathological, because the fiber-smoothness does not determine the infinitesimal lifting property in spectral algebraic geometry. Hence, in this section, we define Artin stacks using differential smoothness. Informally, an Artin stack is an étale sheaf over
\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}
with representable diagonal and admits a differentially smooth surjective map from a spectral algebraic space.

5.1
Artin stacks

Definition 5.1
.

Let
f:X\rightarrow Y
be a morphism in
\mathbf{Stk}
. We say that
f
is
0
-representable, or a
spectral relative algebraic space
, if for any connective
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring
R
, and any morphism
{\rm Spec}R\rightarrow Y
, the fiber product
X\times_{Y}{\rm Spec}R
is a spectral algebraic space over
R
. Here a spectral algebraic space means a functor which is represented by some spectral Deligne-Mumford
0
-stack
12
12

12

Here a spectral Deligne-Mumford
0
-stack
\mathcal{X}
is a spectral Deligne-Mumford stack such that
\mathcal{X}(R)
is discrete for any discrete
R
.
.

Let
n
be a natural number. The morphism
f
is called
n
-representable, if the diagonal
\Delta_{X/Y}:X\rightarrow X\times_{Y}X
is
(n-1)
-representable.

Definition 5.2
.

Let
\mathbb{P}
be one of the following properties of morphisms of spectral relative algebraic spaces:

Smoothness, Fiber-smoothness, Flatness, Étaleness

We say that a morphism
f:X\rightarrow Y
of sheaves has the property
n
-
\mathbb{P}
, if it’s
n
-presentable, and for any
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring
R
, and any map
{\rm Spec}R\rightarrow Y
, there is an
(n-1)
-
\mathbb{P}
surjective map
U\rightarrow X\times_{Y}{\rm Spec}R
, such that the composition
U\rightarrow X\times_{Y}{\rm Spec}R
is a spectral relative algebraic space, and has the property
\mathbb{P}.
We will say that the morphism
f
has the property
\mathbb{P}
, if it has the property
n
-
\mathbb{P}
for some
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}.

Definition 5.3
.

Assume that we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}

such that
U/Y
is a spectral relative algebraic space, and the map
U\rightarrow X
is surjective and has the property
\mathbb{P}
. We will say that
U
is a
\mathbb{P}
-
atlas
of
X
.

Definition 5.4
.

Let
f:X\rightarrow Y
be a morphism in
\mathbf{Stk}
. We say that
f
is

1.

A
relative

n
-
Artin stack
, if it is
n
-representable, and admits a smooth atlas locally, i.e., for any map
{\rm Spec}A\rightarrow Y
, the fiber product
X\times_{Y}{\rm Spec}A
admits a smooth atlas;

2.

A
relative Artin stack
is a relative
n
-Artin stack for some
n\geq 0
;

3.

A
relative locally Artin stack
is such that for any map
{\rm Spec}R\rightarrow Y
, the fiber product
X\times_{Y}{\rm Spec}R
is a union of open substacks that are Artin.

Remark 5.5
.

It’s easy to see that:

1.

If
f:X\rightarrow Y
is a relative
n
-Artin stack, then it’s a relative
m
-Artin stack, for any
m\geq n
;

2.

The pullback of a relative
n
-Artin stack is also a relative
n
-Artin stack;

3.

The finite limit of relative
n
-Artin stacks is also an
n
-Artin stack.

We present some basic properties of spectral Artin stacks, which are proved for derived Artin stacks by Lurie in
[
34
]
. However, the proofs also translate word by word for spectral Artin stacks by definition. We only summarize the main results below and refer to the proofs in Lurie’s thesis.

Proposition 5.6
.

[
34
, Proposition 5.1.5]

A relative
n
-Artin stack
f:X\rightarrow Y
admits a
(-n)
-connective cotangent complex.

Proposition 5.7
.

[
34
, Proposition 5.3.7]
and
[
34
, Theorem 6.4.1]

A relative
n
-Artin stack
f:X\rightarrow Y
is infinitesimally cohesive, nil-complete, and integrable.

Lemma 5.8
.

[
34
, Proposition 5.1.4]

Let
f:X\rightarrow Y
be a map of sheaves. The following conditions are equivalent.

1.

The map
f
is a relative
n
-Artin stack.

2.

For every spectral algebraic space
U/Y
, and every map
\pi:U\rightarrow X
, then the map
\pi
is a relative
(n-1)
-Artin stack.

3.

There is a spectral algebraic space
V/Y,
and a smooth cover
V\rightarrow X
which is a relative
(n-1)
-Artin stack.

4.

There is an
(n-1)
-Artin stack
W/Y
, and a smooth cover
W\rightarrow X,
which is a relative
(n-1)
-Artin stack.

5.2
Artin-Lurie’s representability theorem

We generalize the proof of Artin-Lurie representability for derived Artin stacks
[
34
, Theorem 7.1.6]
and spectral Deligne-Mumford stacks
[
38
, Theorem 18.3.0.1]
to spectral Artin stacks.

Let us first recall the existence of approximate charts by Lurie.

Proposition 5.9
.

[
38
, Proposition 18.3.1.1]

Let
R
be an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-G-ring, and let
X\in\mathbf{Stk}
. Suppose there is a morphism
q:X\rightarrow{\rm Spec}R,
that satisfies the following conditions:

1.

The functor
X
is infinitesimally cohesive, nilcomplete, and integrable.

2.

The morphism
q
is locally almost of finite presentation.

3.

The map
q
admits a cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{X/R}
.

Suppose we are given a field
k
and a map
f:{\rm Spec}k\rightarrow X
that exhibits
k
as a finitely
generated field extension of some residue field of
R.
Then the map
f
factors as a composition

{\rm Spec}k\rightarrow{\rm Spec}B\rightarrow X
, where
B
is almost of finite presentation over R and the vector space

\pi_{1}(\mathbb{L}_{B/X}\otimes k)
vanishes.

Let
X\in\mathbf{Stk}
be a sheaf that satisfies conditions
(1)
-
(3)
above. Let
f:{\rm Spec}B\rightarrow X
be a map such that the homotopy
\pi_{1}(\mathbb{L}_{B/X}\otimes k)=0
, where
x:{\rm Spec}k\rightarrow{\rm Spec}B
is a
k
point. Let
B_{0}
be the discrete
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring
\pi_{0}B
. Using the exact sequence

\mathbb{L}_{B/X}\otimes_{B}B_{0}\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}_{B_{0}/X}\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}_{B_{0}/B}

and
[
37
, Corollary 7.4.3.2]
, the cotangent complex
\pi_{1}\mathbb{L}_{B_{0}/B}=0
, we deduce that
\pi_{0}(\mathbb{L}_{B_{0}/X}\otimes_{B_{0}}k)=0.

Thus we might as well assume that
B=B_{0}
is
0
-truncated.

We can reformulate Lurie’s theorem on the refinement of approximate charts.

Proposition 5.10
.

Let
{\rm Spec}k\rightarrow{\rm Spec}B\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f}}{{\rightarrow}}X\rightarrow{\rm Spec}R
be as above. Then, after a suitable localization of
B
, there is a finitely presented
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebra
B^{\prime}
over
R
, such that there is a factorization

{\rm Spec}B\longrightarrow{\rm Spec}B^{\prime}\longrightarrow X,

and the map
B^{\prime}\rightarrow X
is formally smooth.

Proof.

Assume that
\pi_{j}(\mathbb{L}_{B/X}\otimes_{B}k)=0,j=1,2,...,r
. Recall that every complex
C
over a field
k
is quasi-isomorphic to a split chain complex
C\simeq\oplus_{j}(\pi_{j}C)[j]
, the complex
\mathbb{L}_{B/X}\otimes_{B}k
has a decomposition

N\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}_{B/X}\otimes_{B}k\longrightarrow M

where
M
is
(r+1)
-connective and
N
is coconnective, and bounded below. Assume that
\pi_{-m}N\neq 0
and
\tau_{\leq-m-1}N=0.

Since
B
and
X
are locally almost of finite presentation over
R
, the cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{B/X}
is almost perfect. Then
\pi_{-m}N\simeq k^{n_{-m}}
for some
n_{-m}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}.
This defines a map
k^{n_{-m}}[-m]\rightarrow N\rightarrow\mathbb{L}_{B/X}\otimes_{B}k
. After a suitable shrinking of
B
, we can lift it to a map
N_{0}^{-m}:=B^{n_{-m}}[-m]\rightarrow\mathbb{L}_{B/X}
. The cofiber
M_{0}^{-m}
is
{(-m)}
-connective, and almost finitely presented as
B
-modules. Note that we have that
\pi_{-m+1}(M^{-m}_{0}\otimes_{B}\eta)\simeq(\pi_{-m+1}M^{-m}_{0})\otimes^{\rm cl}_{B}\eta
13
13

13

We let
\otimes^{\rm cl}
denote the classical tensor product of modules
, for any
\eta\in{\rm MaxSpec}B
, which is a finite dimensional
\eta
-vector space. Then shrinking
B
again, we can construct a map
B^{n_{-m+1}}[-m+1]\rightarrow M_{0}^{-m},
lifting the isomorphism
k^{n_{-m+1}}\simeq\pi_{-m+1}(M_{0}^{-m}\otimes_{B}k).
Let
N_{0}^{-m+1}
be the cofiber of the composition

B^{n_{-m+1}}[-m]\longrightarrow M_{0}^{-m}[-1]\longrightarrow N_{0}^{-m}.

Let
M_{0}^{-m+1}
be the cofiber of the map
N_{0}^{-m+1}\rightarrow\mathbb{L}_{B/X}
. Then by induction, we construct a filtration on
\mathbb{L}_{B/X},

N_{0}^{-m}\longrightarrow N_{0}^{-m+1}\longrightarrow...\longrightarrow N_{0}^{0}=N_{0}\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}_{B/X}

such that the cofiber
M_{0}
of the map
N_{0}\rightarrow\mathbb{L}_{B/X}
is
(r+1)
-connective.
Because
N_{0}^{-j}
is obtained by taking the cofiber
B^{n_{-j-1}}[-j]\rightarrow N_{0}^{-j-1}
, we deduce that
N_{0}
is the dual of a connective perfect
R
-module.

The map
\mathbb{L}_{B/X}\rightarrow M_{0}
exhibits
M_{0}
as a
B
-derivation
\mathbb{L}_{B/R}\rightarrow\mathbb{L}_{B/X}\rightarrow M_{0}
. This defines a square-zero extension
B^{1}\rightarrow B
, lying over
X
, with kernel
M_{0}[-1]
. We have a factorization

{\rm Spec}B\longrightarrow{\rm Spec}B^{1}\longrightarrow X.

Using
[
37
, Theorem 7.4.3.1 and Corollary 7.4.3.2]
, the cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{B/B^{1}}
is
(r+1)
-connective, and there is a
(2r+2)
-connective map

M_{0}\otimes_{B^{1}}B\rightarrow\mathbb{L}_{B/B^{1}}.
Then the exact sequence

\mathbb{L}_{B^{1}/X}\otimes_{B^{1}}B\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}_{B/X}\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}_{B/B^{1}}

implies that
\pi_{j}(\mathbb{L}_{B^{1}/X}\otimes_{B^{1}}k)=0,1\leq j\leq r+1.

Then replace
B
by
B^{1}
, and passing to induction, there is a large enough
n
, and a sequence of maps

{\rm Spec}B\longrightarrow{\rm Spec}B^{1}\longrightarrow...\longrightarrow{\rm Spec}B^{n}\longrightarrow X,

such that
\pi_{j}(\mathbb{L}_{B^{n}/X}\otimes_{B^{n}}k)=0,1\leq j\leq n+r,
and there is an exact sequence

N_{i}\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}_{B_{i}/X}\longrightarrow M_{i},

such that

N_{i}
is dual to a connective perfect
B_{i}
-module and
M_{i}
is
(i+r)
-connective, and there is an equivalence
N_{i}\otimes_{B^{i}}B^{i-1}\simeq N_{i-1}.
Because the construction of
M_{i}
and
N_{i}
requires shrinking
B
finitely many times for each
i
, we can only construct such a sequence of sufficient large length
n
. We give another construction, which is suitable for the case
n
arbitrary large. Let
B^{n+1}
be the square-zero extension of
B^{n}
with kernel
M_{m}[-1]
. By construction, the
B^{n}
-module
N_{n}
admits a filtration

N_{n}^{-m}\longrightarrow N_{n}^{-m+1}\longrightarrow...\longrightarrow N_{n}^{0}=N_{n}

where
N_{n}^{-m+j}
is obtained by taking the cofiber of the map

(B^{n})^{\oplus n_{-m+j-1}}[-m]\longrightarrow N_{n}^{-m+j-1}

where the number
n_{-m+j-1}
is independent of
n
. We want to lift this filtration as a filtered
B^{n+1}
-module. For this, we proceed by induction. We first lift
N_{n}^{-m}\simeq(B^{n})^{n_{-m}}[-m]\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}_{B^{n}/X}
as the map
B^{n+1}
-module
(B^{n+1})^{n_{-m}}[-m]\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}_{B^{n+1}/X}
. This is possible because the cofiber of the map
\mathbb{L}_{B^{n+1}/X}\otimes_{B^{n+1}}B^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{L}_{B^{n}/X}
is
(n+r+1)
-connective. Assume that we already have constructed an lifting

N_{n+1}^{-m}\longrightarrow N_{n+1}^{-m+1}\longrightarrow...\longrightarrow N_{n+1}^{j}\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}_{B^{n+1}/X}

We have the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(B^{n+1})^{\oplus n_{j}}[j]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{n+1}^{j}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(B^{n})^{\oplus n_{j}}[j]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{n}^{j}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{n}^{j+1}}

To get a lifting
(B^{n+1})^{\oplus n_{j}}[j]\rightarrow N_{n+1}^{j+1}
, we have to prove that the map
(B^{n})^{\oplus n_{j}}\rightarrow N_{n}^{j}
could be lifted to a map of
B^{n+1}
-modules. It’s equivalent to prove that the corresponding homotopy classes in
\pi_{j}N_{n}^{j}
could be lifted to
\pi_{j}N_{n+1}^{j}
. This holds whenever the induced map
\pi_{j}N_{n+1}^{j}\rightarrow\pi_{j}N_{n}^{j}
is surjective. This could be deduced from the following exact sequence

N_{n+1}^{j}\otimes_{B^{n+1}}J\longrightarrow N_{n+1}^{j}\longrightarrow N_{n}^{j}

Where
J
is the fiber
B^{n+1}\rightarrow B^{n}
, and is
(n+r)
-connective. Therefore, we have that
\pi_{j-1}(N_{n+1}^{j}\otimes_{B^{n+1}}J)=0
, as long as
n
is large enough.

We constructed a projective system of almost of finitely presented
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebras over
R

\longrightarrow B^{n}\longrightarrow B^{n-1}\longrightarrow...\longrightarrow B^{1}\longrightarrow B

, which are lying over
X
, such that
\tau_{\leq n}B^{n+1}\simeq\tau_{\leq n}B^{n}.
Let
B^{\prime}
be the inverse limit of the above sequence. Clearly it’s almost finitely presented over
R
. The induced map
{\rm Spec}B^{\prime}\rightarrow X
is formally smooth, because it’s easy to see that
\mathbb{L}_{B^{\prime}/X}
is equivalent to the
inverse limit of the system
\{N_{n}\},
which is dual to a connective perfect
B^{\prime}
-module by construction.
∎

Let us consider the following adjoint pair

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{CAlg}^{\Delta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Theta^{L}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Theta}

in which the functor
\Theta
is the forgetful functor that carries an animated ring
A
to its underlying
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring
\Theta(A)
. Passing to Yoneda embeddings, we can define a functor

\Theta_{!}:\mathbf{Stk}^{\Delta}:=\mathbf{Shv}_{{\rm\acute{e}t}}(\mathbf{CAlg}^{\Delta},\mathbf{Ani})\rightarrow\mathbf{Stk}
given by the left Kan extension of the composition of functors

\mathbf{CAlg}^{\Delta,\rm op}\longrightarrow\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn,op}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\rm Spec}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbf{Shv}_{{\rm\acute{e}t}}

along the embedding

{\rm Spec}:\mathbf{CAlg}^{\Delta,\rm op}\longrightarrow{\rm Fun}(\mathbf{CAlg}^{\Delta},\mathbf{Ani}).

The functor
\Theta_{!}
is left adjoint to the restriction functor
\Theta^{*}.
In addition, the pushout
\Theta_{*}
is right adjoint to
\Theta^{*}
.

Remark 5.11
.

The functor
\Theta^{*}
preserves affine objects. Indeed, if
A
is a connective
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring, we have
\Theta^{*}{\rm Spec}A={\rm Map}_{\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}}(A,\Theta(-))\simeq{\rm Map}_{\mathbf{CAlg}^{\Delta}}(\Theta^{L}(A),-)
.

Remark 5.12
.

Let
R
be an animated ring, and let
R^{\circ}
be the underlying
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring of
R
. Consider the
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring map
R\rightarrow\pi_{0}(R)
, this map has a unique factorization

R\longrightarrow\Theta^{L}(R)^{\circ}\longrightarrow\pi_{0}(R).

Passing to the

0
-th homotopy, we know that
\pi_{0}(R)
is a retraction of
\pi_{0}(\Theta^{L}(R^{\circ})).

Lemma 5.13
.

Consider the adjoint pair

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{Stk}^{\Delta}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{Stk}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Theta_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Theta^{*}}

induced by the forgetful functor
\mathbf{CAlg}^{\Delta}\rightarrow\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}
, then we have

1.

\Theta^{*}
preserves locally almost finitely presented, nilcomplete and infinitesimally cohesive maps;

2.

\Theta^{*}
preserves surjective map;

3.

\Theta^{*}
preserves formally étale maps and étale maps;

4.

\Theta^{*}
preserves
n
-representable maps;

5.

If
f:X\rightarrow Y
is
n
-differentially smooth then
\Theta^{*}f
is a smooth map of derived stacks;

6.

If
f:X\rightarrow Y
is relative
n
-Artin, then so is
\Theta^{*}f
.

Proof.

(1).
This is clear because the forgetful functor
\Theta:\mathbf{CAlg}^{\Delta}\rightarrow\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}
preserves arbitrary limits and filtered colimits.

(2)
. Let
f:X\rightarrow Y
be a surjective map. Let
R
be an animated ring, and let
u:{\rm Spec}R\rightarrow\Theta^{*}Y
. By adjointness, this is equivalent to giving a map
{\rm Spec}R^{\circ}\rightarrow Y,
where
R^{\circ}=\Theta(R)
is the underlying
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring of
R
. After an étale localization
R^{\circ^{\prime}}
of
R^{\circ}
, there is a lifting
{\rm Spec}R^{\circ^{\prime}}\rightarrow X.

[
34
, Theorem 3.4.13]
and
[
37
, Theorem 7.5.4.2]
implies that
R^{\circ^{\prime}}
has a unique animated
R
-algebra structure
R^{\prime}
, such that
R^{\prime}
is étale over
R
. This gives rise to a lifting
{\rm Spec}R^{\prime}\rightarrow\Theta^{*}X.

(3)
. Because of
(1),
we just only need to consider the formally étale maps. Let
X\rightarrow Y
be a formally étale map. Let us consider the following lifting property of derived stacks

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Spec}R}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Theta^{*}X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Spec}(R\oplus I)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Theta^{*}Y}

This is equivalent to studying the following lifting property of spectral stacks

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Spec}R^{\circ}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Spec}(R\oplus I)^{\circ}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}

By the assumption that
X\rightarrow Y
is formally étale, the lifting
{\rm Spec}(R\oplus I)^{\circ}\rightarrow X
is unique. Therefore, the lifting
{\rm Spec}(R\oplus I)\rightarrow\Theta^{*}X
is unique.

(4)
. If
f:X\rightarrow Y
is
0
-representable,
as
\Theta^{*}
preserves small limits, and affine objects, we can assume that
Y\simeq{\rm Spec}R
is an affine scheme, then
X
is an algebraic space over
R.
There is an étale cover of spectral stacks
\coprod_{j}{\rm Spec}A_{j}\rightarrow X.
Then applying
(1)
and
(2)
, the pullback
\coprod_{j}{\rm Spec}\Theta^{L}(A_{j})\rightarrow\Theta^{*}X
is an étale cover of
\Theta^{*}X.

Assume this holds for
n
-representable maps, then let
g
be an
(n+1)
-representable map, it’s equal to say that the diagonal
\Delta_{g}
of
g
is
n
-representable. Since the functor
\Theta^{*}
preserves limits, we know that the functor
\Theta^{*}g
is
(n+1)
-representable.

(5)
. Without loss of generality, assume that
Y\simeq{\rm Spec}A
is affine. If
f
is
0
-smooth, then it’s a relative algebraic space. Since
X
is locally equivalent to some
{\rm Spec}B
, where
A\rightarrow B
is a smooth map, by
[
38
, Proposition 11.2.2.1]
, we can localize
B
suitably, such that there is an étale map
B\rightarrow A\{x_{1},x_{2},...,x_{m}\}
. Applying the functor
i^{*}
, we get an étale map

\Theta^{L}(B)\rightarrow\Theta^{L}(A)[x_{1},x_{2},...,x_{m}],

which is equivalent to the map
\Theta^{L}(A)\rightarrow\Theta^{L}(B)
being smooth as an animated ring map. Assume that this holds for
n
-differentially smooth maps. Now let
X
be
(n+1)
-smooth over
Y
, there is an atlas

U\rightarrow X
, which is
n
-differentially smooth. By assumption,
\Theta^{*}U\rightarrow\Theta^{*}X
and
\Theta^{*}U\rightarrow\Theta^{*}{\rm Spec}A
are smooth. Thus
\Theta^{*}X\rightarrow\Theta^{*}{\rm Spec}A
is smooth.

(6)
is deduced from
(1)-(5)
.
∎

Proposition 5.14
.

If
q:X\rightarrow{\rm Spec}R
satisfies those conditions in Proposition
5.9
, then there is an effective epimorphism
U\rightarrow X,
such that
U
is equivalent to a disjoint union of spectral schemes, and formally smooth over
X
.

Proof.

Let
U
be the union of all possible
{\rm Spec}B
, which
are constructed in Proposition
5.10
.
Let
{\rm Spec}A\rightarrow X
be an arbitrary map, we will prove that, after an étale localization
A^{\prime}
, there is a lifting
{\rm Spec}A^{\prime}\rightarrow U.

Note that we just need to prove that there is a lifting
{\rm Spec}\pi_{0}A^{\prime}\rightarrow U
of
{\rm Spec}\pi_{0}A^{\prime}\rightarrow X
. Indeed, suppose that there is a lifting
{\rm Spec}\pi_{0}A^{\prime}\rightarrow U
. Consider the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Spec}\pi_{0}A^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Spec}\tau_{\leq 1}A^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces...}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Spec}\tau_{\leq n}A^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces...}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}

Since
U\rightarrow X
is formally smooth, applying
induction, one can construct a lifting
{\rm Spec}\tau_{\leq n}A^{\prime}\rightarrow U
for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
, such that the above diagram commutes. Because
X
is nil-complete, these maps
\{{\rm Spec}\tau_{\leq n}A^{\prime}\rightarrow U\}
determine a unique map
{\rm Spec}A^{\prime}\rightarrow U
. For this reason, we assume that
A
is discrete.

Consider the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Theta_{!}\Theta^{*}U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Theta_{!}\Theta^{*}X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}

Note that any map
{\rm Spec}A\rightarrow X
from a discrete ring
A
factors through
\Theta_{!}\Theta^{*}X
, this induces a map of derived stacks
{\rm Spec}A\rightarrow\Theta^{*}X
. The proof of
[
34
, Lemma 7.2.2]
shows that
\Theta^{*}U\rightarrow\Theta^{*}X
is an effective epimorphism in the
\infty
-topos
\mathbf{Stk}^{\Delta}
. We then replace
A
with some étale algebra
A^{\prime}
, such that there is a lifting
{\rm Spec}A^{\prime}\rightarrow\Theta^{*}U.
Then we have a lifting
{\rm Spec}A^{\prime}\rightarrow U
using the following commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Theta_{!}\Theta^{*}U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Spec}A^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Theta_{!}\Theta^{*}X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}

∎

Let
\mathbf{CAlg}_{\leq j}
be the subcategory of
\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}
spanned by
j
-truncated
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings; the
fully faithful inclusion

i:\mathbf{CAlg}_{\leq j}\rightarrow\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}
admits a left adjoint
\tau_{\leq j}
. Therefore, the inclusion functor
i
preserves limits, and it turns out that the left Kan-extension
i_{!}:{\rm Fun}(\mathbf{CAlg}_{\leq j},\mathbf{Ani})\rightarrow{\rm Fun}(\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn},\mathbf{Ani})
of
i
preserves colimits. In addition, the left adjoint
i^{*}
of
i_{!}
, which is, in fact, the restriction functor, preserves these objects that satisfy étale (hyper)-descent. Hence, we obtain an adjoint pair

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{Shv}_{{\rm\acute{e}t}}(\mathbf{CAlg}_{\leq j},\mathbf{Ani})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{Stk}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{*}}

We denote by
\tau_{\leq j}
the composition
i_{!}\circ i^{*}
, and refer to it as the
j
-th truncation of a sheaf
X
.

Note that the functor
i_{!}
is a fully faithful embedding of
\infty
-categories, and we denote by
{\mathbf{Shv}_{{\rm\acute{e}t}}(\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn},\mathbf{Ani})_{\leq j}}
its essential image, which we refer to as the
\infty
-category of
j
-truncated sheaves.
We have the following fact:

Lemma 5.15
.

[
38
, Theorem 18.1.0.2.]

Let
q:X\rightarrow{\rm Spec}R
be a map of sheaves that is infinitesimally cohesive and nilcomplete. Assume that it has a cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{X/R}
. If the truncation
\tau_{\leq 0}X
is equivalent to a
0
-truncated Deligne-Mumford stack over
\pi_{0}R
, then
X
is equivalent to a spectral Deligne-Mumford stack over
R
. In particular, if
\tau_{\leq 0}X
is equivalent to
{\rm Spec}A
for some discrete algebra
A
over
\pi_{0}R
, then
X
is equivalent to
{\rm Spec}A^{\prime}
for some
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebra
A^{\prime}
over
R
, such that
\pi_{0}(A^{\prime})=A.

Theorem 5.16

(Artin-Lurie representability)
.

Let
X:\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}\rightarrow\mathbf{Ani}
be a sheaf, and let
R
be an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-G-ring. Suppose that there is a morphism
q:X\rightarrow{\rm Spec}R
. Let
n
be a non-negative integer. Then
X
is represented by a spectral
n
-Artin stack locally almost of finite presentation if and only if
X
satisfies the following conditions:

1.

The functor
X
is nilcomplete, infinitesimally cohesive, and integrable.

2.

The functor
X
admits an almost connective cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{X/R}.

3.

The map
q
is locally almost of finite presentation, that is, let
A_{i}
be a filtered system of
m
-truncated
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings over
R
, let
X_{R}
be the fiber of the map
q
, then
X_{R}(\varinjlim A_{i})\simeq\varinjlim X_{R}(A_{i}).

4.

For every discrete
R
-algebra
A
, the space
X_{R}(A)
is n-truncated.

Proof.

We proceed by induction. For
n=-2
, i.e., the natural transformation
X\rightarrow{\rm Spec}R
is an equivalence after restricting to the subcategory
\mathbf{CAlg}_{\leq 0}
of discrete
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings in
\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}
. By lemma
5.15
, we have that the functor
X
is represented by an affine scheme
{\rm Spec}A
for some
A
. Now assume that the theorem holds for
(n-1)
. Let
X
be
n
-truncated restricted to discrete rings. According to lemma
5.8
, we just need to prove there exists a smooth
(n-1)
-cover
U\rightarrow X
from an
(n-1)
-Artin stack. Thanks to proposition
5.9
and Proposition
5.14
, there is a spectral algebraic space
U
, which is locally almost finitely presented over
R
, and a formally smooth effective epimorphism
p:U\rightarrow X
. Then the fiber of the natural map

U\times_{X}U\rightarrow U\times_{R}U
is
(n-1)
-truncated after restricting to
\mathbf{CAlg}_{\leq 0}
, because it’s the base change of the diagonal
\Delta_{X/R}
along the map
U\times_{R}U\rightarrow X\times_{R}X
. By induction assumption,
U\times_{X}U
is an
(n-1)
-Artin stack. We have to show that the induced map
U\times_{X}U\rightarrow X
is a relative
(n-1)
-Artin stack. Let
V={\rm Spec}A
be an affine scheme and consider a map
x:V\rightarrow X
. Shrink
V
such that
x
has a lifting
x^{\prime}:V\rightarrow U
. Denote by
\delta
the induced diagonal map
\delta:V\rightarrow U\times_{X}U
. We have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{U\times_{X}U\times_{X}V}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{U{\times_{X}U\times_{X}U\times_{X}U}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{U\times_{X}U}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{U\times_{X}U}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta}

Every square is Cartesian. The sheaf

U{\times_{X}U\times_{X}U\times_{X}U}
is an
(n-1)
-Artin stack. But
U\times_{X}U
is
(n-1)
-Artin, one has that the fiber product
U\times_{X}U\times_{X}V\simeq U{\times_{X}U\times_{X}U\times_{X}U}\times_{(U\times_{X}U)}V
is an
(n-1)
-Artin stack. This shows that the map
U\times_{X}U\rightarrow X
is
(n-1)
-Artin.
∎

6
Algebraicity of Moduli of log stacks

As we have established the theory of deformations of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log rings, constructed the moduli stack of log structures over spectral log stacks, and studied the relevant deformation properties, now we are able to prove the representability theorem of the moduli of log structures. The main result is Theorem
6.1
. We also study the moduli stack of animated log structures over derived stacks, and we have Theorem
6.11
.

6.1
Algebraicity

We prove the algebraicity of the moduli stack of log structures.

Theorem 6.1
.

Let
\mathcal{S}
be a quasi-compact and quasi-separated spectral Deligne-Mumford stack. Assume that there is an étale cover
{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}R\rightarrow\mathcal{S}
from a spectral affine scheme such that
R
is an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-Grothendieck ring. Let
\mu\in\{\rm coh,fin\}
, and let
\mathscr{L}
be a
\mu
-log structure over
\mathcal{S}
. Fix a natural number
n\geq 0.

Then the stack
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{n-{\rm Adm},\mu}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}
is a
(1+n)
-Artin and locally almost finitely presented over
\mathcal{S}
.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we might as well assume that there is an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-
\mu
log ring
(R,L)
, in which
R
is a Grothendieck
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log ring, and there is an equivalence
(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})\simeq{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R,L)
. We apply Artin-Lurie’s representability Theorem
5.16
.
We have to check the descent and deformation properties of the map
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{n-{\rm Adm},\mu}_{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R,L)}\rightarrow{\rm Spec}R
.
They are guaranteed by Theorem
4.44
, Theorem
4.56
, Proposition
4.62
, Proposition
4.64
, Proposition
4.66
Proposition
4.70
, Remark
4.72
and Lemma
4.73
.
∎

We also have the following result.

Corollary 6.2
.

Under the same assumption as Theorem
6.1
. The Stack
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm Adm,\mu}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}
is locally Artin and locally almost finitely presented over
\mathcal{S}
.

6.2
Classical truncations and comparisons

Let
(\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit},\mathscr{L}^{\heartsuit})
be a classical log Deligne-Mumford stack. Denote by
\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit}_{{\rm\acute{e}t}}\simeq\mathbf{Shv}_{{\rm\acute{e}t}}(\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit},{\rm Set})
the underlying
1
-topos of
\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit}
.
The log structure
\mathscr{L}^{\heartsuit}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit}}
should be thought of as a map in the
1
-category
\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit}_{{\rm\acute{e}t}}\otimes_{\rm Set}\mathbf{CMon}^{\heartsuit}.

Let
\mathcal{S}
be the associated spectral Deligne-Mumford stack of
\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit}
, and let
\mathscr{L}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}}
be the image of
\mathscr{L}^{\heartsuit}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit}}
in
\mathbf{Shv}_{{\rm\acute{e}t}}(\mathcal{S},\mathbf{Ani})
under the canonical functor

\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit}_{{\rm\acute{e}t}}\otimes_{\rm Set}\mathbf{CMon}^{\heartsuit}\longrightarrow\mathcal{S}_{{\rm\acute{e}t}}\otimes_{\mathbf{Ani}}\mathbf{CMon}

It’s not difficult to see that the map
\mathscr{L}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{S}}
is a log structure over
\mathcal{S}
. This defines a fully faithful embedding

\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{SpDM}^{\rm cl}\longrightarrow\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{SpDM}.

Now assume that
\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit}
admits an étale cover of Grothendieck rings, and
\mathscr{L}^{\heartsuit}
is a fine log structure. Define the moduli functor of classical log structures lying over
(\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit},\mathscr{L}^{\heartsuit})
as the following:

\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit},\mathscr{L}^{\heartsuit})}:{\mathbf{DM}^{\rm op}_{/\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit}}}\longrightarrow\mathbf{Grpd}

which sends a relative classical Deligne-Mumford stack
\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit}
to the
1
-groupoid consisting of maps of
log Deligne-Mumford stacks
(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{M})\rightarrow(\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit},\mathscr{L}^{\heartsuit})
. In
[
45
]
, Olsson proved that this functor is a classical Artin stack and is locally finitely presented over
\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit}
in the classical sense. We denote by
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}^{\heartsuit}
the classical truncation of the stack
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm fin}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}
. By the classical Artin’s representability theorem, the functor
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}^{\heartsuit}
is a classical Artin stack and is locally finitely presented over
\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit}
.
We have a canonical map of classical Artin stacks

\theta:\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit},\mathscr{L}^{\heartsuit})}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}^{\heartsuit}.

Theorem 6.3
.

The map
\theta
is an open immersion, whose image is isomorphic to the classical truncation of
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{0-{\rm Adm},\rm fin}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}
.

Proof.

The map
\theta
is
(-1)
-truncated, and essentially surjective. We must show that it’s étale. Since we already know that both of the two stacks are locally finitely presented over
\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit}
, we just need to show that
\theta
is formally étale. Let
R
be a classical ring, and let
I\in\mathbf{Mod}_{R}^{\heartsuit}
. We form a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Spec}R}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit},\mathscr{L}^{\heartsuit})}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Spec}(R\oplus I)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\heartsuit}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x^{\prime}}

Without loss of generality, we assume that both
x
and
x^{\prime}
classify affine log structures
{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R,M)
and
{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R\oplus I,M^{\prime})
respectively, such that
(R\oplus I,M^{\prime})
is a square-zero extension of
(R,M)
. Then
M^{\prime}
is discrete because we have an exact sequence of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-monoids

(1+I)\longrightarrow M^{\prime}\longrightarrow M

This implies that we have a unique lifting
{\rm Spec}(R\oplus I)\rightarrow\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S}^{\heartsuit},\mathscr{L}^{\heartsuit})}
of
x^{\prime}
, making the commutative diagram as above commute. It turns out that the map
\theta
is formally étale.
∎

6.3
Moduli of derived log structures

In this subsection, we consider the moduli stack of derived log stacks.

6.3.1
Derived algebraic geometry

Let
\mathcal{X}
be a derived Deligne-Mumford stack in the sense of
[
34
]
, we denote by
\mathcal{X}^{\circ}
the underlying spectral Deligne-Mumford stack defined in
[
18
, 2.18]
. This gives rise to a functor
(-)^{\circ}:\mathbf{DM}^{\Delta}\rightarrow\mathbf{SpDM}
, which carries a
derived Deligne-Mumford stack
to its underlying spectral Deligne-Mumford stack. Here we denote by
\mathbf{DM}^{\Delta}

the
\infty
-category of derived Deligne-Mumford stacks. We have a commutative diagram of
\infty
-categories

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{DM}^{\Delta}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{SpDM}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{Stk}^{\Delta}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{Stk}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\circ}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Theta_{!}}

.
Both vertical arrows are fully faithful embeddings
[
34
, Proposition 4.6.2]

[
38
, Proposition 1.6.4.2]
. Applying Lemma
5.13
, the stack
\Theta^{*}h_{\mathcal{X}}
of a functor
h_{\mathcal{X}}
which is represented by a spectral Deligne-Mumford stack
\mathcal{X}
is also representable by some derived Deligne-Mumford stacks. In other words, the essential image of functor

\mathbf{SpDM}\longrightarrow\mathbf{Stk}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\Theta^{*}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbf{Stk}^{\Delta}

is landed in
\mathbf{DM}^{\Delta}
, thus it has a unique factorization
(-)^{\Delta}:\mathbf{SpDM}\rightarrow\mathbf{DM}^{\Delta}
, which is a right adjoint of
(-)^{\circ}
.

6.3.2
Derived log stacks

Definition 6.4
.

The strict étale map of animated log rings defines a sub-canonical topology on
\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta}
. We denote by
\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta}_{{\rm s\acute{e}t}}
the corresponding site.
A
derived charted log stack
is a sheaf over the site
\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta}_{{\rm s\acute{e}t}}.
Denote by
\mathbf{Shv}^{\Delta}_{{\rm s\acute{e}t}}:=\mathbf{Shv}(\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta,\rm op}_{{\rm s\acute{e}t}},\mathbf{Ani})
the
\infty
-category of derived charted log stacks.

Definition 6.5
.

Let
{\rm Spec}(A,M):\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta}_{{\rm s\acute{e}t}}\rightarrow\mathbf{Ani}
be the functor
{\rm Map}((A,M),-).
We call it the derived charted log-affine scheme associated with
(A,M)
.

Definition 6.6
.

Let
(A,M)
be an animated log ring. The
derived log-affine scheme

{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(A,M)
associated with
(A,M)
is a pair
({\rm Sp\acute{e}t}A,\mathscr{M}),
in which:

1.

{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}A
is the étale spectrum of
A
defined in
[
34
, Section 4.3]
;

2.

\mathscr{M}
is the étale sheafification of the following presheaf

\mathbf{CAlg}^{\Delta}_{A}\ni A^{\prime}\mapsto M^{a},

where
M^{a}
is the animated monoid of the logification of the pre-animated log ring
(A^{\prime},M)
.

Definition 6.7
.

1.

A
derived prelog Deligne-Mumford stack
is a triple
(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{M},\alpha),
where
\mathcal{X}
is a quasi-compact and quasi-separated derived Deligne-Mumford stack,
\mathscr{M}
is an étale sheaf of animated monoids over the underlying
\infty
-topos of
\mathcal{X},
and
\alpha:\mathscr{M}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
is a morphism of animated monoids.

We denote
\mathbf{PreLog}\mathbf{DM}^{\Delta}
as the
\infty
-category of derived log Deligne-Mumford stacks.

2.

A
derived log Deligne-Mumford stack

(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{M},\alpha)
is a derived prelog Deligne-Mumford stack, such that the induced morphism
\mathscr{M}\times_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}}GL_{1}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}})\rightarrow GL_{1}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}})
is an equivalence.

We denote
\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{DM}^{\Delta}
as the
\infty
-category of derived log Deligne-Mumford stacks.

Definition 6.8
.

Let
(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{M},\alpha)
be a derived log Deligne-Mumford stack, we say that
(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{M},\alpha)
is:

1.

Quasi-coherent
, if there is a cover
U\rightarrow\mathcal{X},
such that
U\simeq\coprod_{j}{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}A_{j}
is a finite disjoint union of derived affine schemes, and the restriction
(U,\mathscr{M}|_{U})\simeq\coprod_{j}{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(A_{j},M_{j})
is a finite disjoint union of derived log-affine schemes;

2.

Coherent
, if it’s quasi-coherent, and there is a choice of
\{(A_{j},M_{j})\}
, which appears in the assertion (1), such that for any
j
,
(A_{j},M_{j})
is obtained from an animated prelog ring
(A_{j},M_{j}^{\prime})
satisfying the property that
\mathbb{Z}[M_{j}^{\prime}]
is almost finitely presented over
\mathbb{Z}
.

3.

Fine
, if it’s coherent and there is a choice of
\{(A_{j},M_{j})\}
, which appears in assertion (2), such that for any
j
, the commutative ring
\pi_{0}\mathbb{Z}[M_{j}]=\mathbb{Z}[\pi_{0}M_{j}]
is a finitely generated integral
\mathbb{Z}
-algebra.

Definition 6.9
.

Let
(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{M})
be a derived log Deligne-Mumford stack. We say that
(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{M})
is
n
-
admissible
, if there is an étale cover
\coprod^{N}_{j=1}{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}R_{j}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}
of derived Deligne-Mumford stacks, such that the pullback

\mathscr{M}|_{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}R_{j}}
are given by

n
-admissible
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log rings. We say that it is admissible if it is
n
-admissible for some
n<+\infty
.

Definition 6.10
.

Let
(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})
be a derived log Deligne-Mumford stack. We define the following functor

\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}:\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{DM}^{\rm op}_{/\mathcal{S}}\longrightarrow\mathbf{Ani},

which sends a quasi-compact and quasi-separated derived Deligne-Mumford stack
\mathcal{X}
to the anima consisting of maps of derived log Deligne-Mumford stacks
(\mathcal{X},\mathscr{M})\rightarrow(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L}).

Let
\mu\in\{\rm qcoh,coh,fin\}
, and let
(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})
be a
\mu
-derived log Deligne-Mumford stack.
We denote by

\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}^{\mu,\Delta},\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}^{n-{\rm Adm},\mu,\Delta},{\rm{and}{\ }}\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}^{{\rm Adm},\mu,\Delta}

the subfunctors of
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\Delta}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}
spanned by
\mu
-log structures,
n
-admissible
\mu
-log structures, and admissible
\mu
-log structures, respectively.

The same arguments in
section 4
guaranty that the following facts hold.

Theorem 6.11
.

1.

Let
\mu\in\{\rm qcoh,coh,fin\}
. The functors
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}^{\mu,\Delta}
,
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}^{n-{\rm Adm},\mu,\Delta}
, and
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}^{{\rm Adm},\mu,\Delta}
satisfy descent with respect to the étale topology.

2.

Let
\mu\in\{\rm qcoh,coh,fin\}
. Let
(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})
be a
\mu
-derived log Deligne-Mumford stack. Then
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}^{\mu,\Delta}
admits a
(-1)
-connective cotangent complex.

3.

Let
\mu\in\{\rm coh,fin\}
. Let
(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})
be a
\mu
-derived log Deligne-Mumford stack. Assume that
\mathcal{S}
is covered by derived Grothendieck rings. Then the functor
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}^{n-{\rm Adm},\mu,\Delta}
is derived
(1+n)
-Artin, and
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{(\mathcal{S},\mathscr{L})}^{{\rm Adm},\mu,\Delta}
is locally Artin.

4.

Let
f:{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(A,M)\rightarrow{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(B,N)
be a map induced from the derived log ring map
(B,N)\rightarrow(A,M)
, then there is a canonical equivalence
\mathbb{L}_{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}A/\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\mu,\Delta}_{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(B,N)}}\simeq\mathbb{L}^{G,\Delta}_{(A,M)/(B,N)}.

6.4
Moduli of general log stacks

The functor
\mathcal{F}:\mathbf{SpDM}^{\rm op}\rightarrow\mathbf{Cat}_{\infty}
associated with the
Cartesian projection
\pi:\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{SpDM}\rightarrow\mathbf{SpDM}

extends to a functor

\mathcal{F}^{\prime}:\mathbf{Stk}^{\rm op}\rightarrow\mathbf{Cat}_{\infty}
via right Kan extension along the inclusion
\mathbf{SpDM}\subset\mathbf{Stk}
. Then, passing to the Grothendieck construction, we obtain a Cartesian fibration
\pi:\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{Stk}\rightarrow\mathbf{Stk}
. We refer to the
\infty
-category
\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{Stk}
as the
\infty
-category of log stacks. We say a spectral log stack
X
is quasi-coherent if, for any map
f:{\rm Spec}A\rightarrow X
, the pullback
f^{*}X
is quasi-coherent.

Remark 6.12
.

A quasi-coherent spectral log stack is given by a map
X\rightarrow\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm qcoh}
, where
X
is an stack.

Remark 6.13
.

The subcategory
\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{Stk}^{\rm qcoh}
of
\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{Stk}
consisting of quasi-coherent log stacks is generated by log-affine objects under colimits of strict morphisms.

Example 6.14
.

We have the following examples of log stacks:

1.

The identity map on
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm qcoh}
determines a log structure
\mathscr{M}^{\rm univ}
on
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm qcoh}
. We call this log structure the
universal log structure
on
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm qcoh}
.

2.

Fix an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring
R
. The map
{\rm Spec}R[M]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm qcoh}_{R}
classifying
{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(R[M],M)
, which factors through the quotient stack

{\rm Spec}R[M]/{\rm Spec}R[M^{\rm gp}]\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm qcoh}_{R}.

Indeed, the composition
{\rm Spec}R[M^{\rm gp}]\rightarrow{\rm Spec}R[M]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{R}^{\rm qcoh}
classifies a trivial log structure, as the logification of the
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-prelog ring
(R[M^{\rm gp}],M)
agrees with
(R[M^{\rm gp}],GL_{1}(R[M^{\rm gp}])
, and we have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces...}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm Spec}R[M]\times{\rm Spec}R[M^{\rm gp}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm Spec}R[M]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm qcoh}_{R}}

Passing to the colimit, we get the desired map
{\rm Spec}R[M]/{\rm Spec}R[M^{\rm gp}]\rightarrow\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm qcoh}_{R}
.
This also defines a log structure on
{\rm Spec}R[M]/{\rm Spec}R[M^{\rm gp}]
.

3.

Let
{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(A,M)
be a log-affine spectral log scheme, where
A
is

p
-complete. Then the natural inclusion
{\rm Spf}A\rightarrow{\rm Spec}A
defines a log structure on
{\rm Spf}A
. This coincides with the classical definition of log formal schemes in
[
30
, Appendix A]
.

Let
S
be a quasi-coherent spectral log stack. one can define the functor
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm qcoh}_{S}
as the following colimit

\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm qcoh}_{S}:={\rm colim}_{(\mathcal{U},f)}\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm qcoh}_{f^{*}X},

where the index
(\mathcal{U},f)
runs over the
\infty
-category of spectral Deligne-Mumford stacks lying over
\underline{S}
, and the colimit is formed in the
\infty
-category
\mathbf{Stk}
. Then we obtain a natural map
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm qcoh}_{S}\rightarrow\underline{S}
that classifies quasi-coherent log stacks lying over
S
.

Remark 6.15

(Representability for general bases)
.

Let
S\in\mathbf{Stk}
be an arbitrary stack. We define
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{S}
as the colimit
{\rm colim}_{\mathcal{U}}\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\mu}_{\mathcal{U}}
. We have natural equivalences
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\mu}_{\mathcal{U}}\simeq\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\mu}\times\mathcal{U}
for all
\mathcal{U}\in\mathbf{SpDM}
by definition, and therefore we have
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\mu}_{S}\simeq\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\mu}\times S
. In particular, the stack
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm Adm}_{S}
is locally Artin over
S
, and relatively of finitely almost presented.

7
(
p
-adic)
\infty
-root stacks

The aim of this section is to develop a derived version of the theory of
\infty
-root stacks, as in
[
16
]
and
[
54
]
. Our construction generalizes the original definition of
\infty
-root stacks to all quasi-coherent stacks and coincides with the definition of classical ones in certain cases, as provided by Remark
7.6
and Proposition
7.9
.

In this section, we will first give the definition of
\infty
-root stacks, and then we will study the associated geometric and functorial properties. Our main result is Theorem
7.27
, which states that the
p
-completed cotangent complexes of certain quasi-coherent spectral log Deligne-Mumford stacks could be recovered from those of associated
\infty
-root stacks. This generalizes the result of Binda-Lundemo-Merici-Park in
[
12
, Theorem 4.12]
and
[
13
, Lemma 3.18]
to the quasi-coherent case.

All constructions and results in this section are suitable for both
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
- and derived contexts.

Convention 7.1
.

Throughout this section, we will work with the
fpqc topology
. All stacks are assumed to satisfy fpqc descent, and all operations are performed in the
\infty
-category
\mathbf{Stk}
of fpqc stacks. In particular, we denote by
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{S}^{\rm qcoh}
the moduli stack of quasi-coherent log structures defined using the fpqc topology, which
is an fpqc sheaf by Remark
4.49
.

7.1
Definitions and basic geometric properties

For simplicity, throughout this section, we fix a prime number
p
, and a
p
-complete
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring
R
. We work with the stack
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{R}^{\rm qcoh}
, and its
p
-adic completion
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{R}^{\wedge}=\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}^{\rm qcoh}_{R}\times_{{\rm Spec}R}{\rm Spf}R
.

Definition 7.2
.

Denote by
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{R}^{\wedge}
the fiber product
\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{R}^{\rm qcoh}\times_{{\rm Spec}R}{\rm Spf}R
. The
universal (
p
-typical)
\infty
-root stack

\sqrt[\infty]{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{R}^{\wedge}}
is defined as the inverse limit of the following sequence

...\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{R}^{\wedge}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{R}^{\wedge}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}...\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{R}^{\wedge}.

The
(
p
-typical)
\infty
-root stack

\sqrt[\infty]{(X,\mathscr{M})}
of a quasi-coherent spectral log stack
(X,\mathscr{M})
is the fiber product
X\times_{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{R}^{\wedge}}\sqrt[\infty]{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{R}^{\wedge}}
. Similarly, one can define the
n
-th root stack

\sqrt[n]{(X,\mathscr{M})}
as the following fiber product

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\sqrt[n]{(X,\mathscr{M})}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{R}^{\wedge}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{R}^{\wedge}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{n}}

We have
\sqrt[\infty]{(X,\mathscr{M})}\simeq\varprojlim_{n}\sqrt[n]{(X,\mathscr{M})}
.

Example 7.3
.

If
X
is a spectral log stack with trivial log structure, then
\sqrt[\infty]{X}\simeq X
.

Remark 7.4
.

Let
(X,\mathscr{M})
be a quasi-coherent spectral log stack. We denote by
(X,p^{n}\mathscr{M})
the spectral log stack which is classified by the map

X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathscr{M}}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{R}^{\wedge}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle p^{n}}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{R}^{\wedge}
. This gives rise to a concrete description of the
\infty
-root stack
\sqrt[\infty]{(X,\mathscr{M})}
. The mapping space
{\rm Map}(U,\sqrt[\infty]{(X,\mathscr{M})})
from a stack
U

is equivalent to the anima consisting of maps in
\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{Stk}
as follows

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(U,\mathscr{N})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(U,p\mathscr{N})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(U,p^{2}\mathscr{N})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces...}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(U,p^{n}\mathscr{N}_{2})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces...}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(X,\mathscr{M})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(X,\mathscr{M})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(X,\mathscr{M})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces...}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(X,\mathscr{M})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces...}

such that the left vertical map
(U,\mathscr{N})\rightarrow(X,\mathscr{M})
is strict.

7.1.1
Basic properties

By the definition of
\sqrt[\infty]{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{R}^{\wedge}}
, it admits a surjective map from the union of all

{{\rm Spf}R[M[1/p]]^{\wedge}_{p}}

where
M
runs over all
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-monoids. Indeed, if we have a map
{\rm Spec}A\rightarrow\sqrt[\infty]{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{R}^{\wedge}}
that is lying over
{\rm Spf}R[M]^{\wedge}_{p}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{R}^{\wedge}
, this is equivalent to providing a map
{\rm Spec}A\rightarrow{\rm Spf}R[M]^{\wedge}_{p}\times_{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{R}^{\wedge}}\sqrt[\infty]{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{R}^{\wedge}}\simeq\sqrt[\infty]{{\rm Spf}R[M]^{\wedge}_{p}}
. Unwinding the definition of
\infty
-root stacks, there is a sequence of fpqc maps of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings

A\longrightarrow A_{1}\longrightarrow A_{2}\longrightarrow...\longrightarrow A_{n}\longrightarrow...,

together with a map of the projective system of stacks

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces...}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Spec}A_{n+1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Spec}A_{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Spec}A_{n-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces...}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces...}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{R}^{\wedge}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{R}^{\wedge}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{R}^{\wedge}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces...}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

such that each vertical map classifies a log-affine log structure
{\rm Spec}A_{n}\rightarrow{\rm Spf}R[1/p^{n}M]^{\wedge}_{p}
, and is compatible with each other. Passing to the limit, we have a map
{\rm Spec}A_{\infty}:={\rm Spec}(\varinjlim_{n}A_{n})\rightarrow{\rm Spf}R[M[1/p]]^{\wedge}_{p}
. As faithfully flat algebras are stable under sequential colimits,
{\rm Spec}A_{\infty}\rightarrow{\rm Spec}A
is a flat cover, and we obtain the desired result.

Proposition 7.5
.

If
R
is discrete with bounded
p^{\infty}
-torsions, then
\sqrt[\infty]{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{R}^{\wedge}}
is classical
14
14

14

We say a stack
X
is classical if it belongs to the essential image of the fully faithful embedding

i_{!}:\mathbf{Shv}_{\rm fpqc}(\mathbf{CAlg}^{\heartsuit},\mathbf{Ani})\rightarrow\mathbf{Stk}=\mathbf{Shv}_{\rm fpqc}(\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn},\mathbf{Ani}).

, and the natural map

\iota:\sqrt[\infty]{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{R}^{\wedge,\heartsuit}}\longrightarrow\sqrt[\infty]{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{R}^{\wedge}}

is an equivalence.

Proof.

By the argument above, we can form a cover of stacks

\pi:\coprod_{M}{\rm Spf}R[M[1/p]]^{\wedge}_{p}\longrightarrow\sqrt[\infty]{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{R}^{\wedge}}.

The
[
2
, Corollary 2.10]
guaranties that the
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring
R[M[1/p]]^{\wedge}_{p}\simeq R[\pi_{0}M[1/p]]^{\wedge}_{p}
is discrete. This holds because
R
has bounded
p^{\infty}
-torsion and then follows from
[
8
, Lemma 4.7]
. Consider the subcategory
\mathbf{FMon}_{R}
of
\mathbf{Stk}_{/\sqrt[\infty]{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{R}^{\wedge}}}
spanned by such canonical maps
\chi_{M}:{\rm Spf}R[\pi_{0}M[1/p]]_{p}^{\wedge}\rightarrow\sqrt[\infty]{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{R}^{\wedge}}
. The map
\chi_{M}
has a canonical factorization

{\rm Spf}R[\pi_{0}M[1/p]]_{p}^{\wedge}\longrightarrow\sqrt[\infty]{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{R}^{\wedge,\heartsuit}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\iota}}{{\longrightarrow}}\sqrt[\infty]{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{R}^{\wedge}}.

Passing to the colimit, we have the

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm colim}_{\mathbf{FMon}_{R}}{\rm Spf}R[\pi_{0}M[1/p]]_{p}^{\wedge}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\sqrt[\infty]{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{R}^{\wedge,\heartsuit}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sqrt[\infty]{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{R}^{\wedge}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}

This follows that
\sqrt[\infty]{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{R}^{\wedge}}
is classical, and the map
\iota
is surjective. Since both sides of
\iota
are classical stacks, the equivalence of
\iota
is tested by discrete rings. Then it follows.
∎

Remark 7.6

(Comparing with classical
\infty
-root stacks)
.

If
(A,M)^{a}
is a classical fine and saturated log ring, where
M
is a fine and saturated monoid. Assuming that
A
is a
p
-complete algebra over
R
, and
R[M]\rightarrow A
is
p
-completely faithfully flat (this is equivalent to the induced map
{\rm Spf}A\rightarrow\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{R}^{\wedge,\heartsuit}
being
p
-completely flat, see
[
45
, Corollary 5.31]
). Then our definition of
\infty
-root stack
\sqrt[\infty]{{\rm Spf}(A,M)}
coincides with the classical definition, using the local description of classical
\infty
-root stacks established in
[
54
, Section 3]
or Proposition
7.9
. See also
[
22
, Section 13]
and
[
13
, Section 3.10]
.

7.1.2
\infty
-root stacks of toric algebras

Let
M
be an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-monoid, the toric log structure on
{\rm Spf}R[M]^{\wedge}_{p}
is the log structure induced from the unit map
\alpha:M\rightarrow\Omega^{\infty}R[M]
. We denote by
\mathbb{A}^{\rm Log}_{M}
the resulting spectral log formal scheme, and refer to
\mathbb{A}_{M}
as the underlying spectral formal scheme of it. We also consider the perfected spectral toric formal scheme

\mathbb{A}_{M}^{\infty}:=\varprojlim_{n}{\rm Spf}R[1/p^{n}M]^{\wedge}_{p}\simeq{\rm Spf}R[M[1/p]]^{\wedge}_{p}\simeq{\rm Spf}R[\pi_{0}M[1/p]]^{\wedge}_{p}

of
\mathbb{A}_{M}
. The map
\alpha
also defines a log structure on
\mathbb{A}_{M}^{\infty}
, and we let
\mathbb{A}_{M}^{\infty,{\rm Log}}
be the resulting spectral log formal scheme.

Remark 7.7
.

We observe that if
\pi_{0}M\simeq\pi_{0}N
, then
\mathbb{A}_{M}^{\infty}\simeq\mathbb{A}^{\infty}_{N}
. In particular, we have

\mathbb{A}^{1,\infty}_{{\rm Spf}R}\simeq\mathbb{A}^{1,\flat,\infty}_{{\rm Spf}R}
. Here
\mathbb{A}_{{\rm Spf}R}^{1,\flat}
is the flat affine line
\mathbb{A}^{1,\flat}\times{\rm Spf}R
over
{\rm Spf}R
.

The functor
{\rm Map}_{\mathbf{CMon}}(M,GL_{1}(-)):\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}_{R}\rightarrow\mathbf{CMon}^{\rm gp},A\mapsto{\rm Map}(M,GL_{1}(A))
is an fpqc hypersheaf of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-groups over
R
. Let
n
be a natural number, and let
\mu^{M}_{p^{n}}
be the fiber of the
p^{n}
-th power self-map of
{\rm Map}_{\mathbf{CMon}}(M,GL_{1}(-))
. Thus, it’s an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-group stack over
R
. Let
\mu^{M}_{p^{\infty}}:=\varprojlim_{n}\mu^{M}_{p^{n}}
.

Remark 7.8
.

We observe that
\mu^{\coprod_{n\geq 0}B\Sigma_{n}}_{p^{n}}\simeq\mu_{p^{n}}={\rm Spf}R\{x\}^{\wedge}_{p}/(x^{p^{n}}-1)
is a spectral group formal scheme. As the functor
\mathbf{CMon}^{\rm op}\rightarrow\mathbf{Stk}_{/{\rm Spf}R},M\mapsto\mu^{M}_{p^{n}}
is limit-preserving, and
\mathbf{CMon}
is generated by
\coprod_{n\geq 0}B\Sigma_{n}
under colimits,
\mu^{M}_{p^{n}}
is an affine
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-group formal scheme.

One can show that
\mu^{M}_{p^{n}}\simeq{\rm Spf}R[(1/p^{n}M)^{\rm gp}/M^{\rm gp}]^{\wedge}_{p}
and
\mu^{M}_{p^{\infty}}\simeq{\rm Spf}R[M[1/p]^{\rm gp}/M^{\rm gp}]^{\wedge}_{p}
.

Proposition 7.9
.

Let
V={\rm Spf}R[M]^{\wedge}_{p}
be a toric spectral formal scheme equipped with the toric log structure, and let
V^{\infty}
be
{\rm Spf}R[M[1/p]]^{\wedge}_{p}
. Then the map
\pi:V^{\infty}\rightarrow V
admits a functorial lifting
\pi^{\infty}:V^{\infty}\rightarrow\sqrt[\infty]{V}
. If we equip
V^{\infty}
with the natural
\mu_{p^{\infty}}
-action and equip
\sqrt[\infty]{V}
with the trivial
\mu^{M}_{p^{\infty}}
-action,
the map
\pi^{\infty}
is
\mu^{M}_{p^{\infty}}
-equivariant, and the induced map
V^{\infty}/{\mu^{M}_{p^{\infty}}}\rightarrow\sqrt[\infty]{V}
is an equivalence.

Proof.

Let
A
be an algebra lying over
{\rm Spf}R
. Unwinding the definition of
\infty
-root stacks, after a suitable flat localization of
A
, a section
s=(f_{0},\{f_{n}\})\in\sqrt[\infty]{V}(A)\simeq V(A)\times_{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{R}^{\wedge}(A)}\sqrt[\infty]{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{R}^{\wedge}}(A)
is given by a collection of
R
-linear
p
-complete maps
f_{n}:R[1/p^{n}M]^{\wedge}_{p}\rightarrow A
,
n=1,2,...,\infty
, which are compatible with transition maps

R[M]^{\wedge}_{p}\hookrightarrow R[1/pM]]^{\wedge}_{p}\hookrightarrow...\hookrightarrow R[1/p^{n}M]^{\wedge}_{p}\hookrightarrow...

Taking the limit, we get a map
f_{\infty}:=\lim_{m}f_{n}:R[M[1/p]]^{\wedge}_{p}\rightarrow A
. In this case, we refer to
f_{\infty}
as an
\infty
-root of
f_{0}
.
We summarize it as the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Spec}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\sqrt[\infty]{V}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sqrt[\infty]{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{R}^{\wedge}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{R}^{\wedge}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{f_{n}\}}

The limit map
f_{\infty}
