determines a factorization

s:{\rm Spec}A\stackrel{{\scriptstyle f_{\infty}}}{{\longrightarrow}}V^{\infty}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi^{\infty}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\sqrt[\infty]{V}

of
s
, lying over
V
. Note that the definition of
\pi^{\infty}
is independent of the choice of the
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring
A
and the map
s
. The map
\pi^{\infty}
is a surjective map of fpqc stacks. It’s easy to see that
\pi^{\infty}
is
\mu^{M}_{p^{\infty}}
-equivariant. To show that it’s a quotient map, we consider its fiber over an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring
B
lying over
{\rm Spf}R
. Let
t\in\sqrt[\infty]{V}(B)
, which corresponds to a pair
(g_{0}:R[M]^{\wedge}_{p}\rightarrow B,\{g_{n}:R[1/p^{n}M]^{\wedge}_{p}\rightarrow B\})
. Denote by
g_{\infty}:=\lim_{n}g_{n}
the associated
\infty
-root of
g_{0}
. We observe that the fiber of
\pi^{\infty}
lying over
t
is equivalent to the following animae

\displaystyle\varprojlim_{n}{\rm fib}({\rm Map}_{R}(R[1/p^{n}M]^{\wedge}_{p},B)\longrightarrow{\rm Map}_{R}(R[M]^{\wedge}_{p},B))

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle{\rm fib}(\varprojlim_{n}{\rm Map}_{R}(R[1/p^{n}M]^{\wedge}_{p},B)\longrightarrow{\rm Map}_{R}(R[M]^{\wedge}_{p},B))

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle{\rm fib}({\rm Map}_{R}(R[M[1/p]]^{\wedge}_{p},B)\longrightarrow{\rm Map}_{R}(R[M]^{\wedge}_{p},B))

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle{\rm fib}({\rm Map}_{\mathbf{CMon}}(M[1/p],\Omega^{\infty}B)\longrightarrow{\rm Map}_{\mathbf{CMon}}(M,\Omega^{\infty}B)).

In other words, it’s equivalent to
\mu^{M}_{p^{\infty}}(B)
.
∎

7.2
Functoriality of
\infty
-root stacks

We will prove that the construction of
\infty
-root stacks is functorial in this subsection.

Theorem 7.10
.

There is a limit-preserving functor

\gamma:\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{Stk}_{/{\rm Spf}R}\longrightarrow\mathbf{Stk}_{/{\rm Spf}R},

such that for any quasi-coherent spectral log stack
X
, the stack
\gamma(X)
coincides with the
\infty
-root stack
\sqrt[\infty]{X}
of
X.

We will give a proof of Theorem
7.10
later.

Let
A
be an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebra lying over
{\rm Spf}R
. For any
n\geq 0
, the
p
-th power map
p^{n}\Omega^{\infty}A\rightarrow\Omega^{\infty}A
gives rise to another
p
-complete
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-prelog ring
(A,p^{n}\Omega^{\infty}A)
. Then we can form the following sequence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces...}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(A,p^{n}\Omega^{\infty}A)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(A,p^{n-1}\Omega^{\infty}A)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces...}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(A,p\Omega^{\infty}A)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(A,\Omega^{\infty}A)}}

Passing to the corresponding spectral log schemes, we define:

({\rm Sp\acute{e}t}A)^{\sharp}:={\rm colim}_{n\geq 0}{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(A,p^{n}\Omega^{\infty}A).

This defines a functor

(-)_{0}^{\sharp}:\mathbf{CAlg}^{{\rm cn,op}}_{/{\rm Spf}R}\longrightarrow\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{Stk}_{/{\rm Spf}R}.

Remark 7.11
.

We define
p^{\infty}\Omega^{\infty}A
as the limit
\lim_{n}p^{n}\Omega^{\infty}A
. We have a natural map
({\rm Sp\acute{e}t}A)^{\sharp}\rightarrow{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(A,p^{\infty}\Omega^{\infty}A)
. This map is not an isomorphism in general. For example, let
A=\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. The log structure of the log stack
{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(\mathbb{Z}_{p},p^{\infty}\mathbb{N})
is trivial, while
({\rm Sp\acute{e}t}\mathbb{Z}_{p})^{\sharp}
is not quasi-coherent.

Taking the left Kan extension of
(-)^{\sharp}
along the Yoneda inclusion
{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(-):\mathbf{CAlg}^{{\rm cn,op}}_{/{\rm Spf}R}\rightarrow\mathbf{Stk}_{/{\rm Spf}R}
, we have the following functor

(-)^{\sharp}:\mathbf{Stk}_{/{\rm Spf}R}\longrightarrow\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{Stk}_{/{\rm Spf}R}.

It’s easy to see that we have the following result.

Lemma 7.12
.

The functor
(-)^{\sharp}
preserves colimits.

Proof.

Taking the left Kan extension of
(-)_{0}^{\sharp}:\mathbf{CAlg}_{/{\rm Spf}R}^{\rm cn,op}\rightarrow\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{Stk}_{/{\rm Spf}R}
along the Yoneda inclusion
\mathbf{CAlg}_{/{\rm Spf}R}^{\rm cn}\rightarrow{\rm Fun}(\mathbf{CAlg}_{/{\rm Spf}R}^{\rm cn},\mathbf{Ani})
, we get a colimit-preserving functor
(-)_{1}^{\sharp}:{\rm Fun}(\mathbf{CAlg}_{/{\rm Spf}R}^{\rm cn},\mathbf{Ani})\rightarrow\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{Stk}_{/{\rm Spf}R}
, and
(-)^{\sharp}
is equivalent to the restriction of
(-)_{1}^{\sharp}
on its subcategory of sheaf objects. Applying
[
38
, Proposition 1.3.1.7]
, we need to show that the functor
(-)_{0}^{\sharp}
has fpqc codescent, which is obvious.
∎

Proof of Theorem
7.10
.

As
(-)^{\sharp}
is colimit-preserving, it’s right adjointable. We denote by
\gamma:\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{Stk}_{/{\rm Spf}R}\rightarrow\mathbf{Stk}_{/{\rm Spf}R}
its right adjunction. Let
(X,\mathscr{N})
be a quasi-coherentspectral log stack lying over
{\rm Spf}R
. We have the following equivalences

\displaystyle{\rm Map}_{\mathbf{Stk}_{/{\rm Spf}R}}({\rm Sp\acute{e}t}A,\gamma(X,\mathscr{N}))

\displaystyle\simeq{\rm Map}_{\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{Stk}_{/{\rm Spf}R}}(({\rm Sp\acute{e}t}A)^{\sharp},(X,\mathscr{N}))

\displaystyle\simeq\varprojlim_{n}{\rm Map}_{\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{Stk}_{/{\rm Spf}R}}({\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(A,p^{n}\Omega^{\infty}A),(X,\mathscr{N})).

The mapping space

\lim_{n}{\rm Map}_{\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{Stk}_{/{\rm Spf}R}}({\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(A,p^{n}\Omega^{\infty}A),(X,\mathscr{N}))
is equivalent to the anima consisting of the following diagrams in
\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{Stk}_{/{\rm Spf}R}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(X,\mathscr{N})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces...}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(A,p^{n}\Omega^{\infty}\mathcal{O}_{A})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces...}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(A,p\Omega^{\infty}\mathcal{O}_{A})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(A,\Omega^{\infty}\mathcal{O}_{A})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{0}}

Here we use the notation given in Remark
7.4
.
This coincides with the anima
\sqrt[\infty]{(X,\mathscr{N})}(A)
. In particular, if
\mathscr{N}\simeq GL_{1}(\mathcal{O}_{X})
, then
\gamma(X,\mathscr{N})\simeq X.

We then have to show that there is a morphism
\sqrt[\infty]{(X,\mathscr{N})}\rightarrow\gamma(X,\mathscr{N})
, which gives the above equivalence. Dually, we will construct a map
\sqrt[\infty]{(X,\mathscr{N})}^{\sharp}\rightarrow X
. Unwinding the definition of
\infty
-root stacks, there is an equivalence

\sqrt[\infty]{(X,\mathscr{N})}^{\sharp}\simeq{\rm colim}_{{\rm Spf}C}{{\rm Spf}C}^{\sharp}
, where
{\rm Spf}C
runs over all formal log-affine objects lying over
\sqrt[\infty]{(X,\mathscr{N})}
. So there is an equivalence

{\rm Map}({\sqrt[\infty]{(X,\mathscr{N})}},\gamma((X,\mathscr{N})))\simeq\lim{\rm Map}({{{\rm Spf}C}},\gamma(X,\mathscr{N}))\simeq\lim{\rm Map}({({{\rm Spf}C})}^{\sharp},(X,\mathscr{N})).

Denote by
\eta:\sqrt[\infty]{(X,\mathscr{N})}\rightarrow X
.
Then by the definition of the functor
(-)^{\sharp}
, a commutative diagram of log stacks as follows

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces...}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{({\sqrt[\infty]{(X,\mathscr{N})}},p^{2}\Omega^{\infty}\mathcal{O}_{\sqrt[\infty]{(X,\mathscr{N})}})^{a}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{({\sqrt[\infty]{(X,\mathscr{N})}},p\Omega^{\infty}\mathcal{O}_{\sqrt[\infty]{(X,\mathscr{N})}})^{a}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{({\sqrt[\infty]{(X,\mathscr{N})}},\Omega^{\infty}\mathcal{O}_{\sqrt[\infty]{(X,\mathscr{N})}})^{a}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(X,\mathscr{N})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta}

defines a map
\sqrt[\infty]{(X,\mathscr{N})}^{\sharp}\rightarrow X
. Then the induced map
\eta:{\sqrt[\infty]{(X,\mathscr{N})}}\rightarrow{\sqrt[\infty]{(X,\mathscr{N})}}
gives such a map by the definition of
\infty
-root stacks.
∎

7.3
Topologically saturated
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-monoids and log structures

Recall that a discrete monoid
M\in\mathbf{CMon}^{\heartsuit}
is called
p
-saturated if, for any
x\in M^{\rm gp}
, if there is a non-negative integer
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, such that
p^{n}x
lies in the image of the group-completion map
M\rightarrow M^{\rm gp}
, then
x\in M
. In other words, the natural map
M\rightarrow M[1/p]\times_{M[1/p]^{\rm gp}}M^{\rm gp}
is an isomorphism of monoids, where the fiber product is formed in
\mathbf{CMon}^{\heartsuit}
. In practice, this condition is required in almost all concrete applications in arithmetic and geometry. In this subsection, we give its topological generalization.

Definition 7.13
.

let
M
be an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-monoid. Consider the map
a_{M}:M[1/p]\rightarrow M[1/p]\times(M[1/p]^{\rm gp}/M^{\rm gp})
induced from the diagonal map of
M
, and we regard it as the natural coaction of
M[1/p]^{\rm gp}/M^{\rm gp}
on
M[1/p]
. Form the following cosimplicial diagram
C_{M}^{\bullet}
induced from the coaction
a_{M}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M[1/p]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{M[1/p]\times(M[1/p]^{\rm gp}/M^{\rm gp})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{M[1/p]\times(M[1/p]^{\rm gp}/M^{\rm gp})^{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces...}

We say that
M
is topologically
p
-saturated if

1.

The natural map

M\rightarrow\lim_{\bullet\in\Delta}C^{\bullet}_{M}
is an equivalence.

2.

The kernel
K
of
\pi_{0}M\rightarrow\pi_{0}M[1/p]\simeq\pi_{0}M_{\rm perf}
is bounded, i.e., there is an integer
N
big enough, such that
p^{N}K=0
.

Our main result of this subsection is the following.

Theorem 7.14
.

Let
R
be a
p
-complete
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring lying over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, and let
(A,M)
be a
p
-complete
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log ring. If
M
is topologically
p
-saturated, then
the natural map

\theta:(A,M)\longrightarrow\lim_{\bullet\in\Delta}(R[C^{\bullet}_{M}]^{\wedge}_{p},C^{\bullet}_{M})^{a}\otimes^{\wedge}_{R[M]^{\wedge}_{p}}A

is an equivalence.

We will give the proof of Theorem
7.14
later.

Lemma 7.15
.

We say a map of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring
f:R\rightarrow A
has descent, if
R
is equivalent to the totalization of the Čech conerve of
f
. Give a sequence of maps
R\rightarrow A\rightarrow B
. Assume that both
R\rightarrow A
and
A\rightarrow B
have descent, and for any map
A\rightarrow C
, the map
C\rightarrow B\otimes_{A}C
has descent, then
R\rightarrow B
has descent.

Proof.

We denote by

C^{\bullet}_{A/R},C^{\bullet}_{B/R},C^{\bullet}_{B/A}

the Čech conerves of the maps
R\rightarrow A
,
R\rightarrow B
and
A\rightarrow B
respectively. Consider the map
A\rightarrow C^{n}_{A/R}
, and its pushout
C^{n}_{A/R}\rightarrow B\otimes_{A}C^{n}_{A/R}

along the map
A\rightarrow B
.
Wwe have the following pushout square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{n}_{A/R}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B\otimes_{A}C^{n}_{A/R}}

Denote by
C^{\bullet,n}
the Čech conerve of this map, and therefore we get a bi-cosimplicial object
\Delta\times\Delta\ni(n,m)\mapsto C^{n,m}
. Note that the diagonal of
C^{\bullet,\bullet}
is equivalent to
C^{\bullet}_{B/R}
. Thus we have that

\lim_{\bullet\in\Delta}C^{\bullet}_{B/R}\simeq\lim_{(p,q)\in\Delta\times\Delta}C^{p,q}\simeq\lim_{p\in\Delta}\lim_{q\in\Delta}C^{p,q}.

As the map
C^{n}_{A/R}\rightarrow B\otimes_{A}C^{n}_{A/R}
has descent, we know that

\lim_{p\in\Delta}\lim_{q\in\Delta}C^{p,q}\simeq\lim_{p\in\Delta}C^{p}_{A/R}\simeq R.

∎

Lemma 7.16
.

For any
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-monoid, the map
R[M]\rightarrow R[\pi_{0}M]
has descent.

Proof.

This is immediately deduced from Dundas and Rognes’ theorem of cosimplicial descent
[
23
, Theorem 1.2]
.
∎

Lemma 7.17
.

Let
M
be a discrete
p
-saturated commutative monoid. Then for any map
R[M]\rightarrow A
, the map

A\rightarrow A\otimes_{R[M]}R[M[1/p]]

has descent.

Proof.

We have factorization
R[M]\rightarrow R[M^{\prime}]\rightarrow R[M[1/p]]
, where
M^{\prime}=M/K
, and we have
M[1/p]\simeq M^{\prime}[1/p]
. Applying
[
41
, Proposition 3.27]
, without loss of generality, we assume that
M\rightarrow M[1/p]
is injective.
We observe that
M[1/p]/M
is group-like. Indeed, for any
x\in\pi_{0}(M[1/p]/M)=\pi_{0}M[1/p]/\pi_{0}M
, there is
p^{n}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, such that
p^{n}x=0
, then
(p^{n}-1)x
is the inverse of
x
. This means that
M[1/p]/M\simeq M[1/p]^{\rm gp}/M^{\rm gp}
. It then follows that the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{M[1/p]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{M[1/p]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{M[1/p]\times(M[1/p]^{\rm gp}/M^{\rm gp})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{M}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({\rm id},0)}

is a pushout square. Applying
[
35
, Proposition 6.1.2.11]
, the diagram
M\rightarrow C^{\bullet}_{M}
is a left Kan extension of
M\rightarrow M[1/p]
along the inclusion
\Delta_{+}^{\leq 0}\rightarrow\Delta_{+}
. Then we reduce to the case that
R\simeq\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, and
A=\mathbb{Z}_{p}[M]
. This is
[
43
, Lemma 3.28]
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
7.14
.

Applying
[
2
, Corollary 2.1]
, Lemma
7.15
, Lemma
7.16
and Lemma
7.17
, we get the desired result.
∎

Remark 7.18
.

By the proof of Theorem
7.14
, we see that for any
p
-complete and almost connective module
J
over
R[M]^{\wedge}_{p}
, the following natural map

J\longrightarrow\lim_{\Delta}R[C^{\bullet}_{M}]^{\wedge}_{p}\otimes^{\wedge}_{R[M]^{\wedge}_{p}}J

is an equivalence.

Definition 7.19
.

An
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-log ring
(A,M)
is called topologically
p
-saturated if
M
is so.

A quasi-coherent spectral log stack
X
is called topologically
p
-saturated if, for any map
f:{\rm Spec}A\rightarrow\underline{X}
, the pullback
f^{*}X
is fpqc locally equivalent to
{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(A,M)
for some
(A,M)
, where
(A,M)
is topologically
p
-saturated.

7.4
Cotangent complexes via
\infty
-root stacks

In this section, we will prove that the
p
-completion of the log cotangent complex of a topologically
p
-saturated quasi-coherent
spectral log stack over
{\rm Spec}R
, can be recovered from its
\infty
-root stack.

Lemma 7.20
.

Fix a stack
S
. Let
\mathcal{C}\rightarrow{\rm Fun}(\Delta^{1},\mathbf{Stk})_{/S}
be a diagram of stacks lying over
S
. Assuming that for any
i\in\mathcal{C}
, the relative stack
X_{i}/S
admits a cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{X_{i}/S}
. If all
\mathbb{L}_{X_{i}/S}
are uniformly bounded below, then the limit
X:=\lim_{i\in\mathcal{C}}X_{i}\rightarrow S
admits a cotangent complex, and the following natural map

{\rm colim}_{i\in\mathcal{C}}f_{i}^{*}\mathbb{L}_{X_{i}/S}\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}_{X/S}

is an equivalence. Here
f_{i}:X\rightarrow X_{i}
is the canonical map induced from the limit.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we might as well assume that
S={\rm Spec}A
, and thus we work with the
\infty
-category
\mathbf{Shv}_{{\rm\acute{e}t}}(\mathbf{CAlg}^{\rm cn}_{A},\mathbf{Ani})\simeq\mathbf{Stk}_{/{\rm Spec}A}
. Let
B
be an
A
-algebra, and
I
be a connective
B
-module. As each
X_{i}
admits a cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{X_{i}/A}
, we have that the fiber of the map
X(B\oplus I)\rightarrow X(B)
over a point
x:{\rm Spec}B\rightarrow X
is equivalent to

\displaystyle\lim{\rm Map}_{B}(x^{*}f_{i}^{*}\mathbb{L}_{X_{i}/A},I)

\displaystyle\simeq{\rm Map}_{B}({\rm colim}x^{*}f_{i}^{*}\mathbb{L}_{X_{i}/A},I)

\displaystyle\simeq{\rm Map}_{B}(x^{*}{\rm colim}f^{*}_{i}\mathbb{L}_{X_{i}/A},I)

Applying the lower bound assumption of
\{\mathbb{L}_{X_{i}/S}\}_{i\in\mathcal{C}}
,
{\rm colim}f^{*}_{i}\mathbb{L}_{X_{i}/A}
is almost connective. Therefore, it’s a cotangent complex of
X/A
.
∎

We have the following corollary.

Corollary 7.21
.

The relative stack
\eta:\sqrt[\infty]{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{R}^{\wedge}}\rightarrow{\rm Spf}R
admits a cotangent complex.

Lemma 7.22
.

Let
A
be a
p
-complete
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebra over
R
, then the associated map
f:{\rm Spf}A\rightarrow{\rm Spf}R
admits a cotangent complex, which is equivalent to
(\mathbb{L}_{A/R})^{\wedge}_{p}\simeq\varprojlim_{n}\mathbb{L}_{A/R}/p^{n}
.

Proof.

We observe that
{\rm Spf}A\simeq{\rm Spec}A\times_{{\rm Spec}R}{\rm Spf}R
. It follows that
f
admits a cotangent complex. As
{\rm Spf}A\simeq{\rm colim}_{n}{\rm Spec}A/p^{n}
, we have

\mathbb{L}_{{\rm Spf}A/{\rm Spf}R}\simeq\varprojlim_{n}\mathbb{L}_{A/R}|_{{\rm Spec}A/p^{n}}\simeq\varprojlim_{n}\mathbb{L}_{A/R}/p^{n}.

∎

Lemma 7.23
.

For any
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-monoid
M
having the form
M=N[1/p]
, the
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-algebra
R\rightarrow R[M]^{\wedge}_{p}
is
p
-completely formally étale, i.e.,
(\mathbb{L}_{R[M]^{\wedge}_{p}/R})^{\wedge}_{p}
is contractible.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we replace
R
by the
p
-adic sphere spectrum
\mathbb{S}_{p}
, as we have an equivalence
{\rm Spf}R[M]^{\wedge}_{p}\simeq{\rm Spf}R\times_{{\rm Spf}\mathbb{S}_{p}}{\rm Spf}\mathbb{S}_{p}[M]^{\wedge}_{p}
. Applying
[
2
, Corollary 2.10]
, we can also assume that
M
is discrete. As

(\mathbb{L}_{\mathbb{S}_{p}[M]^{\wedge}_{p}/\mathbb{S}_{p}})^{\wedge}_{p}
is
p
-complete, it suffices to show that
(\mathbb{L}_{\mathbb{S}_{p}[M]^{\wedge}_{p}/\mathbb{S}_{p}})^{\wedge}_{p}/p\simeq\mathbb{L}_{\mathbb{F}_{p}[M]/\mathbb{F}_{p}}\simeq 0
, while
\mathbb{F}_{p}[M]
is a perfect
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-algebra by assumption.
∎

Lemma 7.24
.

Let
R
be an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring or an animated ring, regard the topological (resp. algebraic ) cotangent complex as a functor
\mathbb{L}^{(\Delta)}_{(-)/R}:\mathbf{CAlg}_{R}\rightarrow\mathbf{Mod}_{R}
. Then, one has:

1.

In the animated setting,
\mathbb{L}^{\Delta}_{(-/R)}
has descent in the fpqc topology;

2.

In the
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-setting,

\mathbb{L}_{(-/R)}
has hyperdescent in the fpqc topology.

Proof.

Assertion
(1)
is proved in
[
9
, Corollary 2.7]
. We now assume that
R
is an
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring. Recall that there is a concrete description of cotangent complexes in terms of indecomposables: the cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{A/R}
of
R\rightarrow A
is canonically equivalent to
{\rm cofib}(I_{A}\otimes_{A}I_{A}\rightarrow I_{A})
, where
I_{A}
is the cofiber of the multiplication map
A\otimes_{R}A\rightarrow A
. Let
A\rightarrow A^{\bullet}
be a faithfully flat hypercover of
A
, and therefore we form an exact sequence of cosimplicial diagrams in
\mathbf{Mod}_{R}
:

I_{A^{\bullet}}\longrightarrow A^{\bullet}\otimes_{R}A^{\bullet}\longrightarrow A^{\bullet}.

Taking the limit, we have that

\lim_{\Delta}(A^{\bullet}\otimes_{R}A^{\bullet})\simeq\lim_{i,j\in\Delta\times\Delta}(A^{i}\otimes_{R}A^{j})\simeq\lim_{j\in\Delta}\lim_{i\in\Delta}(A^{i}\otimes_{R}A^{j}),

because
\Delta
is sifted. Fix
j
, the functor
i\mapsto A^{i}\otimes_{R}A^{j}
is an fpqc hypercover of
A\otimes_{R}A^{j}
. It follows that the limit of the above diagram is equivalent to
A\otimes_{R}A
. This shows that the functor
A\mapsto I_{A}
has hyperdescent in the fpqc topology. Then, the hyperdescentness of the functor
A\mapsto I_{A}\otimes_{A}I_{A}
can be deduced from the following exact sequence

I_{A}\otimes_{A}I_{A}\longrightarrow A\otimes_{R}A\otimes_{A}I_{A}\simeq A\otimes_{R}I_{A}\longrightarrow I_{A}.

We conclude that
\mathbb{L}_{(-)/R}
has fpqc hyperdescent.
∎

Lemma 7.25
.

Let
X/S
be a relative stack, with a cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{X/S}
. Assume that
X
satisfies one of the following conditions:

1.

The diagonal
\Delta_{X/S}:X\rightarrow X\times_{S}X

is representable by a relative algebraic space;

2.

X
can be written in the form of
X\simeq{\rm colim}_{j\in J}{\rm Spec}R_{j}
, for which any
{\rm Spec}R_{j}
is formally étale over
X
, and
J
is sifted.

Then the following natural
map

\mathbb{L}_{X/S}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\simeq}}{{\longrightarrow}}\varprojlim_{(A/B,s)}s^{\prime}_{*}\mathbb{L}_{A/B}

is an equivalence, where the index runs over the
\infty
-category
\mathbf{Aff}_{X/S}
consisting of all commutative diagrams

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Spec}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Spec}B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}

Here the limit is formed in
\mathbf{QCoh}(X)
.

Proof.

According to the base change formula of the cotangent complex functor, without loss of generality, one can assume that
S\simeq{\rm Spec}B
is affine. Denote by
\mathbf{Aff}_{/X}
the
\infty
-category of spectral affine schemes lying over
X
.
For any map
s:{\rm Spec}A\rightarrow X
, consider the conormal exact sequence

s_{*}s^{*}\mathbb{L}_{X/B}\longrightarrow s_{*}\mathbb{L}_{A/B}\longrightarrow s_{*}\mathbb{L}_{A/X}.

Passing to the limit, we get the following new exact sequence of quasi-coherent sheaves over
X
,

\mathbb{L}_{X/R}\longrightarrow\varprojlim_{(A,s)}s_{*}\mathbb{L}_{A/R}\longrightarrow\varprojlim_{(A,s)}s_{*}\mathbb{L}_{A/X}.

We want to show that the last term
\varprojlim_{(A,s)}s_{*}\mathbb{L}_{A/X}
is vanishing.

Assume condition
(1)
.
Denote by
\mathbf{Mod}(X)
the
\infty
-category of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules, c.f.
[
38
, Notation 17.2.4.1]
. The fully faithful embedding
i_{X}:\mathbf{QCoh}(X)\rightarrow\mathbf{Mod}(X)
preserves small colimits and thus admits a right adjoint
Q_{X}:\mathbf{Mod}(X)\rightarrow\mathbf{QCoh}(X)
.
We denote by
\mathcal{L}:=\varprojlim^{\mathbf{Mod}(X)}_{(A,s)}s_{*}\mathbb{L}_{A/X}\in\mathbf{Mod}(X)
. Note that the
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module
\mathcal{L}
is not quasi-coherent in general, but there is an equivalence

Q_{X}(\mathcal{L})\simeq\varprojlim_{(A,s)}s_{*}\mathbb{L}_{A/X}.

We want to show that
\mathcal{L}\simeq 0
. Let
C\in\mathbf{Aff}_{/X}
, we have the following equivalences

\displaystyle\mathrm{R}\Gamma({\rm Spec}C,\mathcal{L})

\displaystyle\simeq\varprojlim_{(A,s)}\mathrm{R}\Gamma({\rm Spec}C,s_{*}\mathbb{L}_{A/X})

\displaystyle\simeq\varprojlim_{(A,s)}\mathrm{R}\Gamma({\rm Spec}C\times_{X}{\rm Spec}A,\mathbb{L}_{{\rm Spec}C\times_{X}{\rm Spec}A/{\rm Spec}C})

\displaystyle\simeq{\rm R}\Gamma({\rm colim}_{(A,s)}({\rm Spec}C\times_{X}{\rm Spec}A),\mathbb{L}_{-/C}).

The second equivalence holds because of the base change formula.
Note that the natural map
{\rm colim}_{(A,s)}({\rm Spec}C\times_{X}{\rm Spec}A)\rightarrow{\rm Spec}C
is an equivalence. Then we have

\displaystyle{\rm R}\Gamma({\rm colim}_{(A,s)}({\rm Spec}C\times_{X}{\rm Spec}A),\mathbb{L}_{-/C})

\displaystyle\simeq{\rm R}\Gamma({\rm Spec}C,\mathbb{L}_{-/C})\simeq 0.

Assume condition
(2)
. For any map
{\rm Spec}B\rightarrow X
, there exists a faithfully flat map
{\rm Spec}B\rightarrow{\rm Spec}B^{\prime}
, and some
j\in J
, such that the induced map
{\rm Spec}B^{\prime}\rightarrow X
factors through
{\rm Spec}R_{j}
. We therefore have an exact sequence of
B^{\prime}
-modules:

\mathbb{L}_{B/X}\otimes_{B}B^{\prime}\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}_{B^{\prime}/X}\simeq\mathbb{L}_{B^{\prime}/R_{j}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{L}_{B^{\prime}/B}.

Applying Lemma
7.24
, the faithfully flat descent of cotangent complexes for
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-rings, the term
\mathbb{L}_{B/X}
is equivalent to the limit of the diagram
\mathbb{L}_{B^{\bullet}/R_{j}}
, where
B^{\bullet}
is the Čech nerve of
(B\rightarrow B^{\prime})
. Let
\mathcal{C}
be the
\infty
-category of triples
(x:{\rm Spec}C\rightarrow X,x^{\prime}:{\rm Spec}C\rightarrow{\rm Spec}R_{i},i)
, where
x^{\prime}
is a lifting of
x
. Observe that the forgetful functor
\mathcal{C}\rightarrow\mathbf{Aff}_{/X}
is cofinal, and we can compute the limit
\varprojlim_{(B,s)}s_{*}\mathbb{L}_{B/X}
using
\mathcal{C}
. On the other hand, the subcategory of
\mathcal{C}
spanned by objects having the form
({\rm Spec}R_{j}\rightarrow{\rm Spec}R_{j},{\rm Spec}R_{j}\rightarrow{\rm Spec}R_{j},j)
, which is equivalent to
J
is also cofinal. Thus we have that

\varprojlim_{(B,s)}s_{*}\mathbb{L}_{B/X}\simeq\lim_{j\in J}s_{*}\mathbb{L}_{R_{j}/X}\simeq 0.

The proof is complete.

∎

As a corollary, we have the following result.

Proposition 7.26
.

The relative stack
\eta:\sqrt[\infty]{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{R}^{\wedge}}\rightarrow{\rm Spf}R
is formally étale.

Proof.

Using Lemma
7.23
and
7.25
, the canonical map

\theta:\mathbb{L}_{\sqrt[\infty]{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{R}^{\wedge}}/{\rm Spf}R}\longrightarrow\lim_{s\in\mathbf{FMon}_{R}}s_{*}\mathbb{L}^{\wedge}_{R[M[1/p]]_{p}^{\wedge}/R}\simeq 0

is an equivalence.
∎

Now we can prove our main theorem.

Theorem 7.27
.

Let
R
be a
p
-complete
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}
-ring lying over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
. Let
X
be a topologically
p
-saturated quasi-coherent spectral log stack over
{\rm Spec}R
, and let
\underline{X}^{\wedge}_{p}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{R}^{\wedge}
be the associated
p
-completion.
Then the map

\phi:\mathbb{L}_{X/R}^{\rm Log}\longrightarrow\eta_{*}\mathbb{L}_{\sqrt[\infty]{X_{p}^{\wedge}}/{\rm Spf}R}

induced from the canonical map
\eta:\sqrt[\infty]{X^{\wedge}_{p}}\rightarrow\underline{X}^{\wedge}_{p}

becomes an equivalence after
p
-completion. In particular, the following map

\phi:{\rm R}\Gamma(\underline{X},\mathbb{L}^{{\rm Log}}_{{X}/R})^{\wedge}_{p}\longrightarrow{\rm R}\Gamma(\sqrt[\infty]{X},\mathbb{L}_{\sqrt[\infty]{X^{\wedge}_{p}}/{\rm Spf}R})

is an equivalence.

Proof.

The
p
-completion functor

(-)^{\wedge}:\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{Stk}_{R}\longrightarrow\mathbf{Log}\mathbf{Stk}_{/{\rm Spf}R},X\mapsto X\times_{{\rm Spec}R}{\rm Spf}R

is colimit-preserving and compatible with fpqc localizations. Using Lemma
7.24
, without loss of generality, we might as well assume that
X\simeq{\rm Sp\acute{e}t}(A,M)
is log-affine. We only need to show that the following natural map

\phi:(\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(A,M)/R})^{\wedge}_{p}\longrightarrow\eta_{*}\mathbb{L}_{\sqrt[\infty]{{\rm Spf}(A,M)}/{\rm Spf}R}

is an equivalence. We form the following commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{((\mu^{M}_{p^{\infty}})^{\bullet}\times{\rm Spf}R[M[1/p]]^{\wedge}_{p})\times_{{\rm Spf}R[M]^{\wedge}_{p}}{\rm Spf}A^{\wedge}_{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mu^{M}_{p^{\infty}})^{\bullet}\times{\rm Spf}R[M[1/p]]^{\wedge}_{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\sqrt[\infty]{{\rm Spf}(A^{\wedge}_{p},M)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\sqrt[\infty]{{\rm Spf}(R[M]^{\wedge}_{p},M)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sqrt[\infty]{\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{R}^{\wedge}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Spf}A^{\wedge}_{p}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\rm Spf}R[M]^{\wedge}_{p}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{R}\mathbf{Log}_{R}^{\wedge}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{0}}

All squares are Cartesian.
We let

{\rm Spf}A^{\bullet}:=((\mu^{M}_{p^{\infty}})^{\bullet}\times{\rm Spf}R[M[1/p]]^{\wedge}_{p})\times_{{\rm Spf}R[M]^{\wedge}_{p}}{\rm Spf}A^{\wedge}_{p},

and

{\rm Spf}R^{\bullet}:=(\mu^{M}_{p^{\infty}})^{\bullet}\times{\rm Spf}R[M[1/p]]^{\wedge}_{p}.

Applying the assumption that
M
is topologically
p
-saturated, and using Proposition
7.26
, the term
\eta_{*}\mathbb{L}_{\sqrt[\infty]{{\rm Spf}(A,M)}/{\rm Spf}R}\simeq\eta_{*}\eta^{*}\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(A,M)/R}
is naturally equivalent to

\lim_{\bullet\in\Delta}((\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(A,M)/R})^{\wedge}_{p}\otimes^{\wedge}_{A^{\wedge}_{p}}A^{\bullet})\simeq\lim_{\bullet\in\Delta}((\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(A,M)/R})^{\wedge}_{p}\otimes^{\wedge}_{R[M]^{\wedge}_{p}}R^{\bullet}).

Using Theorem
7.14
and Remark
7.18
, we have that

\eta_{*}\mathbb{L}_{\sqrt[\infty]{{\rm Spf}(A,M)}/{\rm Spf}R}\simeq(\mathbb{L}^{G}_{(A,M)/R})^{\wedge}_{p}.

∎
